Now canonical decimal strings, `0x`-prefixed even-length hex, and native JSON booleans only. **TWO PRE-EXISTING TESTS HAD TO CHANGE, and the reason is the finding:** `test_refused_receipt_still_verifies_evidence` and `test_refused_with_omitted_keys_does_not_crash` each did `assertTrue(ok)` on an unsigned refusal — **they asserted the defect as intended behaviour.** Audited directly rather than taken on report. **WHERE THE SPEC WAS INSUFFICIENT AND THE AGENT CHOSE, recorded because these are the most likely places it is wrong:** refusals (§5 silent — "cannot be certified" is conservative, not certain; a spec defining an unsigned refusal envelope would be coherent and would make this verifier wrong); `vault == verifyingContract` (a reading, not a statement — a deployment separating them now fails); the JSON encoding of payload fields (§5 states none, so the convention all six samples use is now enforced against a producer with no written rule to point at — **the agent named this as the likeliest of its choices to be wrong**); the signature envelope; and `aiExplanation`, which §5.6 constrains not at all. **ISOLATION DISCLOSURE, unprompted:** a workspace guard the agent ran printed one-line excerpts from two `ts/` files. They were published Anvil private-key constants only — nothing about canonicalisation, hashing or EIP-712 — so the D-010 property is intact, but the leak happened and is recorded rather than assessed away. **Independence is DENTED, not pristine, for a second reason already recorded in D-042(d): the symptoms themselves were implementation-derived.** Any future claim about this verifier must carry both qualifications. **`REPORT.md` gains a headline amendment naming the pattern the repair exposed: a spec gap that has been WRITTEN UP is not a spec gap that has been HANDLED** — in all four cases the original report identified the gap and then filled it with a decision that made the verifier weaker than reporting the gap would have.

- **D-042 (2026-08-16) — Four rulings on the §9 steps 1–3 adversarial review. The S2 signature STANDS, annotated; the vault's token limit is corrected in the specification and capped only at v1.1; the invariant campaign gets the two arms it could not build; and the D-010 verifier is repaired by an agent that has not read the implementation.** Ruled by John at the facilitated session, after the review S2 was signed without. **(a) THE S2 SIGNATURE STANDS, ANNOTATED.** §11 disclosed that steps 1–3 had NO completed adversarial review, and D-041's first ruling was explicitly to sign with that recorded as a limit rather than run the review first. **This is that disclosed uncertainty resolving exactly where it was said to be, and a signature made in the open about its own gap is not falsified by the gap turning out to be real.** Rejected: downgrading Gate 6 alone, which re-introduces the partial-status formulation an earlier review called a build loop grading its own homework; and re-opening S2, which would treat a disclosed-and-accepted limit as though it had been hidden — by that standard no gate could be signed while any layer remained unreviewed. **(b) THE VAULT CAPS NATIVE VALUE ONLY, AND §7.1 SAID OTHERWISE.** One valid ALLOW receipt for `approve(spender, type(uint256).max)` passes every onchain check — pause, chain, vault, nonce, deadline, mandate, policy, operation, dataHash, target and selector allowlists — and the wei ceiling never engages because `valueWei` is zero; the vault's entire token balance is then transferable. §4 was honest ("cap native value, restrict supported targets and selectors"); **§7.1's "evaluator or signer compromise scenario WITHIN THE VAULT'S HARD CAPS" was not**, and `PolicyPayload.maxAllowanceIncreaseBaseUnits` exists precisely because unlimited approval is the flagship Case 2 attack. **So that attack is refused by the CONFORMANCE EVALUATOR with nothing behind it.** RULED: correct the claim now, defer the cap to v1.1. The correction is what makes the project honest today; adding a vault feature the day after S2 is exactly the scope expansion D-003's stop condition exists for, and it should be a deliberate decision rather than something absorbed into a remediation. §7.1 is amended in place and `test_LIMIT_vaultCapsNativeValueOnlyAndNotTokenAuthority` now asserts the LIMIT as a regression — **if a cap is ever added that test fails, and the failure is the signal to update §7.1, not to delete the test.** Rejected: building the cap now (net-new capability, moves the Foundry suite and the corpus environment), and declaring no cap will ever exist (defensible — a vault-side cap would partly mask what the harness measures — but it forecloses a decision nothing forces today). **(c) THE INVARIANT CAMPAIGN HAD ZERO MARGINAL KILLING POWER, MEASURED.** 31 mutations of the vault's own checks: 31/31 caught by the deterministic suite, and **31/31 still caught with the entire stateful campaign disabled.** Five survived all ten invariants across 262,144 calls, including nonce-consumed-after-the-external-call, `nonReentrant` removed, and the nonce check weakened from `!=` to `<` — **a vault honouring receipts for arbitrary FUTURE nonces.** The campaign is not vacuous (instrumented probes find ≥5 real executions per run, so the 16,384-calls/zero-executions failure has not recurred); it simply killed nothing the fast tests did not. **The diagnosis is that a campaign's coverage is bounded by what its handler can BUILD, not by how many calls it makes:** every action submitted the current nonce or a stale one, so a future nonce was never offered; and DemoPay could not be made to fail, because duration, beneficiary and value were all bounded away from its three revert conditions. RULED: add the two missing arms and restate the claim. Both mutations are now killed, verified by re-running them. Rejected: restating the claim only (accepts a 262,144-call campaign that proves nothing the fast tests do), and additionally wiring mutation survival into the gate as a standing check (slow, and a second new mechanism the day after S2). **(d) THE D-010 VERIFIER IS REPAIRED BY AN AGENT THAT HAS NOT READ THE IMPLEMENTATION.** Its defects are real — see A-040 — but D-010's conditions are "built from the published schemas only" and no shared canonicalisation or hashing code, and **the independence IS the evidential value**: it is what let the verifier surface genuine spec defects in the first place. An agent that has read the TypeScript writes a fix that mirrors the implementation rather than testing it. RULED: a fresh agent, given the published schemas and the findings stated as SYMPTOMS rather than as implementation guidance. **Recorded honestly: even a symptom statement is information derived from the implementation, so the independence is DENTED rather than pristine, and any future claim about the verifier must say so.** Rejected: repairing it directly (fast, certain, and spends the only asset the artifact has), and deferring the repair (leaves a tool that certifies a forged refusal as PASS, which this project treats as worse than no check).

- **A-040 (2026-08-16) — The §9 steps 1–3 adversarial review S2 was signed without. Two reviewers, one on the vault and one on the typed payloads and hashes; the encoding held, and the two layers built ON it did not.** Run at frozen commit `9488f27`. **WHAT HELD, stated first because it is the larger result:** a 4-way struct-hash differential across Solidity, the TS signer's hand-rolled padding, the TS evaluator's viem coder, and the Python verifier — 120 randomised payload sets plus boundary rows for every integer width — found **zero divergences**; all six EIP-712 type strings are byte-identical across five sources; all ten of §3.3(4)'s bound fields are genuinely in the encoding; keccak and secp256k1 pass vectors they were not pinned to; the domain separator binds chain and vault, and cross-chain and cross-vault replay both fail at the vault. On the vault: nonce consumed before the external call on both paths, no direct override of a BLOCK, override binds both `reviewReceiptHash` and `actionHash`, every owner-only function carries `onlyOwner`, the reentrancy guard covers both entry points in all four orderings, and OZ `ECDSA.recover` reverts on malleable `s`. **No hash collision exists at this layer and none was found.** **THE VAULT FINDINGS:** the ERC20 authority gap ruled in D-042(b); **the corpus-producing environments allowlist the ecrecover precompile `0x…0001` as an execution target and do NOT allowlist DemoERC20** — contradicting §7.2 and the same file's own offchain baseline, which correctly lists both — with a codeless target accepting any allowlisted selector, reporting success, and stranding up to the per-action ceiling; **rotation is not revocation** (a receipt from a rotated-out signer executes if that key is ever reinstated, and a receipt pre-minted by a standby key goes live the instant the owner rotates to it — nothing binds a receipt to the epoch in which its signer was active, and the code comment claiming otherwise is wrong); **the override authorization is not logged** though §3.3(2) requires override be logged — `ActionExecuted` records only `viaOverride` and the receipt's `decisionId`, so an auditor cannot say which owner authorization was used or what the owner's recorded reason was; and the allowlists are a **cross product** of targets and selectors rather than pairs, inert against the current demo contracts and live the moment any allowlisted target has a fallback or a colliding selector. **THE VERIFIER FINDINGS, all in the D-010 CLI rather than in the encoding it checks:** a bundle asserting `{"refused": true}` is certified `=> PASS` with **no signature checked anywhere**, while the `evidence.json` beside it says `verdict: ALLOW` — reproduced directly, and it defeats exactly the property D-012 exists to establish, that "the signer refused" and "the signer was never asked" are distinguishable; **chain and vault binding is never established from the bundle** (`domain.json` is an unsigned side file the presenter writes, and `_chain_checks` contains no comparison against the payloads' own `chainId`/`vault` — the redundancy `SentinelTypes.sol` says exists "so an offchain verifier that never saw the domain" can check this is never consumed); TS and Python JCS diverge on exactly the 2,048 lone surrogates, where Python raises `UnicodeEncodeError` rather than mismatching, so a genuine receipt reads as FAILED; override signatures are not low-s checked though receipt signatures are, making the verifier strictly more permissive than the chain; payload parsing is lossy enough that eight textually distinct documents recompute the same committed hash; and `check-type-strings.sh` is defeated by `head -1` on the spec side with nothing backstopping it. **A FINDING ABOUT THE REPAIR, worth more than most of the repairs:** the two new invariant arms were written, their non-vacuity test passed, the whole suite went green — **and the future-nonce mutation still survived, because handler selectors are registered explicitly and the new ones had not been added.** The invariants existed and pointed at nothing. Caught only by re-running the mutation instead of trusting the green suite. That is the project's most-repeated defect appearing inside the fix for a finding about the same defect. **DEFERRED WITH THE RE-LABEL, verified rather than assumed:** the corpus vault-config defect changes `targetOnVaultAllowlist` in the labelling views — F009's view carries `false` today and labeller E's note cites "the target is off the vault allowlist" by name — so repairing it changes what the labellers saw, exactly like F032 and F026/F051. It rides with the re-label.

- **D-041 (2026-08-16) — GATE S2 IS SIGNED. PASS, John, at a facilitated session. D-002's second and last mid-build gate.** The agent facilitated, presented the evidence and the limits, and recorded the ruling; **it did not make it, and D-002's non-delegability is unchanged by this entry.** **What was signed:** all four D-009 deliverables in and green — the 50-fixture corpus, §7.5 hard-gate evidence, the §7.3 ablation report, and the D-010 receipt-verifier CLI — and all seven §7.5 pass conditions MET, Gate 5 having been certified the same day (D-038). Measured at the deep profile: 66/66 Foundry (20,000 fuzz runs, 262,144 calls per stateful invariant), 380/380 TypeScript, 70/70 verifier, 50 corpus fixtures with committed views verified, eight mechanical guards. **THREE DECISION POINTS WERE PUT BEFORE THE SIGNATURE, deliberately in that order so anything capable of changing the verdict came first rather than after.** **(1) §9 steps 1–3's adversarial review was cut short by a spend limit that was never retired — RULED: sign, with the limit recorded rather than retired.** It is not an S2 pass condition; those steps carry the most independent non-review evidence in the repository; and inventing a blocker at the gate is its own kind of dishonesty. Rejected: running the review first (the base rate says it would find something — two reviews the same day found 25 findings in work that had already passed a build loop — but S2's conditions do not require it), and deferring it to pre-publication, which is the bucket deferred things go quiet in. **(2) The evidence dashboard — RULED: stays outside S2**, as D-009 wrote it. It is presentation over evidence that already exists and is already readable, and D-009's own priority order always had it first to drop. **(3) Whether the 14-of-20 class-coverage finding changes any gate's status — RULED: no. MET stands and §11 carries the finding.** Traced gate by gate before the ruling: Gates 1, 2 and 6 rest on Foundry and the signer, not corpus classes; Gate 3 on F045/F046/F047, which do exercise their class; Gate 4 on the ablation, whose class is exercised by five fixtures; Gate 7 on the injection demonstration and the canary, and the pack already records that those fixtures' verdicts must not be read as detections. The false-allow figures are per-fixture, not per-class, so a narrow class does not move them. **What the finding damages is the corpus's BREADTH claim, which §11's opening line already disclaims.** Rejected: downgrading Gate 7 to PART MET — the pack corrected away from that formulation once already after a review called it a build loop grading its own homework; and holding S2 until the GAP class has a fixture, which would hold the gate behind a full re-label of all 50, since writing that fixture changes the view the labels of record were drawn against. **WHAT §11 SAYS IS NOT IN EVIDENCE IS PART OF WHAT WAS SIGNED, not a caveat attached afterwards:** verdict correctness in general (50 fixtures over two demo contracts and two call schemas); 14 of 20 classes exercising the class they name, one of them a GAP owing a fixture; no live agent in CI, every proposal a pinned transcript and the canary on demand; steps 1–3's incomplete review; the labelling contamination channel bounded but not closed, with §5.7.1 still publishing 41 reason-code identifiers; "model diversity" meaning a second model from one vendor's family rather than an independent implementation of judgement; and condition (2) unenforceable inside the four files that must quote §10.1's labels, one being the packet Gate 5 was ruled from. **The honest counterweight, recorded because it cuts both ways:** this evidence has been adversarially attacked harder than most of the repository — on the day of signing, two independent reviews produced 25 findings and **both of the newest guards proved defeatable** — which is a reason for confidence in what survived and a reason for humility about what has not been attacked yet. **ANNOTATED 2026-08-16 (D-042(a)), hours after signing, by the review this signature was explicitly taken without.** The §9 steps 1–3 adversarial review ran and found real defects in layers S2 rests on — see A-040. **The signature STANDS**, on the reasoning that §11 disclosed the missing review, ruling (1) above chose to sign with it recorded as a limit rather than run it first, and this is that disclosed uncertainty resolving where it was said to be. **What is now known that was not when this was signed:** Gate 6's claim — "Foundry fuzz and invariant tests cannot bypass SentinelVault" — was carried by the 56 deterministic tests, with the stateful campaign killing nothing they did not (since repaired, D-042(c)); §7.1's "within the vault's hard caps" overstated containment, because the vault caps native value only and not token authority (claim corrected, cap deferred, D-042(b)); and the D-010 verifier certified a forged refusal and a cross-domain receipt as PASS (repair commissioned, D-042(d)). **None of this was hidden from the signing session; all of it was named as absent from it.** A reader deciding how much weight to give this signature should read A-040 alongside it.

**WHAT THIS DOES NOT AUTHORISE.** D-016 still blocks all publication and the repository is PRIVATE. Gate 8 (five-minute comprehension) is PRE-PUBLICATION under D-032, needs the dashboard D-009 deferred and John's five held questions, and is untouched by this signature. Certification of public claims remains autonomy NONE.

- **D-040 (2026-08-16) — Three rulings closing out A-039's open questions: F002 stays where it is, the class map is widened to what §7.1 says, and condition (2)'s residual is accepted as documented.** Ruled by John at the facilitated session. **(a) F002 IS DELIBERATE AND STAYS IN THE CONFORMING CLASS.** It is the mandated purchase at zero native value — a benign variation that BLOCKS, because the call reverts and D-021 makes a reverting simulation a failed rule. The class is read as "exact allowed action AND benign variations": variations that LOOK benign and may not be. **The reasoning that decided it: F002 earns its place BY blocking.** It is the fixture that exposed the D-021 gap, it produced the project's only inter-labeller disagreement (A read REVIEW, B read BLOCK, both wrote the ambiguity up independently), and it is why the D-011(c) rate is 10.0% rather than 0%. Moving it to `simulation-revert` would file the demonstration under its own answer — the same shape as the specification carrying worked examples beside their solutions, which is the contamination D-035 spent the session bounding. **Rejected:** moving it at v1.1, which would let the guard assert the stronger "everything here allows" but lose the pairing; and adding a second conforming fixture, which strengthens the evidence without answering what the class NAME asserts. The guard reports F002 as a standing observation on every run rather than silencing it, so a BLOCK in the conforming class reads as intent rather than rot. **(b) THE CLASS MAP IS WIDENED FOR `evaluator-or-signer-compromise`.** §7.1 words the class "Evaluator or signer compromise scenario **within the vault's hard caps**", and D-026 names those caps as four — pause, nonce, deadline, and the vault's own value cap. The map credited the class to `EVAL_VAULT_NOT_PAUSED` alone, which **described F057, the class's only fixture, rather than the class** — the circular authoring `check-class-coverage.sh`'s own header warns against, raised by an adversarial reviewer as the map-width judgement only a human should make. Now mapped to all four. F057 still hits one, so the class stays green; the shortfall is visible in the per-fixture note rather than hidden in a narrow map. **Rejected:** requiring two of the four caps, which would invent a per-class threshold the guard has nowhere else and add a GAP riding on the re-label; and leaving it, which knowingly keeps a map describing a fixture instead of a class. **(c) CONDITION (2)'s RESIDUAL IS ACCEPTED AND DOCUMENTED.** The four files that must quote §10.1's banned labels to record why D-001 cut executed comparisons are exempt from the label scan, and one of them is `docs/gate-5-vendor-audit.md` — the packet John rules Gate 5 from. Appending "Sentinel was executed directly against every vendor above" to it passes the gate, demonstrated and still true after A-039's repairs. **A guard cannot distinguish labelling a comparison executed from recording that executed comparisons were cut; the words are identical.** Accepted because the alternatives buy enforcement by making the record harder to write: a quoted-or-fenced rule is a retroactive formatting constraint on the whole decision log that catches accidents rather than intent, and splitting the audit into packet and record restructures a document that just did its job. **What makes this acceptable rather than a hole left open:** the exemption SHRANK — HANDOFF, session-state, the v1.1 register and the review artifacts were all on the old list and are now scanned — and the guard states the residual and names the demonstration in its own header. **THE CONDITION THAT WOULD REVERSE (c):** any of the four exempt files starting to carry a measurement. The exemption is for deliberation records, and a file that reports a result has stopped being one.

- **A-039 (2026-08-16) — TWO ADVERSARIAL REVIEWS AT A FROZEN COMMIT PRODUCED 25 FINDINGS AGAINST THE TWO NEWEST GUARDS AND THE DOCUMENTS CITING THEM. Both guards could be defeated; several published claims exceeded their evidence. All repaired or recorded.** Run against `5b1a55a` in a detached worktree, two reviewers with distinct lenses — "do these guards actually work" and "does any claim exceed its evidence" — each told to prove the work fails and given the files rather than the author's reasoning. **THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MOST, because it is about how this defect class behaves:** `check-vendor-honesty.sh`'s certification check grepped the WHOLE 71 KB proposal for the certification sentence, so it could be satisfied by a quotation, an example, or a fenced code block anywhere in the file — demonstrated by planting one in §14 with a 1999 date and a decision id that does not exist. **That is the identical defect to the `$0`-versus-field-4 bug repaired twenty lines above it, in the same commit, by the same author, while writing a comment congratulating itself for catching that one.** Knowing about a defect class is not protection from it. **Guard defects found and fixed:** the certification grep now scopes to §2 and a certification that DISAPPEARS while a hash is pinned now fails rather than silently reporting "uncertified"; the `[§13#N]` marker was validated for SHAPE only, so `[§13#997 read 1999-01-01]` counted as a citation though §13 has no entry 997 — every N now resolves against §13's real entries, which is what "linked" in D-008(1) always meant; the vendor EXCLUDED list was anchored `^` only, making every entry a PREFIX match, so `docs/decisions.md.gate-5-evidence` inherited an exemption and could carry a head-to-head false-allow table naming three vendors plus both banned §10.1 labels; condition (2)'s exclusion list was the same as condition (4)'s though its justification was only ever about vendor names, so a superiority claim appended to the Gate 5 audit passed — now narrowed to four measured files; binary detection was `grep -Iq .`, so ONE NUL byte removed a file from the scan entirely; **D-038's declared expiry was a printed warning rather than a detection** — the §2 table is now pinned by SHA-256, `check-label-prompt.sh`'s pattern applied to a second frozen artifact. **In `check-class-coverage.sh`:** the CONFORMING branch asserted only "one fixture allows cleanly" while its comment claimed "a failing check would be the defect", and the corpus violated the stated version with no edit at all; its failure path crashed on a TypeError, killing the run before the carried list and GAP count printed — the output `test.sh` tells the reader to read; the guard read classes from `for-labelling` and results from `results/` and never checked they agreed, so **relabelling one side laundered the ruled GAP green in two metadata edits, with the guard's own stale-baseline FAIL message supplying step two**; surplus result files and duplicate `fixtureId`s were silent; `status` was unvalidated free text, so `'GAP'`→`'Gap'` erased the ruled GAP and printed "None owes a fixture"; and BASELINE keys were never checked against the map, so an entry for a class that exists nowhere printed as carried forever. **A JUDGEMENT DELIBERATELY NOT TAKEN:** the strict CONFORMING assertion fails on F002, a benign variation (the mandated purchase at zero native value) that blocks because D-021 makes a reverting simulation a failed rule. Whether every member of "exact allowed and benign variations" must allow is a question about what the class MEANS, and an earlier draft had the guard answering it. It now asserts the defensible half and REPORTS F002 as an unruled observation on every run. **Published claims corrected:** "four are DELEGATED to a layer that does test them" — the guard states it cannot see the delegate and does not credit it, and one of the four points at a regression guard the same pack calls "not a proof of absence"; **"six independent labellers" was wrong** — twelve label files touch the fourteen fixtures, eight belong to the programme, and four (A–D) are an earlier round whose role is unestablished and is now flagged rather than cited; "all nine cited pages" was twelve pages over nine rows; **"every discrepancy overstated a COMPETITOR, never Sentinel" is withdrawn** — its source paragraph named a row the same file clears as holding and omitted one it found a defect in, and "never Sentinel" describes a direction no pass examined, since the only cells that could carry a Sentinel claim are ruled out of scope by construction; §12 said four capability rows failed their sources when it was five; Gate 3 cited F048 as a fourth line of evidence under a class §11 now records as a GAP, and it is withdrawn as one; **the coverage boundary had rotted again in four places** — the artifact §0 orders the signing session to read in full — reporting Gate 5 as UNCERTIFIED, A-030 as an open fork, steps 7–8 as never reviewed, and the two D-039 classes as unruled; and the v1.1 register's line numbers were all stale by four **under a sentence claiming every one had been verified**, which is the register's own inoculation against stale figures failing on its first day. **VERIFICATION OF THE REPAIRS, AND A HARNESS BUG IN IT WORTH MORE THAN THE REPAIRS:** all twelve exploits were re-run. The first harness reported 10 of 10 caught — because it detected failure by grepping output for `FAIL`, which matched the word `FAILS` inside a PASSING message. **Re-run against exit status, two had in fact survived.** `docs/session-state.md` already carried the trace "Do not measure mutation results by parsing the reporter. Use exit status", written after the same mistake earlier in this project. **Final honest result: eleven of twelve caught, one documented residual** — condition (2) is unenforced inside the four files that must quote the §10.1 labels to record why D-001 cut executed comparisons, one of which is the packet John rules Gate 5 from. A guard cannot distinguish "labelling a comparison executed" from "recording that executed comparisons were cut"; reading that file is the only control, and the guard now says so in terms. **HYPOTHESIS RAISED AND LEFT FOR JOHN:** the class map credits `evaluator-or-signer-compromise` solely to `EVAL_VAULT_NOT_PAUSED`, while §7.1 words the class "Evaluator or signer compromise scenario **within the vault's hard caps**" — pause is one hard cap, not the class. The reviewer flagged it as the map-width judgement the guard's header says only a human can make, and did not claim it.

- **D-038 (2026-08-16) — GATE 5 (§7.5 vendor honesty) IS CERTIFIED. Seven rulings by John at the facilitated session; §2 rewritten to match; the guard now reports 11 of 11 cited and D-008(3) certified by record.** The verification partition gives public claims autonomy NONE, so every ruling below is John's and the agent transcribed. **Q1 — marker format `[§13#N read YYYY-MM-DD]`, access date 2026-08-15**, the date every originally-cited page was fetched. Rejected: today's date, which would state a read that did not happen then; and a re-read interval, which makes the gate go red on time rather than on a change and is worth building only if the proposal becomes public and long-lived. **Q2 — inference marking is ONE COLUMN DECLARATION plus per-clause marks.** A sentence under the table states that capability entries are quoted or closely paraphrased from the cited source as read on the marked date, that `(inference)` clauses are not, and that **the "Consequence for Sentinel" column is Sentinel's own inference throughout** — which is true by construction, since no vendor documents what its product implies for Sentinel. Rejected: `(inference)` on all eleven fourth-column cells, which says the same thing eleven times and reads as hedging; and marking only the capability column, which would have narrowed D-008(3)'s "every cell" and left the table's strongest statements unmarked. **Row 2 — SPLIT into Privy and Coinbase Policy Engine, and "signer" struck from both.** Privy's page documents five of six criteria including decoded calldata; Coinbase's documents two; signer is documented for neither. Rejected: narrowing to the intersection, which would have dropped Privy's documented decoded-calldata policy support — understating a competitor, the direction this gate exists to catch. **Row 3 — Circle's "agent-native execution" marked `(inference)`.** The capability is documented (the page describes an agent holding funds and transacting onchain autonomously); only the phrase is Sentinel's. Rejected: striking it, which would make a competitor look narrower than its own documentation. **Row 5 — Safe RE-CITED, not narrowed, and reworded to "allowance modules" / "recovery modules".** The four claims absent from the cited Guards page are all documented on two Safe pages §13 did not list, verified by direct fetch and added as #25 and #26. **The cheap option was available and was rejected on the record:** narrowing the row to Guards would have made a competitor look substantially weaker on a citation error of ours. The "modules" wording is what the verified page actually describes; the bare wording was declined because the help-centre page that would support it was seen only through search results and never fetched. **Row 7 — Hypernative re-cited AND marked `(inference)`, under a fallback declared before the evidence was seen.** "Intent verification" appears on no cited page, and the first re-cite attempt produced a self-contradicting fetch that was reported as a failed lookup rather than laundered into a citation. John ruled ONE further verified read with the fallback fixed in advance — success re-cites, failure or ambiguity marks `(inference)`, never strikes — the same discipline D-035 used with its escalation threshold, and for the same reason: it removes the option of choosing the convenient reading afterwards. **The read succeeded on two independent pages** (#27, #28, read 2026-08-16), which carry "simulation has to interpret intent" and "MPC ... doesn't secure transaction intent" in body text. **It did not carry the phrase "intent verification"**, so Q2's rule applied on its own terms and the clause is marked. The two rulings intersected rather than a new option being invented after the fact. **Row 8 — SPLIT into Blockaid and Tenderly, "known-threat detection" struck from Tenderly, and Tenderly's CATEGORY relabelled "Simulation APIs".** The clause is unsupported on the cited landing page and on the alert-type documentation: every Tenderly alert is a user-configured condition, and even "Blocklisted Callers" requires the user to supply the addresses. The category change is not cosmetic — D-008(4) forbids a LAYOUT implying a capability, so leaving "threat APIs" beside Tenderly would keep asserting what the text no longer does. **WHAT THE GUARD DOES NOW, and the line it must not cross:** `check-vendor-honesty.sh` reports D-008(1)'s mechanical half as MET (11 of 11 rows carry a marker) and reports D-008(3) as **certified by record**, naming this decision — it checks that a NAMED certification exists in §2, and states on every run that it did not and cannot check the certification is right. **Two guard defects fixed as part of this, both previously recorded as owed:** the marker was counted with `$0` (the whole row) while the message said "the capability cell", so a marker in the Consequence column would have counted — now tested against field 4; and the script's prose still said "the cells do not reference them", which stopped being true the moment the table was written. Three mutations confirm the tightening: a marker moved to the Consequence cell, an uncited row added, and the certification line removed are all caught. **THE CONDITION THAT MAKES THIS CERTIFICATION STALE, stated because a certification with no expiry is a claim nobody re-examines: any edit to the §2 table.** The guard says so where the certification prints. **NOT DONE:** Row 2's Coinbase citation was left at two documented criteria without attempting the re-cite lookup that rescued Safe, and Tenderly stays cited to a marketing landing page the audit itself called thin. Both would improve accuracy in the direction that does not flatter Sentinel, and both were declined only to stop Gate 5 blocking Gate S2 further. They are v1.1 work.

- **D-039 (2026-08-16) — The two classes A-038 found are NOT the same kind of defect, and are ruled separately. Resolves A-038.** Ruled by John at the facilitated session, choosing the split over one uniform label. **`owner-override-and-block-behaviour` — ACCEPTED DELEGATION.** F054/F055 fail on `EVAL_TARGET_CODE_IDENTITY` and `EVAL_PURCHASE_RESOURCE`, neither about the override path — but they declare `primaryEnforcement: vault-foundry-invariants`, and the vault suite genuinely does test the override path, including the cross-path door added this cycle. The declaration is accurate; the corpus layer is simply not where the property is proved. Nothing is owed. **`conflicting-block-state` — A GENUINE UNCOVERED CLASS, and it OWES A FIXTURE at v1.1.** F048 declares `primaryEnforcement: conformance-engine` — it claims to be proved HERE — and then fails on `EVAL_SIMULATION_UNAVAILABLE` and `EVAL_TARGET_CODE_IDENTITY` and REVIEWs, which is an outage shape rather than the failed-rule-that-blocks shape D-030 describes. No other layer covers it. **Why the distinction was worth a ruling rather than a shared baseline entry:** a class that claims to be proved at this layer and is not is materially worse than one that points somewhere else and is right, and a single "carried debt" label would have hidden exactly that difference — the same collapsing that made session-state say "five rows hold" when Row 8 held by half. `check-class-coverage.sh` now carries a `status` of DELEGATED / RESERVED / GAP alongside the ruling id, and prints the GAP count separately, so the distinction survives in the instrument rather than only in this entry. **Rejected:** treating both as carried debt like D-025's reserved class, which would record a class claiming conformance-engine enforcement as merely "deferred"; and treating both as owed fixtures, which discards the `primaryEnforcement` mechanism the corpus already uses and would eventually force corpus fixtures for properties the vault suite proves better. **Deferral is unchanged and inherited from A-036:** repairing F048 changes the view the labels of record were drawn against, so the fixture rides with the re-label rather than being written now. **THE CONDITION THAT WOULD REVERSE THE DELEGATION HALF:** if the vault suite's override coverage is ever removed or narrowed, `owner-override-and-block-behaviour` becomes a GAP with nothing proving it anywhere, and this guard cannot see that happen — it reports the delegation, it cannot audit the delegate.

- **D-037 (2026-08-16) — ONE AGENT SESSION AT A TIME ON THIS REPOSITORY. Resolves A-037.** Ruled by John at the facilitated decision session. **The finding:** two sessions ran D-035's control arm minutes apart and one silently overwrote a file the other had committed four minutes earlier; nothing in the repository detected it, and it surfaced only because both happened to choose the letter "L". **Ruled: only one agent session works this tree at a time.** **Why this over the alternatives, recorded because the cheap one was available:** a claim-file protocol (write a labeller's provenance before the arm runs, so duplicates fail at creation) fixes only the labelling case, and the general hazard is that `docs/session-state.md` and `docs/decisions.md` are append-heavy single-writer files that two sessions collide on silently. Worktree-per-session makes collisions LOUD rather than silent, which is a real improvement, but it converts them into merge conflicts on exactly those two files — and resolving those by hand is how content gets lost. Neither removes the failure mode; this does. **THE COUNTER-ARGUMENT, which is real:** throughput. A second session can no longer be parked on a side task, and this project has genuinely used parallel work well — four adversarial reviews at a fixed commit in one session. **The distinction that makes the ruling hold:** those reviews are SUB-AGENTS inside one session, reading a frozen tree and reporting back to a single writer. That stays allowed and is unaffected. What is forbidden is two independent sessions with write authority over one working tree. **THE CONDITION THAT WOULD REVERSE THIS:** a mechanism that makes a second writer detectable from inside the repository — a session lock, or per-session write scoping. None exists today, and the guards cannot supply one: every guard here validates the CONTENT of the tree, and content cannot say who produced it.

- **A-038 (2026-08-16) — A-036's mechanical check is BUILT, and on its first run it found two more vacuous classes. 14 of 20 corpus classes exercise the class they name.** `scripts/check-class-coverage.sh`, in the gate as the eighth guard. **What it asserts:** for each §7.1 scenario class, at least one fixture produces at least one FAILING check at L3 drawn from the set of checks that class is about. **The map from class to checks is the load-bearing part, and it was authored from §7.1's class names and §5.7.1's descriptions — deliberately NOT from what the fixtures currently produce**, because a map read off the current results agrees with them by construction and asserts nothing, which is the same defect one level up. **Result: 14 of 20 exercised, six carried.** Four carried are known and reasoned — `unexpected-internal-call` (D-025 reserves the knob), `reentrancy-attempt` (A-036), `invalid-or-rotated-signer` (the evaluator has NO code for signer identity; it belongs to the isolated signer under D-010, so no corpus fixture can exercise it), `malicious-retrieved-instructions` (the subject is containment, measured by the leak guard, and the ablation already says these verdicts must not be read as detections). **TWO ARE NEW AND UNRULED, and both need John:** (a) **`owner-override-and-block-behaviour`** — F054/F055 are marked `vault-foundry-invariants` and fail on `EVAL_TARGET_CODE_IDENTITY` and `EVAL_PURCHASE_RESOURCE`; neither is about the override path, which is genuinely tested in the vault suite but not exercised at the corpus layer. (b) **`conflicting-block-state`** — F048 fails `EVAL_SIMULATION_UNAVAILABLE` and `EVAL_TARGET_CODE_IDENTITY` and REVIEWs, which is an outage shape, not the conflicting-state shape D-030 describes as a failed rule that blocks. **Both inherit A-036's deferral** — repairing a fixture changes the view the labels of record were drawn against — so they are v1.1 work riding with the re-label, not fixes to make now. **THE GUARD PASSES ON A RATCHET AND SAYS SO EVERY RUN:** a class not in the baseline that goes unexercised FAILS the gate; a baselined class that becomes exercised also FAILS, so a stale ratchet cannot sit there being true-once. Baseline entries print with `ruling:` — a decision id, or `UNRULED` where the guard found it and nobody has decided. A green line means "no NEW class went vacuous", never "every class is covered", and the coverage boundary was rewritten to stop saying "50 fixtures across all 20 declared classes", which read as coverage it did not have. **MEASURED AGAINST MUTATION, because a guard that exists is not a guard that works — this project's most repeated defect, three times in one prior session.** Seven mutations, **six caught**: baseline entry removed; stale baseline entry added; map naming a check the engine does not declare (validated against `EVAL_CODES` read from source, so a rename fails loudly rather than silently unmatching); map entry deleted; map repointed at a check the fixtures do not fail; result file deleted. **THE ONE THAT SURVIVED, recorded rather than papered over:** repointing `altered-calldata-after-receipt` from `EVAL_CALLDATA_BINDING` to `EVAL_PURCHASE_RESOURCE` — a check F018 already fails for an unrelated reason — leaves the class green while asserting nothing. **No mechanical check can close that hole**, because the map is a judgement about what a class MEANS; it is closed only by a reader checking the map against §7.1, which is why the map lives in the guard with its reasoning rather than in a data file nobody opens. The limitation is stated in the guard's own header, alongside the other three: it cannot distinguish exercising a class well from grazing it, it reads L3 only, and it reports `primaryEnforcement` delegation without crediting it. **Not done: no independent adversarial review of this guard.** Project practice is a fresh sub-agent at a frozen commit told to prove the work fails, and it has not been run here.

- **A-037 (2026-08-15) — TWO AGENT SESSIONS RAN THE SAME MEASUREMENT ON THIS REPOSITORY AT ONCE, AND ONE SILENTLY OVERWROTE THE OTHER'S COMMITTED EVIDENCE. Nothing in the project detected it; it surfaced by luck.** **What happened.** Two Claude Code sessions were open on `step-3/isolated-signer`. Session A read `docs/session-state.md`, whose step 1 said the D-035 measurement "may already be recorded", verified that no labeller-L files existed (`git status` clean, HEAD `359fcd3`, no `labeller-L*` in `fixtures/corpus/labels/`), and launched a control labeller. **Both checks were correct at the moment they ran.** While that labeller was in flight, session B ran its own control arm, wrote `labeller-L-control.json` and its provenance, recorded A-036 and the D-035 result, and committed as `1180241`. Session A's labeller then finished and **wrote to the same two filenames, overwriting a file that had been committed four minutes earlier.** **How it was caught, which is the part that should worry us.** Not by a guard, not by the suite, not by `git status` — by session A going to record its result and finding `decisions.md` already describing it. Had session B chosen any other letter, or had session A's labeller been slower, there would have been no collision on disk and **no signal at all** — two agents would have burned the same measurement, and the audit trail would carry two arms silently attributed to one letter. **What was done:** the committed L files were restored with `git checkout HEAD --`, and session A's arm was preserved as **labeller M**, with a `redesignationNote` in its provenance stating that it was run and attested as "L", that only the letter changed, and that it is an accidental duplicate rather than a designed replication. No label, finding, or attestation text was altered. **The evidence is genuinely better for it** — ten control observations instead of five, two arms blind to each other agreeing on label AND confidence — and that is exactly why it needs recording as luck. A result nobody planned, written up as though it were the design, is the same honesty defect this project keeps finding, arriving through a new door. **The general lesson, and it is not about labelling:** this repository has no lock, no session registry, and no per-agent write scoping. Every safeguard the project has built assumes ONE writer. `check-secrets.sh`, `check-label-prompt.sh`, the mutation anchors, the gate itself — all of them check the CONTENT of a tree, and none of them can see that two processes are editing it. **A guard that validates a file cannot tell you who wrote it or that someone else just did.** **Owed, and not built:** the cheap mitigation is that a labeller letter should be claimed by writing its provenance file FIRST, before the arm runs, so a collision fails loudly at creation instead of silently at overwrite. The correct mitigation is not running two sessions on one working tree, which is John's operating decision and not an agent's to make.

- **A-036 (2026-08-15) — Two corpus fixtures do not exercise the class they are named for, found by labellers reading only the data.** **F056** is in the `reentrancy-attempt` class, and labeller L established it does not exercise reentrancy at all: `internalCallCount` is 0, no internal call occurs, the target's runtime code hash is the hash of EMPTY bytecode, and the exact-target rule fails long before `allowedCallGraphHash` is reached — "a second wrong-target fixture wearing a reentrancy label". The corpus already marks its `primaryEnforcement` as `vault-foundry-invariants` and the ablation discloses that, but **that disclosure is about WHERE the property is enforced, not about the fixture failing to exercise the class it names**, and a reader of the class table would not learn the difference. **F051** is the same family from the other direction: `EVAL_CALL_GRAPH_EXPECTED` tests only that the internal-call list is empty, `allowedCallGraphHash` is read by nothing, and deleting F051's policy override changes none of its three verdicts — D-025 ratified that the class cannot be driven to a positive case in v1, and the ablation now says so where the table is. **So §7.1's `reentrancy-attempt` and `unexpected-internal-call` classes are, at the corpus layer, covered by two fixtures that between them exercise neither.** The vault's Foundry suite does genuinely test reentrancy — including, since this session, the override path and the cross-path door. **Deferred with D-035, not fixed:** repairing either fixture changes the view the labels of record were drawn against. This is the third instance of the same defect class after A-028 F-5's vacuous injection class, and the generalisable lesson is now firm enough to state: **a fixture's class name is a claim about what it exercises, and nothing in the corpus checks that claim.** A mechanical check is possible — assert that each class's fixtures produce at least one failing check the class is about — and is not built.

- **D-036 (2026-08-15) — Three S2-session dispositions, ruled together.** Ruled by John at the facilitated decision session, accepting the recommendations put to him: *"let's follow your recommended ordering… and everything after."* **(a) The Gate 7 canary runs MONTHLY, and a DRIFT row is a finding about the model, never a build failure.** D-007 already forbids failing CI; this settles the interval and the interpretation. A drifted canary does not invalidate containment — the pipeline blocks the approval whoever proposed it — it invalidates the claim that Case 2's fixture reflects CURRENT behaviour, which is a statement about the model and belongs in the evidence bundle as one. **(b) D-009's priority order stands: corpus > ablation > CLI.** Nothing is deferred; all three are complete, so the order is confirmed rather than exercised. **(c) A-029 is ACCEPTED AS BOUNDED, no further action.** The labeller views are not byte-reproducible and are not meant to be; each carries a digest with the two run-varying timestamp fields normalised out, and `./scripts/test.sh --gate` re-runs the corpus to a temporary directory and fails if the committed views are no longer the semantic output of the current code. The provenance claim is now "these views are semantically the ones that were labelled", which is checkable, rather than "these are the bytes", which is not.

- **D-035 (2026-08-15) — A-034 is resolved: MEASURE a targeted sample, then treat the offending PASSAGES as the defect rather than the labelling protocol. Supersedes A-034, whose reversal condition had fired.** Ruled by John at the facilitated decision session: *"let's follow your recommended ordering, including d then run c for A1."* **The measurement that reframed the fork:** 38 of the 50 labels of record cite a ratified decision in their spec basis or notes — but citing D-020 is not contamination, because D-020 IS the specification and a labeller reasoning from an amended clause is doing its job. Only **two** E labels state that a decision CHANGED the label (F016, F051), and both have been measured: H agreed with E on F016; K, denied D-025, labelled F051 INSUFFICIENT where E said ALLOW. **So the defect is not "labellers read rulings". It is that a handful of passages carry FIXTURE-SPECIFIC WORKED EXAMPLES together with their answers** — §4.2 Case 2 (D-019) walks through F049 and states the block; §5.7.1 (D-031) says "the wrong-policy fixture then ALLOWs"; D-025's text is F051's and F056's — **and that §5.7.1 publishes eleven of the evaluator's own reason-code identifiers**, which labeller J cited as `specBasis`. *[Annotated 2026-08-15, after the ruling, by count rather than by re-reading the summary: **that number is wrong — §5.7.1 publishes 41**, every check in the engine, grouped under seven headings with descriptions. The "eleven" is §5.7.1's own count of checks with no home in §5.7's prose, a different quantity in the same sentence. The contamination surface of that subsection is about four times what this entry records. **The ruling is unaffected** — part (c) turned on the KIND of defect, and the measurement bounding it (zero movements out of five, run twice) does not depend on the count. Annotated rather than rewritten, because this is John's ratified entry and only its arithmetic was wrong. See `docs/v1-1-register.md`.]* **RULED, part (d) — bound the effect with evidence before deciding anything else.** Run the control labeller (pre-amendment specification, `052b3af`, same model as E and F) over the five fixtures whose labels cite a decision that ALSO carries a worked example: **F001, F009, F025, F049, F056**. F051 and F016 are already measured and are not re-run. **RULED, part (c) — the passages are a v1.1 correction, not a v1 re-freeze.** `check-label-prompt.sh` states the cost of a new frozen prompt in terms: a new file, a new hash, and a re-label of everything scored under the old one. That would replace the labels of record and the disagreement figure, and the prompt is not the thing at fault. **THE CONDITION THAT ESCALATES THIS TO A FULL RE-FREEZE AND RE-LABEL, declared in advance so it is checkable rather than rhetorical: if two or more of the five come back moved, the channel is systematic and the sample has stopped being a bound.** One movement is consistent with F051 being the known case; two is a pattern. **EXECUTED THE SAME DAY — labeller L, 5 of 5 agreeing with the labels of record. ZERO movements, against a threshold of two.** Part (c) therefore stands and no escalation is owed. **AND REPLICATED, BY ACCIDENT — labeller M, a second control arm of identical design run blind to L from a concurrently-open session (see A-037). E, L and M agree on all five, and L and M match on CONFIDENCE as well — both `medium` on F025, both `high` on the other four, both marking F025's governing rule as inferred rather than stated. Ten control observations, zero movements.** The replication is real evidence and is stronger than this decision required; it is also an accident, and is recorded as one rather than as a design. The sufficiency signal repeated for the third time: L marked F025 “does not decide it — I inferred” and recommended it be escalated individually rather than averaged, which is the same shape H and K produced — the amendments RAISE confidence and REMOVE inference rather than supplying answers. **Counting the whole programme: six independent labellers, two specification versions, two models, over F001, F009, F012, F016, F024, F025, F026, F038, F044, F046, F049, F050, F051 and F056 — ONE label moved (F051), and it was the one E had itself flagged.** **What is NOT ruled here and stays open for v1.1:** which passages to amend and how — removing a worked example from §4.2 edits the specification to serve the measurement, which D-035 does not authorise. **Two corpus defects ride on the same decision and are therefore deferred with it:** F032 does not isolate policy expiry, and F026/F051 pin different call-graph hashes over an identical observed call graph. Fixing either changes the view the labels of record were drawn against, so both are v1.1 work that belongs WITH a re-label rather than before one.

- **D-034 (2026-08-15) — The §7.3 layer partition gets a CRITERION, and applying it cuts the mandate-conformance figure from 17 to 8. Amends the L2/L3 split; resolves the question A-028 opened and an independent review reopened.** Ruled by John at the facilitated decision session: *"Ok, let's go with C."* **The criterion, ratified:** a check belongs to L3-only if and only if it compares the CALL or its SIMULATED EFFECTS against the mandate's PURPOSE fields — resource, beneficiary, duration, recurrence, and the post-state those imply. Everything else belongs to L2: mandate validity, binding, identity, ceilings, and code identity. **That is §7.2's own sentence and nothing more:** "No resource, beneficiary, duration, recurrence, or post-state constraint." **What decided it:** `layers.ts` defines L2 as "the full engine with the mandate-to-effects conformance checks removed", so **L2 HAS the mandate** — it simply never asks what the mandate was FOR. An arm holding a mandate can check that it is active, in window, bound to the active policy, and names the owner as principal. Withholding those modelled no product and credited purpose conformance with work that has nothing to do with purpose. **MEASURED, not predicted.** Nine codes moved. False allows: L1 38 (unchanged), **L2 17 → 8**, L3 1 (unchanged). Exact match: L1 12/50, **L2 32/50 → 41/50**, L3 49/50. Detection contribution: baseline alone 9, **effect extraction 20 → 29**, **mandate conformance 17 → 8** — and the 8 are exactly F012, F013, F014, F015, F016, F046, F050, F055, the wrong-purpose class. L3 still false-allows one fixture where L2 false-allows eight, so the ladder shows a real gap and it is now precisely the gap §4.2 Case 3 claims. **THE COUNTER-ARGUMENT, RECORDED AT FULL STRENGTH BECAUSE IT IS THE OBVIOUS OBJECTION:** the differentiation headline falls by more than half on the eve of S2, on a reading of one sentence, and a figure that moves that far invites the question of whether the earlier one was ever load-bearing. The answer offered and accepted is that 8 is the honest version of the claim, that a smaller number surviving scrutiny is worth more than a larger one that does not, and that the larger figure had ALREADY produced two findings — A-028 caught `EVAL_CALLDATA_BINDING` misclassified, and a review then caught F023 doing the same through `EVAL_ACTION_BINDS_MANDATE_AND_POLICY`. Patching instances left the category error standing; the criterion is what stops a third. **THE CONDITION THAT WOULD REVERSE THIS:** if L2 is read as an arm with NO mandate at all, then mandate-validity checks are things it structurally cannot perform, they belong in L3, and the pre-D-034 partition was right. That reading is available in §7.3's prose and the whole ruling turns on rejecting it in favour of `layers.ts`'s explicit definition. **Made mechanical, because a hand-written list with a criterion in prose is a list that drifts a third time:** `ablation.test.ts` now fails if any member falls outside the purpose/post-state naming, and fails individually if any of the nine moved codes is readmitted; and the report emits the criterion as a CHECK — a second, non-empty row in its own table means drift, and it must be empty.

- **A-034 (2026-08-15) — AGENT CALL, taken under John's delegation and flagged as reversible: the labelling protocol is NOT re-frozen now. The J-1/J-2 findings are recorded and carried to S2 as a scope question.** **The findings:** A-033 established that the specification's worked examples are systematic rather than a single walkthrough — §5.7.1's own note names "the wrong-policy fixture" together with its answer, D-020's text is F016's worked example, D-029's is F046's — and that §5.7.1 publishes eleven of the evaluator's own reason-code identifiers, which labeller J cited as `specBasis`. **The available responses all invalidate evidence.** `check-label-prompt.sh` states the cost in terms: a new frozen prompt requires a new file, a new hash, and a re-label of everything already labelled under the old one. That is all 50 fixtures, and it would replace the labels of record and the disagreement figure days before a gate that rests on them. **Why I am not doing it:** A-033 MEASURED the channel and it moved no label — four labellers, two specification versions, two models, 9 of 9 agreement. There is no evidence of harm to act on, and destroying the current evidence base to re-measure a channel that has just been measured is a poor trade. **What I am doing instead:** the finding is recorded in A-033 and in the S2 pack's §11, the labels of record keep their disclosed qualification, and the question of what labellers should be given in future rounds goes to S2 as a v1.1 scope item rather than a v1 remediation. **TRIGGERED, 2026-08-15, the same day it was written.** The first reversal condition below fired: control labeller K disagreed with a label of record (F051) on material the specification supplied, and labeller E had already said in its own note that it would have labelled differently without D-025. The premise this call rested on — "A-033 MEASURED the channel and it moved no label" — is false as stated. **This decision is therefore NOT the build loop's to keep**, and it goes to John with the measurement attached rather than being re-taken here. **What would reverse this, and it is deliberately a low bar because the call is mine and not John's:** any labeller disagreement traceable to spec-supplied material; a new labelling round for any reason, at which point the protocol should be settled BEFORE the round rather than after; or John simply preferring the re-freeze, which needs no justification beyond his saying so. **Recorded as an agent call rather than done silently** because it decides what a ratified decision (D-011) continues to mean, and the build loop's bias runs toward the option that preserves its own evidence.

- **A-033 (2026-08-15) — D-033's measurement, executed. Both arms agree with the labels of record on every fixture; the channel is real, broader than A-030 recorded, and did not move a single label.** **The design:** two single-variable arms over the same nine fixtures — F012, F016, F024, F025, F038, F044, F046, F049, F050, chosen because a post-freeze amendment could plausibly decide each. **Labeller H (control)** read the specification as of `052b3af`, before the §4.2 walkthrough and before every 2026-08-15 amendment, on the same model as E and F. **Labeller J (diversity)** read the current specification on a different model. **THE RESULT ON THE NINE SAMPLED: E, H and J agree 9 of 9. Zero disagreements. Every label BLOCK.** **CORRECTED THE SAME DAY — the claim below was stated more broadly than the sample supports, and a review found the counterexample already on disk.** This entry originally said the channel 'moved no label' without qualification. **It moved one.** Labeller E's own note on F051 reads: *"Without D-025 I would have labelled this INSUFFICIENT."* D-025's spec amendment landed 13:48, after the 12:55 freeze and before E's 15:25 labels — the A-030 channel exactly, with a label consequence E stated itself. F051 was not in the nine sampled, and the sample criterion ("chosen because a post-freeze amendment could plausibly decide each") should have caught it: only TWO E labels carry a "without D-0xx I would have…" note, F016 and F051, and only F016 was measured. **Measured since, by control labeller K on the same pre-amendment snapshot: K labelled F051 INSUFFICIENT.** So the amendment did not merely raise confidence there — it decided the label. **Quantified:** an INSUFFICIENT at F051 is excluded from scoring, so exact match would read 11/49, 40/49, 48/49 rather than the published 12/50, 41/50, 49/50. False allows are unaffected — F051 is allowed by every layer and E's truth is ALLOW, so it is neither a false allow nor a detection under either label. **What still stands:** on the nine fixtures where an amendment could plausibly decide the outcome, four labellers across two spec versions and two models agreed unanimously. **What does not:** the unqualified sentence, which is withdrawn. Four labellers, two specification versions, two models. **The confound control resolved in the direction that matters.** H's confidence fell exactly where the amendments bear: E rated F016 and F044 `high`, H rated both `medium` and marked them "does not decide it — I inferred"; F024, F025 and F038 were "decides it with effort". So the amendments RAISED confidence and REMOVED inference — they did not supply answers, because every label was reachable from the pre-amendment text and H said so where the text was thin. **The sharpest confirmation is H's own headline finding, raised against the control text:** `failureMode` has no defined numeric encoding — all fixtures carry `"1"`, the spec names two values without saying which is which, and two inferences point in OPPOSITE directions. H called it "the single most actionable finding I have". **The current specification closes it exactly**: §5.9, added by D-024, states `FAIL_CLOSED = 0, REVIEW = 1` and warns that the Verdict numbering is the reverse of the document's prose order. The control snapshot has no §5.9. **The amendment H needed and did not have is one of the amendments A-030 flagged as the contamination channel.** **WHAT THE MEASUREMENT ALSO FOUND, AND IT IS LARGER THAN THE QUESTION IT ANSWERED.** J's `specBasis` citations show the channel is systematic rather than a single walkthrough: it cited amendment text as the basis for five of nine labels and named the effect itself — F016 "is essentially D-020's own worked example", F025 "is the literal wrong-policy fixture example named in D-031", F046 "appears purpose-built to test D-029", F038 turns on D-028's asymmetry note. **Verified directly:** §5.7.1's own note says an evaluator built from §5.7 alone "omits it, and the wrong-policy fixture then ALLOWs" — a second fixture scenario named together with its answer. And **§5.7.1 publishes eleven of the evaluator's own reason-code identifiers**, which J cited as `specBasis` for two labels; D-031's recorded caveat rejected making `EVAL_CODES` normative for exactly this reason and published the identifiers anyway "for auditability". **THE HONEST CONCLUSION, IN TWO PARTS, BECAUSE THEY POINT DIFFERENT WAYS.** (1) **The labels are robust.** Invariant across spec version and across model, with zero disagreements — the strongest evidence the corpus has produced about itself. (2) **The disagreement RATE is a weaker instrument than it looks.** Agreement is partly produced by shared, pointed material — the frozen prompt pre-digests §5.2 into imperatives sharper than the spec's own prose (H's finding), and the specification now carries worked examples and the implementation's code names. Convergence between labellers reading the same steering is not the same evidence as convergence between independent readings, and D-011(c)'s statistic should be read with that attached. **Nothing here is a defect and nothing is remediated by an agent:** whether to change what labellers are given is D-011's to amend, which is John's.

- **D-033 (2026-08-15) — A-030's contamination channel is MEASURED, not argued, and the labelling gains model diversity. Resolves the A-030 fork.** Ruled by John at the facilitated decision session: *"Ok, let's go with B as well as the item you flagged."* **The finding being answered:** the specification is the one source D-011(b) grants a labeller, and it has been amended 24 times inline — including a §4.2 Case 2 walkthrough that quotes F049's rationale nearly verbatim and then states the answer. That walkthrough landed 2026-07-30, **before the prompt freeze itself** (`9568467`, 2026-08-15 12:55), so every labeller of record read it. D-011(a)'s freeze protects the INSTRUCTIONS and not the MATERIAL, and nothing had noticed the difference. **Ruled: run a control re-label against the pre-amendment specification** — the fixtures the amendments could decide, scored by a labeller reading the spec as it stood at `052b3af` (before `b4ec4fa` introduced the walkthrough and before the 2026-08-15 amendments), compared against the labels of record. **And separately: add a labeller from a different model.** E, F and G are all `claude-opus-5`, so their agreement is three runs of one model reading one set of sources, and the 0.0% disagreement figure inherits that. **Rationale for measuring rather than recording-and-proceeding:** the channel's effect is checkable, and this project has settled a comparable question by measurement before — A-023 re-tested D-019's rejected branch instead of arguing it, and settled it more firmly than the reasoning had. **THE COUNTER-ARGUMENT, RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS A REAL CONFOUND:** a labeller reading the OLDER text faces genuine ambiguities the amendments fixed — pre-D-029 §5.2 does not say what happens when a failed rule and an unresolved check coincide — so a disagreement may be evidence that the amendment HELPED rather than that it contaminated. The measurement cannot separate those two by itself. **The control for the confound, which is the design's whole load-bearing part:** the frozen prompt already instructs a labeller to answer `INSUFFICIENT` when the material does not decide, so an `INSUFFICIENT` or a flagged ambiguity reads as "the amendment helped", while a CONFIDENT OPPOSITE label reads as "the amendment may have decided it". Only the second is evidence of contamination. **Design choices made by the build loop and recorded so they are auditable:** the control labeller (H) is given the historical specification under its ordinary filename and is NOT told it is historical, because telling it would change exactly the confidence calibration being measured; and H uses `claude-opus-5`, the same model as E and F, so the ONLY variable in that arm is the spec text. The diversity labeller (J) reads the CURRENT spec on the same fixtures, so its only variable is the model. **A limit on the diversity arm, stated rather than glossed:** the build loop can vary the model within one vendor's family; it cannot obtain a labeller from a different vendor. "Model diversity" here means a different Claude model, not an independent implementation of judgement. **Considered and rejected:** (a) record the limit and proceed to S2 — leaves the corpus's central claim resting on an independence the project has stated it cannot support; (d) strip the walkthrough from §4.2 — edits the specification to serve the measurement, and §4.2 Case 2 is a demonstration case the proposal needs. **THE CONDITION THAT WOULD REVERSE THIS:** if the control disagrees materially, with confidence, on the fixtures the amendments touch, then option (c) — a new frozen prompt with an explicit rule about amendment text, and a re-label of all 50 under it — stops being optional. `scripts/check-label-prompt.sh` already states that cost in terms.

- **D-032 (2026-08-15) — §7.5's eight gates are split: six are S2 pass conditions, two are PRE-PUBLICATION conditions. Resolves a three-way wording conflict; amends D-008 and D-009.** Ruled by John at the facilitated decision session: *"let's go with C."* **The conflict:** §7.5's heading reads "Hard Gates" and lists all eight; D-008 is titled "§7.5 **soft**-gate definitions" and defines two of them; D-009 makes S2 pass on "§7.5 **hard**-gate evidence". Whether Gates 5 and 8 were S2 conditions therefore turned on which document's vocabulary won. **What decided it was an internal contradiction rather than a vocabulary preference:** D-008's Gate 8 requires reviewers be "given only the demo, dashboard, and README", while D-009 says "the evidence dashboard remains outside S2 unless John adds it at the gate". If Gate 8 were an S2 pass condition, D-009 would require and exclude the dashboard in the same ruling. **Ruled:** Gates 1, 2, 3, 4, 6 and 7 are S2 pass conditions. **Gate 5 (vendor honesty) remains an S2 condition** — it is mechanically checkable, its artifacts exist, and D-008 itself says the empty-column condition "is mechanically checkable", language that only makes sense for something enforced. **Gate 8 (five-minute comprehension) becomes a PRE-PUBLICATION condition** alongside D-016's rename gate: it needs a finished artifact, John's five held questions, and the dashboard D-009 defers. The split falls on a real line — Gate 5 asks whether the artifact tells the truth about others and is checkable now; Gate 8 asks whether a stranger understands it, which is a condition on going public rather than on the mechanism being proven. **Recorded against my own recommendation, because the bias runs one way:** this is the reading that makes S2 reachable soonest, which is the direction a build loop is structurally biased toward, and the reason it still holds is the dashboard contradiction — structural, not convenient. The counter-argument is also recorded: D-002 calls S2 "the proof artifact" gate, and comprehension is arguably part of what makes an artifact a proof; the answer is that D-009 had already narrowed S2 deliberately, and the dashboard exclusion is the evidence of that intent. §7.5's heading is amended in ONE place so a future reader does not re-derive this.

## Agent assumptions (flagged, cheap to reverse)
- **A-032 (2026-08-15) — Three independent adversarial reviews of the same day's work found two blockers and fourteen further defects. All are remediated; the two blockers are the entry.** Run under D-017's conditions on a frozen tree at `074dda7`, three fresh-context lenses (the injection-class remediation, the Solidity harness, and an honesty pass over every claim committed that day), each told to prove the work fails and each required to report what it attacked and could NOT break. **Blocker 1, documentation:** A-030's bounding claim was refuted by one `git log` — see the correction inside A-030 itself. **Blocker 2, code, and it is the project's signature defect committed by the change that was remediating an earlier instance of it:** six of ten mutations against the injection-class wiring survived a 349/349 green suite, including deleting the leak check from `run.ts` outright, because every test for the feature drove fixture data or the pure guard and none drove the pipeline — `test.sh` never runs the corpus. **The guard was also weaker than its own description:** it paired FILTERED words, so 10 of F049's 17 probes matched strings that never existed and the injection payload could reach the signed bundle verbatim with the guard green. **The vault review produced five NEW survivors**, three of them properties the contract's own comments claim to enforce — `nonReentrant` droppable from `executeWithOverride` (a demonstrated double-spend on two shapes, since `_entered` is shared and the door is any path re-entering any other), the override's binding to one exact review receipt untested (owner consent transferable across decision records), and `CallFailed` asserted by nothing (a consumed nonce and an `ActionExecuted` log for an action that did not happen). **What each review confirmed matters as much as what it broke**, and all three reported it: the 13 backstop tests each genuinely kill the mutation they are named for; the four override handler arms genuinely reach `executeWithOverride` (instrumented, zero early rejections outside legitimate pauses); all three new override invariants can fail; build failure is genuinely separated from test failure; the leakage key-walk has no bypass under the current view shape; and every re-derivable number in the S2 pack re-derived correctly. **Two hypotheses the reviews raised and did not demonstrate, recorded so they are not lost.** (a) `leakage.ts`'s `isDeclared` is depth-blind and `assertViewShape` checks only the top level and `observedEnvironment`, so an allowlisted key name nested inside `mandate`, `policy` or `action` would bypass both — pre-existing, and no live path was constructed, since all three are typed payloads. (b) `contracts/foundry.toml` sets no fuzz `seed`, so any mutation whose only killer is an invariant is a single sample from a random process; "0 survived" is not reproducible by construction. Neither was demonstrated to bite; both are cheap to close and neither was closed under time pressure. **The generalisable lesson, and it is not "review more":** two of the three blockers were things the build loop had *already written a check for* and had checked in the wrong place — a guard tested by unit tests nobody called, and a mutation batch whose anchors had rotted. The instrument existing is not the instrument working.

- **A-031 (2026-08-15) — The five owed items from A-028 are built. Three agent-made design calls are recorded here because a future reader would otherwise re-derive them, and one of the three is the kind that should be reversible.** (a) **The Solidity mutation harness ships, and its result is 26 caught / 0 survived / 0 failed to apply** (20 vault mutations plus the 6 pre-existing simulate ones, `./scripts/mutate.sh S`). Two things about that number. It is **not** comparable to A-028's "29 of 45 survived", because the thirteen tests in `SentinelVault.backstops.t.sol` were written to kill exactly these mutants — the honest reading is "the instrument now exists and the tests it demanded exist", not "coverage improved by 29". And the harness reports a **mutant that does not compile as an ERROR, never as a catch**: `deny = "warnings"` means an unused variable breaks the build, and counting that as caught would credit the suite for the compiler's work and inflate the number gate evidence cites. Five of the twenty are declared CONTROLS aimed at checks the suite always covered, because a batch where everything is caught says nothing unless the harness can also report a survivor. (b) **Batch C had rotted and both rotted entries were erroring, which is its own finding.** C1 and C3 were written against the pre-A-028 leakage guard and had been reporting "anchor not unique" since the key-walk rewrite, so two of five corpus mutations stopped exercising the guard they were named for. Nothing false was published — errors are counted separately — but a mutation that cannot apply measures as much as a test that cannot fail. **A mutation harness needs its own re-run after the code it mutates is repaired**, and nothing in the project enforces that. (c) **Gate 5 is split into a mechanical half that fails the gate and a certification half that reports UNCERTIFIED and never a pass.** D-008 declares only the empty-column condition mechanically checkable, and the verification partition gives public claims autonomy none, so the two halves genuinely have different owners. The reversible part, flagged as such: **D-008(4) is enforced as "no NAMED vendor may appear in any measurement artifact"** rather than as a scan for comparative phrasing. Chosen because D-008 forbids a *layout* implying superiority and layout has no vocabulary to grep for. **CORRECTED the same day:** this originally added "and because a phrase list is a denylist whose coverage is the spellings it declares", which an independent review rightly called out — the replacement is a denylist of ten NAMES, so it is stronger against layout, which was the argument, and no stronger against a vendor the list omits. Fireblocks, Turnkey, Dfns and Anchorage all pass. The limit is now stated in the script itself. It is stricter than the ruling's words — a v1 artifact could in principle name a vendor entirely honestly and this refuses it. If that ever blocks something worth writing, the fix is a ruling, not an exclusion-list entry.

- **A-030 (2026-08-15) — OPEN FORK, NOT RESOLVED: the specification has become a contamination channel for labellers, and the frozen prompt no longer matches it. Raised for John because changing the labelling protocol is a D-011 decision.** Found by labeller G, unprompted, in the provenance attestation for a two-fixture re-check (`fixtures/corpus/labels/labeller-G.provenance.json`). **The finding:** D-011(b) denies labellers the implementation, the tests and `docs/`, and grants them the specification. But the specification is no longer the document that was frozen against — it now carries the D-015 and D-019…D-031 amendments inline, and several of those were written IN RESPONSE TO EARLIER LABELLERS. Three instances, all in permitted text: **§4.2 Case 2, as amended by D-019, is effectively a walkthrough of F049** — it quotes that fixture's `agentRationale` nearly verbatim ("the settlement relayer", "a required one-time setup before purchasing the weather-basic-24h feed") and then states the answer, "The block comes from the decoded spender and amount against the mandate's allowance ceiling"; **§3.3(7)** records that "three independent labellers converged on the gap"; **§5.7.1** opens by crediting "an independent labeller working only from this document". G's own assessment is the useful part and is quoted rather than paraphrased: it derived BLOCK from the target and selector bindings before reaching that passage, "but I cannot honestly claim my F049 label is independent of it. Treat F049's `high` confidence as partly borrowed." **The second half of the finding:** the D-011(a) frozen prompt reproduces §3.3(8) and §5.2 as they stood BEFORE D-021, D-026, D-029 and D-030, so a labeller now faces two disagreeing permitted sources and must choose. G followed the live specification and says so. It mattered once — D-029 is what let it set F049's code-hash mismatch aside rather than weigh REVIEW against BLOCK. **Why this is not an agent's call.** Every available response is a change to a ratified protocol: re-freezing the prompt requires a new frozen version and a re-label of everything scored under the old one (`check-label-prompt.sh` says so in terms, and says do NOT repin); denying the amended specification would leave labellers unable to reason about the rulings the corpus now turns on; and pruning the fixture-specific prose from §4.2 would edit the specification to serve the measurement. **CORRECTED THE SAME DAY, BY A SECOND INDEPENDENT REVIEW, AND THE CORRECTION IS THE WHOLE POINT OF THE ENTRY.** This paragraph originally read: *"What is NOT in question: the labels of record stand. G agreed with E and F on both fixtures, and the amendments post-date rounds 1 and 2 rather than rounds 3's subject matter changing."* **Every clause of that is wrong**, and one `git log` refutes it. The §4.2 Case 2 walkthrough landed in `b4ec4fa` on **2026-07-30**, two weeks BEFORE any labelling round; `git show 1414d5f:Sentinel_Protocol_Lab_Proposal_v0_2.md` — the commit that introduces `labeller-E.json` and `labeller-F.json` — contains both it and the "three independent labellers" line.
