Two carry THIN briefs in round two's style — break an instrument, catch a false statement, no surfaces named. **The third's target is ASSIGNED, which no previous round did: mutation-sweep the five modules of the D-010 verifier that have never been swept** — `keccak.py`, `secp256k1.py`, `eip712.py`, `refusal.py`, `reasoncodes.py`, 890 lines. The reason for the departure is that A-049 closed one hole of exactly this shape (`keccak256(canonical bytes) matches evidence.hash`, a named check no tamper mode targeted) and then stated in the boundary and the register that it "was the only hole of this kind anybody has LOOKED for, not the only one that exists". **A gap the project has already named does not need discovering; it needs measuring.** The reviewer is told its target was assigned and instructed to score its findings there as DIRECTED rather than independent. **A CONSEQUENCE OF ASSIGNING IT, worth stating because it cuts both ways:** a NULL result from an assigned target is *stronger* evidence than a null from an open lens, because it means somebody actually looked where the gap was declared; a POSITIVE result is *weaker* as discovery but exactly as strong as a defect. **THE READING, declared before results, with the confounds.** **(a) A surviving mutation in the verifier — one that demonstrably changes behaviour and leaves the suite, the sample walk, the tamper suite and the gate green — means the generalisation gap is REAL and quantified. That outcome is unambiguous and is the only one that is.** **(b) Instruments hold and only prose findings appear** → evidence for the migration this project has been watching across three rounds: round one found instrument defects, round two found instrument defects plus prose, round three finding only prose would say the instruments have converged while the claims about them have not. Under D-046's reversal logic that is saturation for the instruments and a fourth round on the same commits would be unjustified. **(c) Nothing at all** → **AMBIGUOUS, and it must not be reported as vindication.** The same confound as round two applies with more force: the subject is smaller again, newer again, and written in direct response to round two, so a null is equally consistent with "the work is sound" and with "three rounds have exhausted what this technique finds here". **STOPPING RULE FOR THIS ROUND, declared now rather than after the results:** this is the last review round on this work **unless the sweep finds a surviving mutation** — that is, a real instrument defect, not a prose correction. Prose findings will be fixed but will not buy a fourth round. **Recorded because the alternative is deciding when to stop by how the results feel.**
- **A-051 (2026-08-17) — ROUND THREE. THE VERDICT CHECK WAS DEFEATED FOR THE THIRD TIME, AND A DIRECTED SWEEP FOUND 41 SURVIVING MUTATIONS IN THE VERIFIER. John scoped the remediation to the verdict-flippers and my false claims.** **THE SWEEP IS THE HEADLINE AND IT FIRED A-050's ONLY UNAMBIGUOUS BRANCH.** 142 behaviour-changing mutations over the six non-`verify.py` modules; **84 caught, 41 survived a fully green gate**, six of those verified end-to-end through `./scripts/test.sh` with the D-010 stage line byte-identical to baseline. So the generalisation gap A-049 declared open is real and now quantified rather than assumed. **THREE SURVIVORS FLIP A VERDICT and are closed.** **(1)** `_HEX_BODY` widened to admit whitespace makes two byte-distinct addresses encode to one ABI word — I reproduced it: `mandate.beneficiary` with two trailing spaces yields a colliding `mandateHash` and the gate PASSES, so the verifier certifies a mandate document that is not the one signed. **The existing witness inserted ONE space, which breaks hex-pair alignment and is therefore rejected by any pair-quantified pattern including a widened one — a test whose witness is structurally incapable of failing.** **(2)** `strict=False` at any of four `struct_hash` call sites lets unauthenticated fields ride inside a PASSing document; I reproduced `approvedBy` and `note: "cleared by legal"` in `mandate.json` with the gate green. Only the RECEIPT site had a test, and **no tamper mode over-populates any payload** — 24 modes cover substitution, removal, reordering and re-signing, never over-population. **(3)** `parse_signature` relaxed from `!= 65` to `< 65` accepts arbitrary trailing bytes, so unboundedly many byte-distinct signatures certify one receipt. **All three now have witnesses, each run against its mutation and confirmed to fail, and the `strict=` test covers ALL FOUR uncovered sites rather than the one the reviewer exploited** — the argument, not the demonstration. Suite 149 → 154. **THE VERDICT CHECK, DEFEATED A THIRD TIME, IS NOW BUILT ON A DIFFERENT PRINCIPLE.** v1 asserted the exit status (beaten by `@unittest.skip`); v2 asserted `Ran N >= 146` (beaten because unittest COUNTS skipped tests in `Ran N`); v3 asserted a bare `OK` parsed from `2>&1` — **beaten by two lines: the skip, plus `print("OK")`.** unittest writes its verdict to line-buffered stderr while a test file's stdout is block-buffered and flushed at interpreter exit, i.e. AFTER the verdict, so `tail -1` took the injected line and the gate printed `verdict OK` with all 149 assertions disabled. **Found independently by two reviewers in the same round, and I reproduced it.** **The argument all three versions missed: the file under test also writes the report the gate reads — parsing it is asking the suspect for its own alibi, and moving to stderr would not help because a test can write there too.** v4 reads the unittest RESULT OBJECT — `skipped`, `expectedFailures`, `unexpectedSuccesses`, `failures`, `errors` are structural properties no printing can alter — and writes the counts to a temp file rather than a stream the suite shares. Falsified against all four defeats including `print("OK")`, on a clean baseline first. It also fails closed on an unreadable report, because a crashed runner must not read as clean. **FOUR OF MY OWN CLAIMS WERE FALSE AND ARE CORRECTED.** "Distinct modes cannot be padded that way" — **false**: replacing the real `signature` mode with a registered no-op (added to `TAMPER_MODES`, added to `TAMPER_MUST_STILL_VERIFY` so it need not mutate anything) raised the pair count to 63 while the floors stayed green and the mode proving a corrupted receipt signature is rejected no longer existed. Both floors are ratchets against accident, not intent; only `evidence-hash` has a structural test naming it. "Absolute values vary with chain time" — **false for `expiryBefore`**, which is the constant `"0"` in 35 of 36 views; and the relation comparison is TYPE-BLIND, so a view can change its JSON schema undetected. "The verifier's other **five** modules" — **six**; I omitted `jcs.py`, the RFC 8785 canonicalizer, in three documents AND in the sweep's own brief. "**Four** `ts/src` files" — five, caught independently by two reviewers inside a claim I had labelled "measured rather than assumed". And `decisions.md`'s A-049 restated the casing residual as lower-case-only, **which is the exact error A-048 recorded A-047 making**: `SaFe` and `sAfE` evade, my seventeen probes contained no mixed case, and I generalised past my own sample. **MY BRIEF'S ERROR COST COVERAGE, AND THE SWEEP SAID SO.** The `jcs.py` omission meant the assigned sweep excluded the one module with a previously demonstrated thin margin. I caught it mid-run and sent a correction; the reviewer swept it and **two of its seven top-tier findings are in that file**, including a UTF-16 sort test whose witness pair (`{U+FFFD, U+1F600}`) orders identically under BE and LE, so the named check cannot fail. Without the mid-run correction the round loses both. **A directed sweep is only as complete as the list it is handed, and the selection of those six was still a list rather than a measurement.** **A-050's DECLARED READING WAS INCOMPLETE, and that is recorded rather than retrofitted.** It enumerated three outcomes; reality produced a fourth — a severe instrument defect found by the CLAIMS lens, which neither the "surviving mutation" branch nor the "prose only" branch describes. The stopping rule keyed on the sweep, and the sweep did fire, so the outcome is the same; but the taxonomy was wrong and pretending otherwise would be the failure this project studies. **WHAT IS DELIBERATELY LEFT, per John's scoping:** ~37 survivors, the `bytesN`/`uintN` coverage gap, complement-based charset tests, the BE/LE witness, the ERC-191 constant, the low-s boundary, and the `reason_codes_hash` defence-in-depth loss — all in `docs/v1-1-register.md` §9 with reproductions. **AND THE LARGEST MEASURED GAP IS NOW NAMED: `verify.py`, 1681 lines, the file that decides PASS or FAIL, has never been swept.** **A HARNESS TRAP worth more than several findings:** a same-SIZE mutation landing in the same mtime second makes CPython reuse stale `__pycache__`, so the mutated run executes clean bytecode and reads as a no-op — and same-size mutations are the interesting ones. **THE HONEST COUNTERWEIGHT:** `keccak.py` came back 17/17 caught with zero survivors and its four green results proven no-ops over 609 inputs; `jcs.py` caught 23/33 including the `1e-6` threshold in both directions. The well-covered parts are genuinely well covered. **LIMIT, fourth entry running: falsified only by its author.** Three rounds have now each broken the previous round's fixes.
- **A-052 (2026-08-17) — THE SECRET GUARD LET A REAL PRIVATE KEY THROUGH, AND THE REGISTER WAS HIDING IT AMONG COSMETIC ITEMS. Fixed and re-filed at John's direction, at a facilitated walkthrough.** **THE DEFECT.** `check-secrets.sh` suppressed any line matching `(YOUR_|REPLACE_|EXAMPLE|PLACEHOLDER|xxx|\.\.\.)` **anywhere on the line**. Two of those alternatives are catastrophic in this repository: `\.\.\.` matches `...`, the TypeScript spread operator, in a codebase written in TypeScript; and `EXAMPLE` matches an ordinary trailing comment. **A real 64-hex private key bound to `privateKey:` therefore passed the guard — and the pre-commit hook that depends on it — whenever the line happened to contain a spread or the word EXAMPLE.** Reproduced with a control before being believed: the identical key with the spread replaced by an explicit field is BLOCKED, so the syntax and only the syntax was the difference. Found by two independent reviewers on 2026-08-16 and relayed twice before anybody, including me, actually ran it. **THE FIX.** The placeholder markers are anchored to the VALUE — `[:=][[:space:]]*["']?(0x)?MARKER` — instead of being matched anywhere on the line, and `\.\.\.` is deleted outright, because spread is syntax and never a placeholder. **Falsified in BOTH directions against controls, which is the half that matters for a guard whose failure mode is crying wolf:** four real-key spellings (plain, with spread, with a `// EXAMPLE` comment, with a trailing `...`) are all blocked; four genuine placeholders (`YOUR_PRIVATE_KEY_HERE`, `PLACEHOLDER`, `xxx`, empty string) are all still suppressed; the clean tree stays green. **The shell-quoting idiom was reused from the file's own existing line rather than retyped** — the first attempt at retyping it unbalanced the quotes and broke the script, caught by `bash -n` before it ran. **THE FILING, which is the half John ruled on.** This sat in `v1-1-register.md` §8.2 between a `head -1` scoping nit and a note about a binary-extension allowlist, in a register with a deliberately flat structure, in a repository under a publication plan. **Everything else in §8 is an instrument that reports a property it does not check; this one's failure mode was a credential reaching a remote.** §8.2 now opens with a severity note saying so explicitly and instructing the reader not to take the list order as an ordering. The two remaining `check-secrets.sh` items — the line-wise `ANVIL_ALLOW` and the case-sensitive absolute-path regex — stay open and are genuinely lower severity: both need an author to write something odd on purpose rather than to use ordinary language syntax. **NO KEY WAS EVER COMMITTED;** the tracked tree was checked. **The general lesson, and it is about the register rather than the guard: a flat list of findings is itself a claim — that they are comparable — and this one was false for two days while three review rounds ran past it.**
- **A-053 (2026-08-17) — A FOURTH SWEEP, SCOPED TO `verify.py` ALONE. Ruled by John at the facilitated walkthrough; the reading is declared here before any result.** **Authority:** A-050's stopping rule was "last round unless the sweep finds a surviving mutation"; it found 41, so the exception fired on its intended branch. John scoped this narrowly — one directed reviewer at one file, not a three-lens round. **WHY THIS FILE.** `verify.py` is 1681 lines with 79 named `Check(...)` constructions, it decides PASS or FAIL, and **it has never been swept**. The sweep that found 41 survivors covered the six modules it IMPORTS — 890 lines. The deciding file is twice that and unmeasured. A separate reviewer also reported that **32 of the 79 named checks are never made to fail by any tamper mode**; that figure is unverified by me and the sweep is asked to verify, correct or refute it. **THE READING, DECLARED FIRST.** **(a) A survivor that lets the verifier CERTIFY something it should reject** — unambiguous, and the only outcome that is; it would mean the deciding file carries the same construction-pinned/validation-unpinned asymmetry its dependencies do. **(b) Survivors that only degrade diagnostics** — real but lower value, and consistent with a file whose acceptance paths are well covered. **(c) Nothing survives** — that would make `verify.py` better covered than the six modules around it (41 of 142 survived there), which is surprising enough that I would want to see the sweep's size and its no-op controls before believing it. **A null here is bounded by one sweep's imagination and must not be reported as "verify.py is sound".** **STOPPING RULE:** this is the last sweep on this work unless it finds a survivor of kind (a). Kind (b) findings get recorded, not escalated. **ONE DELIBERATE CHANGE TO THE BRIEF, from A-051's failure:** the reviewer is explicitly invited to report that the brief itself is wrong or misdirected. Last round my brief named five modules when there were six, and the omitted file held two of the round's best findings; that error was mine and it cost coverage until I caught it mid-run. A brief is an instrument like any other here and nothing was checking it.
- **D-047 (2026-08-17) — THE REVIEW LOOP'S TERMINATING CONDITION, SET BY JOHN AND NOT RE-AUTHORABLE BY AN AGENT. Supersedes the ad-hoc stopping rules in A-046, A-050 and A-053.** **THE RULE: adversarial review of this work continues until ONE FULL ROUND PRODUCES NO FINDING THAT WOULD CHANGE CODE OR A CLAIM. That round is the last one.** **WHY THE RULE EXISTS AT ALL, stated plainly because it is a finding about the agent rather than the code.** I wrote a stopping rule three times — A-046 ("last round unless SEVERE"), A-050 ("unless the sweep finds a surviving mutation"), A-053 ("unless it certifies something false") — and **each time the exception fired and I ran another round.** Every one was defensible in isolation. But a rule whose author re-authors it whenever it is about to bind is not a stopping rule; it is a formality attached to a decision already taken. The loop was terminating on my judgment while appearing to terminate on a condition. **THE MITIGATION IS THE OPERATIVE HALF: an agent may NOT re-author, narrow, reinterpret or attach exceptions to this rule mid-loop. Only John changes it.** An agent that believes the rule is wrong must say so and stop, not amend it. **DEFINITIONS, fixed here so "clean" cannot be gerrymandered later.** **A ROUND** is at least one independent reviewer, at a frozen commit, on its own worktree, briefed to prove the work fails, reporting under the standing format including a provenance attestation. **CLEAN** means: no finding from that round results in a change to code, a comment, a document, or a recorded claim — checkable from `git log`, since a clean round produces no commits beyond the record of the round itself. A round that finds only things I decline to fix is NOT clean; declining is a change to a claim. **ANTI-GAMING, because the obvious exploit is mine.** A round scoped narrowly enough to come back clean would satisfy the letter and defeat the rule. **So the scope of a round may not shrink relative to the previous round without John ruling it** — and where a round is deliberately narrow (as A-053's `verify.py` sweep is, at John's direction), its cleanliness does not end the loop; only a round of at least the previous round's breadth can. **CONSEQUENCE FOR THE RUNNING SWEEP:** A-053's own stopping rule ("last sweep unless it certifies something false") is superseded. Whatever that sweep returns, the loop ends only when a round of full breadth comes back clean. **THE COUNTER-ARGUMENT, recorded because John heard it and ruled anyway:** the condition is unbounded in principle, the base rate is roughly fifty findings across three rounds, and Sentinel is a portfolio lab whose §7.1 already disclaims general accuracy — so a verifier hardened against its twentieth sweep may be well past what the artifact's purpose requires, while the project's actual blocking gates (Gate 8, the D-016 rename) are untouched by any of this. **Reversal condition:** if two consecutive rounds return findings that are exclusively documentation corrections with no code change, that is evidence the instruments have converged and the loop is measuring prose; bring D-047 back to John rather than continuing on the letter of it.
- **A-054 (2026-08-17) — CHARSETS PINNED BY COMPLEMENT, NOT BY BAD LIST. The parallel-safe half of the register work, done while the `verify.py` sweep runs.** John's instruction was to wait for the sweep and then do the register items in one pass; this is the subset that touches files the sweep is not in (`reasoncodes.py`, `refusal.py`), which I had flagged as safe to parallelise. **THE DEFECT CLASS.** Both charsets were pinned by an ENUMERATED LIST OF BAD INPUTS, which pins those spellings and nothing else. A-051's sweep widened them and nothing noticed: `+`, tab, CR and backslash all accepted when added to the reason-code class (space and newline were the only spellings the old test covered), and §5.5.1's width bounds asserted only the SHORT side, so `{64}` → `{64,}` and `{40}` → `{40,}` let over-length `actionHash` and `signer` values through, along with a trailing-space verdict name. **§5.5.1's charsets are the entirety of its injectivity argument**, so half of that argument was unasserted. **THE FIX IS A DIFFERENT KIND OF TEST.** `TestCharsetsByComplement` walks the character space — all 128 ASCII codepoints plus four non-ASCII that have bitten this project — and asserts the accept/reject PARTITION against the declared class. Any widening fails regardless of which character was added, which is the property an enumerated bad list cannot have. Width bounds are asserted on both sides; verdict names are asserted to admit no padding, no case variation and no trailing newline. **THE TESTS TRANSCRIBE THE DECLARED CLASSES RATHER THAN IMPORTING THE PATTERNS UNDER TEST, deliberately.** A test that reuses the pattern it checks cannot detect that pattern changing — which is exactly the ERC-191 defect the same sweep found in `test_an_eip191_signature_fails_and_says_so`, where the test builds its input with the function under test so any mutation applies to both sides and it passes regardless. Avoiding it here cost one comment and is the difference between a witness and a mirror. **FALSIFIED AGAINST SIX MUTATIONS, each run with `-B` and a cleared `__pycache__`** because A-051 established that a same-size mutation in the same mtime second makes CPython execute stale bytecode and read as a no-op: `+` added to the class, a tab added, the upper length bound dropped, HASH32 widened, ADDRESS widened, and a trailing-space verdict name — **all six now fail, and the clean tree stays green.** Suite 154 → 158. **TWO OF MY OWN ERRORS, caught by running the baseline first rather than by the mutations:** the tests called `refusal.validate_value`, which does not exist (the function is `validate_field(name, kind, value)`), and that produced three ERRORs on a clean tree — a test that errors on clean code would have "caught" every mutation for the wrong reason, which is the always-fires failure mode from A-048. **STILL OPEN and stated so it is not read as closed:** `_HEX_BODY`'s full character class (only its whitespace case is closed, A-051), and the fact that asserting an injectivity argument is not proving one.
- **A-055 (2026-08-17) — `verify.py` SWEPT FOR THE FIRST TIME: TWO LIVE CERTIFICATION DEFECTS NEEDING NO MUTATION, 14 LATENT VERDICT-FLIPPERS, AND MY CORE INFERENCE REFUTED. John scoped the fix to the live defects plus the missing tamper mode.** **THE TWO LIVE DEFECTS ARE THE HEADLINE, because neither needed a mutation — both were shipped behaviour, and I reproduced both against the unmutated verifier before believing them.** **O-2, the worst finding of the session: THE PRESENTER OF A BUNDLE CHOSE THE TRUST ROOT.** `_find_domain` searched the BUNDLE directory before the parent, and `domain.json` carries `signerAddress` — the one thing binding a receipt to the deployment's signer rather than to whoever signed it. A receipt re-signed with an arbitrary outsider key, with `receipt.signer` set to that key's address and a `domain.json` naming it, verified **`=> PASS`, exit 0**. Under `--all`, one hostile directory inside a corpus overrode the signer identity for itself and the run still printed `N/N verified`. **The D-010 verifier exists to establish independently that a receipt came from the deployment's signer, and it could be told what that meant by the party presenting the receipt.** Fixed: `--domain` wins, then the DEPLOYMENT's copy in the parent; a bundle-supplied copy is used only when no deployment copy exists, and that case now FAILS via a named check — "the trust root is the deployment's, not the presenter's" — because a bundle plus its own idea of the signer is not a certifiable claim, and answering it with PASS was the defect. A bundle shipping a copy that CONTRADICTS the deployment's is an error, not a preference. Both paths verified. **O-1: an invariant silently skipped while the verifier stated something false about the bundle in front of it.** `refusal-vault-paused/receipt.json` — the only refusal artifact in the repository — puts `reasonCodes` inside `refusalRecord` and `signerFindings` at the TOP LEVEL, and the code consulted `receipt_doc` for findings only when `reasonCodes` was missing. So `findings` resolved to `None`, the subset check printed `[SKIP] the bundle carries no signerFindings array` about a bundle that carries one, and an uncommitted reason code injected there verified `=> PASS`, exit 0. `test_signer_findings_must_be_a_subset` co-locates both keys in one envelope, so it never exercised the corpus's own shape while `TestRefusalBundle`'s docstring claimed both were covered. Fixed: findings resolve independently of `published`, and the message now states where it looked. **THE MISSING MODE: `receipt-wrongkey`.** `override-wrongkey` and `refusal-wrongkey` both existed; the PRIMARY §5.4 artifact had neither, and that absence is the single reason the receipt's binding to the deployment signer was asserted by nothing — neutering `recovered == domain_signer` left all 154 tests passing. The new mode re-signs with an OUTSIDER key and rewrites `receipt.signer` to match, so the bundle is entirely self-consistent and only the deployment binding can reject it. **Falsified: that same neutering now fails the suite and the tamper arm.** ~~Suite 154 → 158~~, tamper 62 → 68 over 25 modes. **[CORRECTED 2026-08-17 (A-065, from round five's B-5): THE SUITE FIGURE IS FALSE AND WAS COUNTED TWICE.** This commit (`3bb4b21`) added **zero** test methods — verified with `git show 3bb4b21 -- verifier/test_verifier.py | grep -c '^+    def test_'`. A-054's commit (`6b4d213`) added four and made the 154 → 158 transition, and A-054's entry claims it too, so the same four tests are counted in two consecutive entries. **The TAMPER figure stands** — this commit did add `receipt-wrongkey`, which is the mode the entry is about. The error is the one this project keeps finding in its own numbers: a figure attached to the entry being written rather than to the change that produced it, in the log John's rulings are read from.**]** **MY CORE INFERENCE IS REFUTED AND RETIRED, and this is the most useful thing the round produced.** I wrote "a named check that no mode targets is a check nothing asserts" in A-049 and let it become load-bearing across three entries. Measured in BOTH directions: of 33 checks no mode ever makes fail, 18 probed → **10 CAUGHT** by the unit suite, so being un-targeted says nothing; and of checks that DO fail under some mode, 10 neutered → **5 SURVIVED**, because the mode is caught by a *different* check failing alongside, so the matrix scores them covered while nothing asserts them. **The tamper matrix is not a coverage measure; mutation is.** Retired in `verify.py`, in A-049 in place, and in the register. The "32 never-failed" figure it rested on is CORRECT but under-counts: 79 sites, 46 ever constructed, **24 ever made to fail**, and 33 never constructed at all. **THE BRIEF WAS WRONG AGAIN, in a second way, and the reviewer said so because I asked it to.** Beyond the refuted inference, my scoping was too narrow: `_find_domain` and the trust-root question are inside `verify.py` but are not a check-mutation question, so a pure `Check(...)` sweep would have missed **the one defect that needs no mutation to exploit**. Inviting the reviewer to criticise the brief — added to A-053 after A-051's five-versus-six error — is what surfaced that, and it is now the second consecutive round where the brief was a defect. **13 LATENT SURVIVORS RECORDED, not fixed** (register §11), clustering on the override stage and cross-artifact agreement. **The recurring shape is worth more than the list: a test that asserts a property of the CORPUS rather than of the VERIFIER** — `test_only_review_receipts_carry_an_override` asserts no fixture overrides a BLOCK, which cannot catch a verifier that accepts one. Three of the 13 survive on that confusion alone. **ALSO: the suite has no anchor test at all.** **UNDER D-047 THIS ROUND WAS NOT CLEAN, so the loop continues** — and it was a deliberately narrow round, so its result could not have ended the loop either way. **Environment, not a code finding:** three `ts/src/signer/main.ts` processes from the live tree have been running 21+ hours, leaked from earlier gate runs; flagged to John rather than killed.
- **A-056 (2026-08-17) — THE OVERRIDE CLUSTER, THE ANCHOR, AND THE CATEGORY ERROR BEHIND THREE OF THEM. John scoped it; two survivors deliberately left.** **THE CATEGORY ERROR IS THE FINDING; the six closed checks are its consequence.** Three of `verify.py`'s unasserted checks survived on one confusion: **a test that asserts a property of the CORPUS cannot catch a verifier that accepts what the corpus happens not to contain.** `test_only_review_receipts_carry_an_override` asserts no fixture overrides a BLOCK receipt — true, worth knowing, and completely silent on whether the verifier would accept one. The sweep changed §5.5's check to `verdict in ("REVIEW", "BLOCK")` and every test passed. `test_owner_is_not_the_sentinel_signer` and `test_review_receipt_hash_is_the_receipt_hashstruct` have the same shape. **A fixture property says what the repository CONTAINS; a verifier property says what the code ACCEPTS. Only the second checks the verifier**, and the distinction is now named in a test class docstring so the next author meets it rather than rediscovering it. **FOUR RE-SIGNING TAMPER MODES, and re-signing is the whole point.** `override-repoint` (no mode touched those three bindings at all), `override-nonce-resigned`, `override-signer-mints` and `receipt-anchor-split`. The two pre-existing override modes could never witness their bindings: `override-nonce` bumps a SIGNED field and leaves the old signature, so the signature check fires first; `override-wrongkey` signs as the Sentinel signer but leaves `ownerAddress` declaring the owner, so the recovery check fires and §3.3(7) — *the isolated signer must not be able to mint the owner's credential* — never bites. **The new modes re-sign, producing bundles that are entirely self-consistent and wrong in exactly one binding.** `override-signer-mints` declares the Sentinel signer AS the owner and signs with its key, which is §3.3(7)'s threat in its exact form. **ALL FOUR FALSIFIED THE ONLY WAY THAT MEANS ANYTHING: each targeted check was neutered and the mode confirmed to stop catching it** — a mode whose target is caught by a *different* check alongside is worthless, which is exactly what A-055 measured and why the tamper matrix is not a coverage measure. **THE ANCHOR HAD NO TEST AT ALL** — not a weak one, none — so `receipt-anchor-split` splits `simulationBlockHash` from the evidence's anchor inside the signed body and re-signs with the deployment key, leaving a valid, correctly-attributed receipt anchored to a block the evidence was not gathered at. **§5.5's BLOCK-override got a CONSTRUCTED-ARTIFACT test rather than a mode**, because no fixture contains one — which is the very reason the corpus-property test could not see it. It is built by DERIVING from the genuine override rather than hand-assembling a payload: my first version hand-built one and it raised inside `struct_hash`, a test erroring for a reason unrelated to §5.5, which is the same defect class one layer up. It is paired with a test asserting the constructed override's signature is genuinely valid, **so the rejection cannot be passing because the bundle was malformed.** Falsified: with §5.5 neutered the suite fails; before this test existed that mutation was invisible. **Suite 158 → 160, tamper 68 → 77 over 29 modes.** **TWO SURVIVORS DELIBERATELY LEFT, with the reason stated rather than omitted:** receipt malleability's `(r, N−s, v^1)` boundary and the contrived `verdict: 7` case. Every test added inside `verifier/` erodes the independence D-010 rests on — the artifact's value comes partly from having been built without knowledge of the implementation — and for the marginal survivor that cost is not obviously worth paying. **Recorded so it is a judgement rather than an omission.** **ALSO, from Item 1 of the same walkthrough:** three leaked signer processes were killed and seven stale sockets cleared, oldest from 2026-07-30. **Their sockets vanished on SIGTERM before the `rm` ran, which means the signer's teardown WORKS** — the leak is a parent dying without signalling its child, a smaller and different defect than "teardown is missing", and the "fix the teardown" option John declined would have been fixing the wrong thing. Gate re-verified green after the kill, and a clean run now spawns and reaps its own with zero sockets left behind.
- **D-048 (2026-08-17) — THE REVIEW LOOP SEQUENCES BEFORE PRE-PUBLICATION. Ruled by John; resolves a collision between D-047 and the §14.8 pre-publication path that NEITHER ruling mentioned.** **THE COLLISION, found by checking rather than assumed.** D-047 says adversarial review continues until one full round returns clean, and that scope may not shrink. The pre-publication programme fires D-043(b)'s re-label trigger, which replaces the labels of record and **invalidates every figure in `docs/gate-s2-evidence.md`** — which is exactly what the claims-lens reviewers verify, round after round. `grep` confirms D-047 contains no mention of pre-publication and the pre-publication path contains no mention of review or D-047: they were authored in different contexts and collide. **THE RULING: pre-publication does not start until a full-breadth review round returns clean.** **The reasoning is the base rate, not a principle.** Round four found **two LIVE certification defects in shipped code** — `_find_domain` letting the presenter of a bundle choose its own trust root, and an invariant silently skipped while the verifier printed something false about the artifact in front of it. Round three found a live path for a real private key to reach a remote. **Publishing a verifier while one's own review loop is still finding it certifying outsider-minted receipts is precisely the honesty defect this project exists to study.** Secondarily: a round started now audits figures the re-label is about to discard, which wastes the round and muddies D-047's cleanliness test. **A NUANCE THAT MATTERS AND IS PART OF THE RULING: a clean round is a PRECONDITION for pre-publication, not a TRIGGER for it.** When the loop closes, the programme still requires John's separate authorisation — nothing here pre-authorises it, and an agent that reads a clean round as permission to begin has misread this entry. **THE COUNTER-ARGUMENT, recorded because it is strong and John ruled against it:** Sentinel is a portfolio lab whose value is being seen; two days have gone into hardening a private repository; D-047 is unbounded in principle; and the gates that would let anyone see the artifact — Gate 8 and D-016's rename — have not moved at all. **There is a version of "finish the loop first" that is simply never shipping, and this ruling is exposed to it.** **WHAT MAKES THE EXPOSURE TOLERABLE:** the findings are converging in KIND — round one was almost entirely instruments, round four was mostly `verify.py` internals with the prose largely holding — which reads as one or two more rounds rather than ten. **REVERSAL CONDITIONS, two:** (a) D-047's own — two consecutive rounds returning documentation-only findings means the loop is measuring prose, and it comes back to John; (b) new here — **if a round returns severe INSTRUMENT defects at round one's rate, the loop is not converging, and an agent must SAY SO and hand the decision back rather than continue on the letter of this ruling.** Grinding through rounds because the rule says so, while the evidence says the technique has stopped narrowing, would be the same defect as re-authoring a stopping rule whenever it binds.

- **A-057 (2026-08-17, agent-made call under D-047) — ROUND FIVE: A FULL-BREADTH ADVERSARIAL REVIEW ROUND, AND ITS READING IS DECLARED HERE BEFORE ANY RESULT.** **Authority: John triggered it.** `session-state.md` §1 named a full-breadth round as the next move and said John triggers it; he did, at the top of this session, against that handoff. **This entry contains NO stopping rule and may not.** D-047 fixes the terminating condition and forbids an agent re-authoring, narrowing, reinterpreting or attaching exceptions to it. The loop ends when a full-breadth round produces no finding that would change code or a claim, and not otherwise; if this round finds nothing, that is D-047's condition being met and not a rule I wrote. **SCOPE, and why it does not shrink — D-047's anti-gaming clause is the binding constraint here.** Round three was three reviewers over a commit range; round four was one directed reviewer over one file (1830 lines), deliberately narrow at John's direction, so under D-047 its result could not have closed the loop either way. **Round five is eight independent reviewers over the WHOLE TREE at frozen `8234aba`** — Solidity, TypeScript, Python, shell and the documents — which is strictly wider than either. Each gets its own detached `git worktree` at that commit with `ts/node_modules` and `contracts/lib/*` symlinked read-only, is told to prove the work fails, is invited to report that its own brief is wrong, and must return a provenance attestation and a statement of what it did NOT cover. **THE BASELINE WAS MEASURED FIRST, on the untouched tree, because this project has shipped a check that failed on every run including a clean one and therefore made every falsification "succeed".** At `8234aba`, fast profile, exit 0: **73/73 Foundry · 405/405 TypeScript · 160 verifier (floor 160, verdict clean) · 7 samples · 77 tamper cases over 29 modes · 50 fixtures · eight guards.** `session-state.md` §3's headline agreed with all of it — the first time that line has been checked and found correct rather than stale. **COMPOSITION: THREE THIN LENSES AND FIVE DIRECTED ONES.** The thin briefs name no surfaces at all — break an instrument, catch a false statement, and one wholly free lens — because round two established that thin briefing did not cost yield and in fact raised it. The five directed targets were chosen from the SWEPT-VERSUS-UNSWEPT map rather than by feel: the D-010 verifier's six supporting modules have been swept (41 of 142 mutations survived a green gate) and its deciding file has been swept (14 survivors plus two live certification defects), while **the TypeScript conformance engine and decoders, the isolated signer, the vault and its containment claims, and the corpus/ablation apparatus have never had an independent directed sweep.** The only mutation sets covering them were written by the implementer, and this repository's own traces warn that such a set probes only the checks its author already thought about. Each directed reviewer is told its target was assigned and scores its findings as DIRECTED, since a null from an assigned target is stronger evidence than a null from an open lens while a positive is weaker as discovery and exactly as strong as a defect. **THE READING, DECLARED BEFORE RESULTS.** **(a) A finding that flips a verdict, or that gets something certified, signed or executed which must not be** — unambiguous, and it would also settle a question four rounds have left open: whether the construction-pinned/validation-unpinned asymmetry measured in the verifier is a property of that artifact or of this codebase. **(b) Findings only in documents and claims, with the instruments holding** — real, fixable, and the first of the two consecutive documentation-only rounds that fire D-047's own reversal condition. It would NOT fire it alone: round four found two live defects in shipped code, so this would be the first, not the second. **(c) Nothing at all — the round is CLEAN under D-047 and the loop ends.** Recorded now because it is the outcome I have the strongest incentive to misread: **the correct response to a null is not to report vindication but to check that the round was REAL** — that all eight lenses ran, that each ran the baseline before its probes, and that no coverage statement is a budget exhaustion dressed as a null. Four rounds is a base rate of four for four on breaking the previous round's fixes, twice against an author who had falsified its own work first. **AND THE CONSTRAINT THAT CUTS THE OTHER WAY, from D-048(b): if this round returns severe INSTRUMENT defects at round one's rate, the loop is NOT converging, and I must say so and hand the decision back to John rather than grind on under the letter of the ruling.** **What this round does not authorise:** nothing here starts pre-publication (D-048 makes a clean round a precondition, never a trigger — the programme still needs John's separate authorisation), no gate is signed, no public claim is certified, and the three declined items of D-043/D-044 stay declined. **ENVIRONMENT, flagged not fixed:** ten `anvil` processes from earlier sessions are running, the oldest from 2026-08-16, on ports 8858–9175. This is the same leak A-056 found in the signer and killed; these are not mine to kill mid-round and are recorded for John. `.sentinel/` holds no stale sockets.

- **A-058 (2026-08-17) — ROUND FIVE'S RESULT: NOT CLEAN. 51 findings, TWO CRITICAL, and the same repair defect three times over. D-048(b)'s reversal condition has fired and the decision goes back to John.** **THE VERDICT FIRST, because D-047 turns on exactly one question.** Eight lenses at frozen `8234aba` returned **51 findings — 2 CRITICAL, 11 HIGH, 22 MEDIUM, 13 LOW, 3 INFO**; 35 would change code, 13 a document, 1 a comment, 2 nothing. **Under D-047 this round is NOT CLEAN and the loop continues.** Under D-048 pre-publication remains blocked and this round does not move it. **I RE-VERIFIED SIXTEEN FINDINGS MYSELF — from scratch, with my own keys and my own controls against the unmutated tree, not by re-running a reviewer's script** — and the rest are recorded as leads in `docs/v1-1-register.md` §13 with their reviewer's confidence, not promoted. **THE TWO CRITICALS ARE BOTH IN THE D-010 VERIFIER, BOTH LIVE — no mutation, shipped behaviour — AND BOTH ARE A-055's OWN REPAIRS WITH THEIR ARGUMENT LEFT UNGENERALISED.** **H-1: THE PRESENTER STILL CHOOSES THE TRUST ROOT; A-055 CLOSED THE DEMONSTRATION AND NOT THE ARGUMENT.** `_find_domain` distinguishes two PATHS — bundle directory versus parent — and treats "parent" as "the deployment's". The parent directory is the presenter's too. A presenter who ships `tree/domain.json` alongside `tree/bundle/` supplies the trust root, and the verifier prints **`[PASS] the trust root is the deployment's, not the presenter's / deployment copy (parent directory)`** — a check whose text affirmatively states the invariant it is failing to enforce. **My reproduction:** a key I generated in this session, the receipt re-signed and `receipt.signer` rewritten INSIDE the signed body so the bundle is entirely self-consistent, `domain.json` in the parent naming my address — **`=> PASS`, exit 0, and `--all` over the presenter's tree prints `1/1 sample(s) verified`.** **Control, and it is what makes the finding rather than the claim:** put the REAL `domain.json` in that parent and the identical bundle fails on `recovered signer == domain.json signerAddress`. The only variable is a file the presenter hands over. This is byte-for-byte A-055's O-2 moved up one directory. **H-2: THE §5.5 OVERRIDE STAGE HAS NO DEPLOYMENT-ANCHORED OWNER IDENTITY AT ALL, and this one needs no directory trick — it works against the GENUINE trust root.** `ownerAddress` is a sibling declaration, not a field of the signed `OverrideAuthorizationPayload`, so anyone can sign the identical payload with their own key and declare themselves the owner. The stage's only party check is §3.3(7) — *recovered ≠ the Sentinel signer* — which any third party passes trivially. **My reproduction:** the real `fixtures/samples/domain.json` as trust root, the override payload byte-identical to the owner's, only `ownerAddress` and `ownerSignature` replaced with a key I made seconds earlier — **eleven consecutive `[PASS]` lines including `override owner is NOT the Sentinel signer (§3.3(7))`, then `=> PASS`, exit 0.** **THE CHAIN IS NOT FOOLED and that distinction belongs in the finding:** `SentinelVault.sol:251` does `if (digest.recover(ownerSig) != owner) revert NotOwnerOverride()` against its immutable owner, so this is **the independent verifier certifying an artifact the vault would reject** — which is precisely the failure D-048 names as its reason for sequencing the loop before publication. **The fix route exists and is cheap:** `mandate.principal` IS the owner address (verified: it equals the genuine `override.ownerAddress` on the sample), it is inside the signed mandate, and the mandate is bound into the receipt by `mandateHash` — and **`grep -n principal verifier/verify.py` returns nothing.** **THE PATTERN IS THE FINDING, and it is sharper than any single item: THREE of this round's most severe results are the SAME DEFECT — a repair that generalised its DEMONSTRATION instead of its ARGUMENT — and all three are themselves repairs of findings in that same class.** H-1 is A-055's trust-root fix. **H-3** is A-055's `signerFindings` fix, which resolved the two arrays by PRECEDENCE rather than requiring both, so adding `"signerFindings": []` inside the envelope shadows the top-level array the shipped corpus actually uses and the subset invariant passes vacuously — the presenter again choosing which array is checked. **A-1/C-1 is A-052's secret guard, holed for the THIRD time:** the placeholder markers were anchored to a value, but the suppressor still runs against the WHOLE LINE, and a line can carry more than one assignment. **Reproduced by two independent lenses and again by me with a control: a real 64-hex key on a plain line is BLOCKED; the identical key with an ordinary sibling field (`apiKey: "YOUR_API_KEY"`) or an ordinary trailing comment (`// see: EXAMPLE bundle`) passes `secret guard: clean`, rc 0.** The pre-commit hook shares the code path. **`v1-1-register.md` §8.2 currently records this as FIXED; that entry is corrected in place, at the top of the section, because a register that marks a live credential hole closed is worse than one that never listed it.** **FIVE INSTRUMENT DEFECTS ON SURFACES NO SWEEP HAD EVER COVERED — which is what commissioning the wider round bought.** **F-VAULT-2:** re-pointing ONE line — the registration of the future-nonce handler arm — leaves Foundry **73/73 green**, silently restoring the exact blind spot D-042 was written to close; A-040 already named this failure mode as "the project's most-repeated defect appearing inside the fix for a finding about the same defect", and nothing defends against it, while the mutation that would witness it exists nowhere in `scripts/mutate.sh`. **G-1: the labels of record are pinned by NOTHING** — `grep -rn 'labeller-E\|corpus/labels' scripts/*.sh` returns zero; the labelling PROMPT is hash-frozen and the MEASUREMENTS TAKEN UNDER IT are not, though every §7.3 figure is computed from them. **D-03: no profile of the gate compares the corpus's engine verdicts** (`grep -c results scripts/test.sh` → 0), and the deep stage's comparison arm **cannot** detect a verdict flip because the labeller views are verdict-free BY CONSTRUCTION — `assertNoLeakage` forbids `verdict`, `checks`, `reasonCodes`, `failing`, `outcome`, `label`, `expected`, `layers` by substring. **D-01/D-02: the window checks' LOWER bounds are exercised by nothing** — every `validAfter` in the entire TypeScript suite is `0n`. **AND THE GATE PRINTS FALSE FIGURES ON EVERY GREEN RUN, found independently by five of the eight lenses.** The COVERAGE BOUNDARY block publishes "7/7 samples, 62/62 applicable tamper cases across 24 distinct modes, 149/149 tests" and calls them **"ALL THREE FIGURES ARE FLOORS THIS RUN ASSERTS"**, while the same run's floors are `160 / 7 / 77 / 29` and its own stage line prints them 142 lines earlier; it states "No mutation sweep has been run over `verify.py` itself, which is 1681 lines" when A-055 swept it and the file is **1830** lines; `session-state.md` repeats both. **G-2: `docs/ablation-report.md` is not the output of its own generator on the committed inputs** — the verdict columns regenerate exactly, but L3's published p95 of **166 µs is arithmetically unreachable when the maximum L3 `micros` across all 50 committed results is 161.167** (L1 30 vs 29, L2 178 vs 174). A published S2 artifact carrying a column from a run that is not the committed one. **MY OWN INSTRUMENT WAS DEFECTIVE TWICE, recorded because this project's rule is that the brief and the harness are instruments too.** All eight lenses were given ONE scratchpad directory; four independently chose `scratchpad/baseline.log` and clobbered each other, and a reviewer read a log holding another lens's run before catching it (`D-11`). No finding is known to rest on a clobbered baseline — each lens used its own exit status — **but that is the correct reading only because a reviewer noticed.** And the deep profile was UNREACHABLE from every worktree (`D-12`): the corpus runner's unix socket path exceeds macOS's 104-byte `sun_path` limit from those paths, so all eight baselines were the FAST profile and anything visible only under `--gate` was out of reach for the entire round. **D-048(b) HAS FIRED AND I AM HANDING THE DECISION BACK RATHER THAN CONTINUING ON THE LETTER OF THE RULING.** Its condition is severe INSTRUMENT defects at round one's rate; round one produced seven confirmed guard defeats, and this round produced at least five of comparable severity plus two live CRITICALs. **THE CONFOUND, which John needs because it changes what the number means.** Rounds two through four NARROWED — round four was a single file — and the yield fell, which read as convergence. Round five WIDENED and the yield returned, with the same defect CLASSES appearing on four surfaces that had never been swept. **"The loop is not converging" and "the loop had been converging because it was looking at one file" are different diagnoses with opposite implications**, and I do not think an agent should pick between them here. My reading, offered as a recommendation and not a ruling: the technique is still finding real defects at a high rate, but what it is now measuring is BREADTH OF NEVER-SWEPT SURFACE rather than depth of remaining defect, and that is a different and more finite quantity. **WHAT I DID NOT DO:** no code is fixed, no claim is rewritten, and nothing is scoped. The only edits in this session's commits are this record, A-057, the register's §13, and the §8.2 correction — all of them the round's own record, which D-047 permits. **Remediation scoping is John's, and D-047 makes declining to fix a finding a change to a claim, so the scoping decision cannot be pre-empted by an agent choosing to leave something alone.**

- **D-049 (2026-08-17) — JOHN'S TWO RULINGS ON ROUND FIVE, taken at the fork A-058 handed back.** **(a) THE LOOP CONTINUES, AND "FULL BREADTH" IS TO BE DEFINED.** Presented with D-048(b)'s fired condition and the breadth confound, John ruled **continue — and rule on what a full-breadth round IS, as a named surface list.** The reasoning he was given and accepted: round five was wider than any round before it, so D-047's cleanliness test currently has no fixed denominator and **any round can be defeated by widening the next one**, which makes "clean" a judgement rather than a check — the exact property D-047's anti-gaming clause exists to prevent. **The definition itself is still HIS to ratify; an agent may not adopt one, because that is reinterpreting D-047.** A draft is at A-060, unratified. **(b) REMEDIATION IS SCOPED TO THE THREE LIVE SECURITY DEFECTS**, plus the false FIXED record in register §8.2. The other 48 findings are recorded in register §13 and are NOT to be worked. **Rejected, with his reasons available in the fork as put:** fixing all sixteen re-verified findings (several touch published S2 figures and would pull the re-label trigger's neighbourhood into an unscoped session), and holding everything for a facilitated walkthrough (two live CRITICALs and a credential-guard hole left open for no gain, since none of the three needs a ruling).

- **A-059 (2026-08-17) — THE THREE LIVE DEFECTS ARE FIXED, AND THE FIRST DRAFT OF THE FIRST FIX SHIPPED THE VERY DEFECT IT WAS FIXING.** **THE NEAR-MISS IS THE ENTRY, because it is the whole lesson of round five happening inside round five's own remediation.** H-1 is "a repair generalised its DEMONSTRATION and not its ARGUMENT". My first draft of the H-1 repair made `--domain` the asserted trust root **and then invented a second source**: it treated the directory named under `--all` as the caller's assertion. That reads as obviously right — the caller did name it — and it is wrong for one reason: **when a presenter hands you a tree, the directory you name IS the presenter's.** The single-bundle exploit went from `=> PASS` to `=> FAIL` and the `--all` form of the identical hostile tree still printed **`1/1 sample(s) verified`, exit 0.** I caught it only because I re-ran the exploit through BOTH invocation shapes rather than the one that produced it — which is the discipline A-028 and A-043 cost this project a CRITICAL to learn. **The corrected rule has no second source at all: `--domain` is the only assertion, and `scripts/test.sh` now passes it.** A comment at that site says so and says why, because the next author will have the same good idea. **H-1, FIXED.** `_find_domain` no longer distinguishes bundle-copy from parent-copy — that distinction was a PATH masquerading as a PROVENANCE. Anything discovered anywhere in the presented material is presenter-supplied and cannot certify; the check that used to read *"the trust root is the deployment's, not the presenter's"* — a sentence that asserted the invariant it was failing to enforce — now reads **"the trust root was ASSERTED by the verifying party, not found in the material"**, which is a claim the code can actually support. A discovered root is still LOADED so the operator gets real diagnostics instead of one bare error; it simply cannot carry a PASS. **Falsified three ways:** the exploit fails in both invocation shapes; **neutering the new check re-opens it (exit 0) and fails 4 tests**, so the check is asserted by something rather than merely present; and the real samples still certify, so this is not a guard that cries wolf. **H-2, FIXED, and the fix was available in the artifact all along.** The override stage had no deployment-anchored owner identity of any kind: `ownerAddress` is a sibling declaration, not a member of the signed §5.5 payload, so any key signs the identical payload and names itself. §5.1's `MandatePayload` carries `principal`, the mandate is bound into the signed receipt by `mandateHash`, and this stage already required the override to match it — so the chain runs asserted root → receipt signature → mandateHash → `mandate.principal` → this override. **`grep principal verifier/verify.py` returned NOTHING before this repair: the 1830-line file that decides the verdict never read the one field naming the party it was certifying.** **The new tamper mode is `override-outsider-mints`, and it exists because neither party mode could ever witness this** — `override-wrongkey` and `override-signer-mints` both mint as the SENTINEL SIGNER, so §3.3(7) catches them and the question "is this the owner?" is never asked. **Falsified to A-056's standard, which is the only one that means anything: with the new check neutered, the new mode is WRONGLY ACCEPTED** — so the mode witnesses that check and not a different one failing alongside — **and three tests fail.** The paired positive is asserted too, so the check cannot be satisfied by rejecting every override. **A CORRECTION TO A-058's SEVERITY FRAMING, made here rather than left standing:** the vault is NOT fooled by either CRITICAL. `SentinelVault.execute` recovers the owner signature against its own immutable `owner`. What was defective is precisely the INDEPENDENT VERIFIER's certification — which is the artifact D-010 exists to be, and which D-048 names as its reason for sequencing this loop before publication. **THE SECRET GUARD, FIXED FOR THE THIRD TIME, AND THIS TIME AT THE ARGUMENT.** A-052 anchored the placeholder markers to a value; necessary, not sufficient. Every filter in that pipeline is a SUPPRESSOR and each ran with `grep -v` against the WHOLE LINE — and a line can hold more than one assignment. **The fix is one letter, `grep -nE` → `grep -onE`:** with `-o` each record is `LINENO:MATCH`, so a suppressor can only ever discard the occurrence it actually matched. **Falsified with a twelve-row probe run against the PRE-FIX code first, as A-044 requires: five of six MUST-BLOCK rows failed before the change and all twelve pass after**, with the six MUST-STAY-CLEAN rows green throughout so the guard has not been turned into one that cries wolf. **It also closes register §8.2's second item as a side effect** — one real key beside one allowlisted Anvil key was the same defect wearing a different filter, and fixing the argument rather than the demonstration necessarily takes both. That is stated so nobody re-derives it as a new finding. **A RESIDUAL I AM RECORDING RATHER THAN CLOSING: that twelve-row probe lives in a scratchpad and is not re-runnable from the repository.** A guard whose falsification is not re-runnable rots, and this one has now rotted twice. Building it into the tree is new tooling and is outside D-049(b)'s scope; it is register §13.2. **SUITE MOVEMENT, ratcheted in the SAME edit under this project's own rule that a count is updated by the edit that changes it: verifier 160 → 170 tests, tamper 77 → 78 cases over 29 → 30 modes**, and `scripts/test.sh`'s three floors moved with them. Fast profile and deep profile both green; the deep profile re-executed the 50-fixture corpus with its committed views verified file by file. **ONE PARTIAL CORRECTION, DECLARED AS PARTIAL.** The gate's COVERAGE BOUNDARY block is a recorded round-five finding (B-1/B-2/B-3/C-2) and is NOT in scope. But this session moved the three D-010 figures it misstates, so under the same "update it in the edit that changes it" rule those three are corrected — **and the block now says in terms that its other false statements are still there and unfixed**, naming them, because a half-corrected block that looks whole is this project's own documented failure mode. **WHAT IS NOT FIXED — and this sentence was WRONG when first written, corrected the same day before the walkthrough that would have been run off it.** It said "the other 48 findings … including five HIGHs I re-verified myself" and then listed five, one of which is a MEDIUM. **Counted from the round's own data rather than from memory: 51 findings, 4 finding ids fixed here (H-1, H-2, and A-1/C-1, which are one defect reported twice) — so 47 REMAIN OPEN, of which 12 are ones I re-verified myself: EIGHT HIGH — `C-2` the gate printing false verifier figures, `D-01` the mandate window's unexercised lower bound, `D-03` no profile comparing corpus verdicts, `E1` the signer's prototype-chain verdict check, `F-VAULT-1` the per-action cap and the containment claim, `F-VAULT-2` the invariant arm one line de-registers, `G-1` the labels of record pinned by nothing, `H-3` the signerFindings precedence hole — and FOUR MEDIUM (`A-2`, `B-1`, `B-2` on the coverage boundary, `G-2` the ablation report's unreachable latency column). The remaining 35 are reviewer leads I have not confirmed.** The original error is left visible rather than silently overwritten because "a published number can be true once" is a round-five finding against this project and I had just written three of them. None of the 47 is closed, and D-047 makes leaving them a change to a claim, which is why they are recorded rather than quietly dropped.

- **A-060 (2026-08-17, DRAFT — NOT RATIFIED, awaiting John under D-049(a)) — A PROPOSED DEFINITION OF "FULL BREADTH", so D-047's cleanliness test has a fixed denominator.** **This is a proposal. It is not in force, and no agent may treat it as if it were.** **THE PROBLEM IT ANSWERS:** D-047 says a round's scope may not shrink and that only a round of at least the previous round's breadth can close the loop. Round five was eight lenses over the whole tree, so "at least round five's breadth" is now the bar and nobody has said what it is. Without a list, "clean" is settled by whoever writes the next brief — and both of the last two rounds had a defective brief. **PROPOSED: a round is FULL BREADTH when every surface below is covered by at least one reviewer that ran its own baseline first, and each returns a coverage statement.** (1) The mechanical guards and the gate script, `scripts/**`. (2) The isolated signer, `ts/src/signer/**`, with the Solidity type mirror. (3) The conformance engine and decoders, `ts/src/evaluate/**` and `ts/src/decode/**`. (4) The vault and demo contracts, `contracts/src/**`, with the invariant campaign. (5) The corpus, labels and ablation apparatus, `ts/src/corpus/**`, `ts/src/ablation/**`, `fixtures/**`. (6) The D-010 verifier, `verifier/**`. (7) **The simulation and effect pipeline, `ts/src/simulate/**`, `ts/src/propose/**`, `ts/src/tools/**` — WHICH ROUND FIVE DID NOT ASSIGN TO ANYONE.** Its 1,400-odd lines were reachable only by the three free lenses, and one of them found a surviving mutation there (`C-3`, the whole internal-call trace walk deletable with the suite green). **A definition of breadth that round five itself would fail is the right definition, and this is the item that shows the list is doing work rather than describing what I happened to do.** (8) The claims: every document, code comment and printed line of output. (9) At least one wholly free lens with no assigned surface, because the list can only name what somebody has already thought of. **PLUS TWO CONDITIONS FROM ROUND FIVE'S OWN INSTRUMENT DEFECTS:** at least one reviewer must be able to run the DEEP profile — all eight of round five's were confined to the fast one by a socket-path limit and never knew it until one measured it — and each reviewer must have its own evidence directory, because four of eight independently chose the same baseline filename and clobbered each other. **COST, stated because it is the argument against: this is a nine-reviewer floor per round, and D-047 is unbounded in principle.** **THE COUNTER-ARGUMENT: a fixed list is also a ceiling, and this project's defects have repeatedly been in places nobody listed.** Item (9) is the mitigation and it is a weak one. **A definition that cannot be met is worse than none**, so if the answer is that nine reviewers per round is more than a portfolio lab warrants, the honest move is to say so and change D-047's terminating condition rather than to keep a bar nobody will clear.

- **D-050 (2026-08-17) — SIX RULINGS AT A FACILITATED WALKTHROUGH, taken one item at a time. John's session; the agent presented facts, options, costs and a recommendation for each and made none of the calls.** **(1) A-060 IS RATIFIED AS DRAFTED and is now what "full breadth" MEANS under D-047** — the nine surfaces (guards and gate; signer; evaluator and decoders; vault; corpus, labels and ablation; the D-010 verifier; the simulation and effect pipeline; the claims; and one wholly free lens), plus its two conditions: at least one reviewer must be able to run the DEEP profile, and each reviewer gets its own evidence directory. **A round is full breadth when every surface is covered by a reviewer that ran its own baseline and returned a coverage statement.** *Counter-argument recorded because John heard it: this sets a nine-reviewer floor per round while D-047 is unbounded in principle, and a fixed list is also a CEILING in a project whose defects have repeatedly been where nobody looked — item (9) is the mitigation and it is a weak one.* **Reversal condition, from A-060 itself: a definition that cannot be met is worse than none. If nine reviewers a round proves to be more than this artifact warrants, that comes back to John as a change to D-047's terminating condition — it is not an agent's to quietly under-run.** Note that **round five would FAIL this definition**: nobody was assigned `ts/src/simulate|propose|tools`, and the free lens that wandered there found a surviving mutation (`C-3`). **(2) REMEDIATION IS CLUSTER C ONLY** — the two live code defects, `E1` and `H-3` (built as A-061). **Declined, each with what it is:** cluster A, finishing the gate's false COVERAGE BOUNDARY statements; cluster B, the four things the evidence apparatus claims but does not assert — the labels of record pinned by nothing, no profile comparing corpus verdicts, the window checks' unexercised lower bounds, the invariant arm one line de-registers; and cluster D, the §7.1 containment claim that is still false after D-042 corrected it and the ablation report that is not the output of its own generator. **D-047 makes declining a change to a claim, so all of it stays recorded in register §13 and this round remains not-clean on any reading.** Cluster D was additionally the one an agent could not have settled alone: §7.1 states what the product guarantees, and certification of public claims is autonomy NONE. **(3) THE 32 UNCONFIRMED LEADS FOLD INTO ROUND SIX** rather than being adjudicated now — its nine surfaces cover the same ground, and an independent reviewer re-finding a lead is better evidence than the author confirming it. The brief carries forward round five's rule that re-reporting a recorded item is not a new finding. *The argument against, recorded: 22 of them would change code, so every real one guarantees round six cannot come back clean.* **(4) ROUND FIVE'S RAW REPORTS ARE COMMITTED** as `docs/review-2026-08-17/` — eight lens reports with reproductions, brief critiques, coverage statements and provenance attestations, plus a README. **The probe artifacts are NOT committed:** they carry key-shaped constants, and a secret-guard exemption to accommodate them is the exact shape of hole §8.2 keeps recording. **(5) One push at the end of the session** to the private remote — backup, not publication, and D-016 is untouched. **(6) All thirteen leaked processes killed** — twelve orphaned `anvil`s and one key-bearing signer.

- **A-061 (2026-08-17) — CLUSTER C BUILT: the signer's prototype-chain verdict check and the verifier's array-precedence hole, both fixed at the ARGUMENT and both falsified against the pre-fix code.** **`E1` — `verdict in VERDICT` WALKED THE PROTOTYPE CHAIN.** Every member of `Object.prototype` — `__proto__`, `toString`, `constructor`, `hasOwnProperty`, `valueOf` — passed the RPC boundary as a legal verdict name. **The consequence was not cosmetic:** on the refusal path the signer produced a genuinely SIGNED §5.5.1 RefusalRecord whose `requestedVerdict` sits outside the published schema, and **the ratified D-010 verifier enforces that schema**, so an unauthenticated caller could pick a value that makes the signer's own proof-of-refusal unverifiable — which is precisely what D-012's record exists to prevent. On the attest path the same value reached `BigInt(verdictNumber(v))` and threw, returning SIGNER_ERROR from inside the signing routine rather than BAD_REQUEST at the boundary. **Fixed with `Object.hasOwn`. The ARGUMENT was checked before the fix, not after: a grep for this pattern across `ts/src` returns exactly one site**, so unlike H-1's near-miss there was no sibling branch to leave open. **The test asserts over the WHOLE of `Object.prototype` — every own property name, whatever the runtime supplies — rather than over the three spellings the reviewer happened to try**, because an enumerated bad list pins those spellings and nothing else (A-054's lesson, applied without being asked). **Falsified: it fails against the pre-fix code and passes after, and a paired positive requires the three real verdicts to still be accepted**, so the test cannot be satisfied by rejecting everything. **`H-3` — A-055's TWO-ARRAY REPAIR RESOLVED BY PRECEDENCE, AND PRECEDENCE IS THE SAME DEFECT ONE LAYER IN.** A-055 fixed `signerFindings` resolving to `None` on the repository's one refusal artifact; its repair consulted the envelope FIRST and the top level only when the envelope key was ABSENT. **Absent is not the same as trustworthy.** Adding `"signerFindings": []` inside the envelope shadows the top-level array — the one the shipped corpus actually uses, and the one the repair was written for — so the subset invariant is computed over an empty set and passes vacuously. **I reproduced it myself before fixing it: an uncommitted reason code in the top-level array verified `=> PASS`, exit 0, against the unmutated verifier.** **Fixed at the argument rather than the demonstration: a presenter must not choose which of two co-located arrays is invariant-checked, so BOTH are read and a disagreement between them is a FAILURE** — publishing two different answers to the same question is not a certifiable claim, which is the same reasoning that rejects a bundle whose `domain.json` contradicts the asserted trust root. **The identical hole applied to `reasonCodes`** — the list a reader sees could differ entirely from the list that was hashed — **and it is closed in the same change and has its own test, because fixing only the array the reviewer exploited is this project's most-repeated defect.** **Falsified: both new tests fail against the restored pre-fix behaviour and pass after; a third, the unmodified corpus refusal, passes in both and is the control.** **SUITE: verifier 170 → 173 tests, and `VERIFIER_MIN_TESTS` plus the COVERAGE BOUNDARY figure moved in the SAME edit.** Fast and deep profiles both green. **A LIVE DEMONSTRATION OF THE SECRET-GUARD REPAIR, worth recording because it is the first real content the fixed guard saw:** committing round five's eight reviewer reports was BLOCKED by `check-secrets.sh` — all eight files, house rule 6's machine-specific-absolute-path arm. That is the guard behaving correctly on material I would otherwise have committed without looking. The paths were rewritten to `<scratchpad>`, `<repo>` and `$HOME` rather than an exemption being added, and **the README says the files are therefore not byte-identical returns**, because provenance is the whole reason those files are in the tree. **No credential-shaped content was flagged in any report.**

- **A-063 (2026-08-17, overnight, under John's standing authorisation to decide and execute) — F-VAULT-1: D-042's CORRECTION WAS ITSELF FALSE, IN THE ONE DIMENSION IT HAD JUST NARROWED TO. Four sites repaired, a limit test added, and the claim left UNCERTIFIED because an agent may not certify a public claim.** **THE FINDING.** §7.1's row "evaluator or signer compromise scenario within the vault's hard caps" was corrected on 2026-08-16 (D-042) to say the caps "bound the NATIVE-VALUE dimension only". **They do not bound it either.** `maxNativeValueWei` is compared PER ACTION (`SentinelVault.sol`, inside the view check), and **no cumulative, rate-limited or velocity bound exists anywhere in `contracts/src`** — verified by grep, not assumed. Under the exact threat model the row names, a compromised signer mints one receipt per nonce at exactly the ceiling and the vault's whole native balance leaves. **So a correction written to repair an overstatement of containment overstated containment itself. That is the second time on this one row**, which is why the repair this time is a TEST rather than a third sentence. **MEASURED, and the control is the half that matters:** a probe vault capped at 0.01 ether and funded with 1 ether is drained to zero by 100 sequential valid ALLOW receipts, each at exactly the cap, relayed by `address(0xDEAD)` — `execute` is permissionless, so the relayer needs no key — while the paired control confirms that ONE action at cap+1 still reverts with `ValueOverCap`. Without that control the drain would be equally consistent with the ceiling being absent, and the test would assert nothing. **THE OTHER THREE ITEMS D-042 LISTED AS ONCHAIN CONTAINMENT ARE NO BETTER, and listing them is the error underneath both corrections:** `actionNonce` prevents REPLAY but not a fresh receipt at N+1; `deadline` lives in the ActionPayload the receipt commits to and `expiresAt` in the receipt itself, so under signer compromise **both are the attacker's own fields** — the test now sets `deadline: type(uint64).max` deliberately rather than for convenience, and the vault accepts it, because the vault rejects only values already in the past. **Of the four, only PAUSE bounds a compromised signer, and pause is an owner REACTION rather than a cap:** it bounds damage after somebody notices, which is a different and weaker guarantee. **THE TEST IS FALSIFIED THE ONLY WAY A LIMIT TEST CAN BE — by giving the vault the bound it lacks.** A cumulative ceiling at 10× the per-action cap was added to the execution path and `test_LIMIT_nativeCeilingIsPerActionAndBoundsNoAggregate` went RED; removed, it is green. **THE FIRST ATTEMPT AT THAT FALSIFICATION DID NOT COMPILE AND I NEARLY RECORDED IT AS A PASS** — the per-action cap check lives in a `view` function, so the probe's state write was rejected by solc, and the run produced no PASS/FAIL line at all. **Caught by reading the output rather than the grep result.** That is the second dead probe of this session; the other was `evidence-hash`'s (see A-062). **FOUR SITES, because the previous correction landed on one and this project's most-repeated defect is a repair that reaches the demonstration and not the argument:** the proposal's §7.1 row; `scripts/test.sh`'s COVERAGE BOUNDARY, whose "WHAT IS NOT COVERED" list carried the same false sentence; `docs/gate-s2-evidence.md`, corrected in place with the error stated BEFORE its content (A-048's rule, after a correction in this same pack once misdescribed what it was correcting); and the backstops suite, which now asserts both limits side by side. **A grep for the claim's other spellings found no fifth site.** **NOT CERTIFIED, AND THIS IS NOT A FORMALITY.** §7.1 states what the product guarantees. The HANDOFF verification partition puts public claims at autonomy NONE and John certifies them; **his authorisation to decide and execute overnight does not move a standing rule that exists precisely for the case where an agent is working unsupervised.** The corrected text is marked in the proposal as an agent-drafted repair of a demonstrated falsehood awaiting certification. **What John is being asked to certify is a correction of something FALSE to something MEASURED — not a new claim** — and the measurement, the control and the falsification are all in the tree.

- **A-064 (2026-08-17, overnight) — CLUSTER B: THE FOUR THINGS THE EVIDENCE APPARATUS CLAIMED AND DID NOT ASSERT. All four closed, each falsified by making it fail first.** **This is the cluster John declined at the walkthrough and then authorised overnight when the argument changed: the open findings do not merely sit as debt, they make the NEXT round's result uninterpretable, because a round that returns known items cannot distinguish "the artifact is sound" from "the reviewers found our own backlog".** **`G-1` — THE LABELS OF RECORD WERE PINNED BY NOTHING, AND THEY ARE THE GROUND TRUTH.** `check-label-prompt.sh` hash-freezes the labelling PROMPT under D-011(a); nothing touched the labels produced under it. `grep -rn 'labeller-E\|corpus/labels' scripts/*.sh` returned zero. Round five's corpus lens changed one word — F035 from BLOCK to ALLOW — regenerated the ablation report, and the §7.3 figures moved to a perfect score while the gate printed PASS with byte-identical stage lines. **D-003's kill criterion 3 names ground-truth labels as evaluator-tamper territory and halts on it; the instructions were guarded and the measurements taken under them were not.** **Closed by `scripts/check-label-integrity.sh` (the gate's NINTH guard) and `fixtures/corpus/labels/LABELS.sha256`, pinning ALL TWENTY labelling artifacts — not the two the finding named.** Every other arm is audit trail — the contamination controls, the model-diversity arm, the re-designated duplicate — and an audit trail that can be edited silently is not one. **Three failure modes, because a manifest that only re-hashes what it lists is defeated by ADDING a file** (A-054's lesson: pin by complement, not by enumeration): content drift, a pinned file missing, and **an unpinned label file existing at all**. All three falsified and all three fire; the clean tree passes. **`D-03` — NO PROFILE COMPARED THE ENGINE'S VERDICTS, AND THE ARM THAT LOOKED LIKE IT DID CANNOT.** The deep stage re-executes the corpus and compares the labeller VIEWS — which are verdict-free BY CONSTRUCTION, since `assertNoLeakage` forbids `verdict`, `checks`, `reasonCodes`, `failing`, `outcome`, `label`, `expected` and `layers` by substring. `$CORPUS_TMP/results/` was written and deleted unread. So the engine's verdicts on all 50 fixtures were pinned by nothing, and the register's framing of this as a laundering route through committed `results/` UNDERSTATED it: an edit to `ts/src/evaluate/**` — no artifact touched — was equally invisible. **Closed by comparing the results directory too.** **Which fields vary was MEASURED, not assumed: the corpus was run twice and every key walked. Exactly two move** — `layers[].micros`, and `layers[].checks[].detail` only where a message embeds the wall clock. So `micros` is dropped and `detail` is normalised on that one pattern, rather than discarding a whole field to make a comparison pass — which is how `expiryBefore`/`expiryAfter` escaped every layer in 36 of 50 views (A-048). The check also fails if the FILE SETS differ before any content is compared. **THE FALSIFICATION IS THE ENTRY: removing the mandate window's lower bound from the evaluator now prints `2 result file(s) MOVED: F033.json, _index.json` and GATE FAILED — while the line immediately above it still reads `committed views verified FILE BY FILE`.** The old arm passing beside the new one failing is the finding demonstrated in the gate's own output. **`D-01` and `D-02` — BOTH WINDOW CHECKS COMPARE TWO BOUNDS AND ONLY THE UPPER ONE WAS EVER PERTURBED.** Every `validAfter` in the entire TypeScript suite was `0n`, so `now >= mandate.validAfter` and `now >= policy.validAfter` could each be deleted with 405/405 green. `D-02` was round five's single unconfirmed HIGH and is confirmed here. **The check table now carries a row per BOUND rather than per code**, with a `note` field distinguishing them; falsified by deleting both lower bounds, which reddens exactly the two new rows. **`F-VAULT-2` — THE INVARIANT ARM D-042 ADDED CAN BE DE-REGISTERED BY ONE LINE, AND THE COMMENT ABOVE IT ALREADY SAID SO.** That block warns in terms that "REGISTERING A HANDLER ACTION IS A SEPARATE STEP FROM WRITING ONE, and forgetting it is silent" — and nothing enforced it, which is the A-028 shape: a comment that describes a hazard and files it as an inconvenience. Re-pointing `sel[12]` left Foundry at 73/73 and restored the exact pre-D-042 blind spot. **Closed by asserting the STRUCTURE that produces the behaviour** — `targetSelectors()` is forge-std's own record of what was registered, not our copy of it — with a length assertion first, because a DUPLICATE registration would otherwise satisfy a membership loop while an action went missing, which is precisely how the defect presented. **Falsified with round five's exact probe: re-pointing `sel[12]` now fails the suite.** **SUITE: Foundry 74 → 75, TypeScript 407 → 409. Nine guards now, not eight; the session-state line saying "eight" moved in the same edit.**

- **A-066 (2026-08-17, overnight) — THE DEEP PROFILE CAN NOW BE RUN FROM A REVIEW WORKTREE, which D-050(1) requires and which nothing satisfied. Found while drafting round six's brief, not by a reviewer.** **WHY IT IS MORE THAN AN INCONVENIENCE.** A unix socket path must fit macOS's 104-byte `sun_path`, and `REPO/.sentinel/corpus-N.sock` is 129 bytes from the worktree paths this project actually uses — so `npm run corpus`, and therefore `./scripts/test.sh --gate`, aborted with `connect EINVAL` before evaluating a single fixture. **All eight of round five's reviewers were therefore confined to the FAST profile, none of them knew it, and one measured it only after its findings were written** (D-12). D-050(1) then ratified A-060, whose second condition is that at least one reviewer per round must be able to run the deep profile — **so as of last night, round six could not have satisfied its own definition of breadth.** The condition was ratified and unmeetable at the same time, which is the shape of thing this project keeps finding: a rule with no mechanism behind it. **THE FIX triggers on LENGTH, not on being-in-a-worktree**, because the byte count is the only thing that breaks;
