the live tree's path is 60 bytes and takes the unchanged branch, so default behaviour and every committed artifact are untouched. `SENTINEL_CORPUS_SOCKET_DIR` overrides it explicitly. **THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS FIX WAS WRONG IN A WAY WORTH RECORDING, because it would have silently weakened a security property that an earlier adversarial review put there.** It placed the socket directly in `tmpdir()` and died with `EPERM: chmod '/var/folders/.../T'`. `startSignerServer` deliberately chmods the socket's PARENT directory to 0700 — unix-socket permissions are checked at connect time, so a 0700 parent closes that window however the socket is created — and pointing the signer at a shared system directory asks it to lock down a directory it does not own. **Had that call been permitted rather than refused, the fix would have made the corpus signer's socket world-traversable.** `mkdtempSync` now gives it a fresh private directory, so the hardening applies to a directory that is ours. **FALSIFIED AT THE SAME LONG PATH, BOTH DIRECTIONS: the frozen `run.ts` fails there with `connect EINVAL`, exit 1; with the fix, exit 0 and 51 result files.** The live deep gate is green and its corpus stage still verifies the committed views file by file and the verdicts against the committed set. **THREE OF THIS SESSION'S PROBES WERE DEAD BEFORE THEY WERE RIGHT, and the count belongs in the record rather than the successes alone:** a mutation that raised a value already at the maximum so no percentile moved (A-062); a Solidity probe that did not compile and printed no PASS/FAIL line (A-063); and two corpus runs here that died on a missing build artifact long before reaching the socket code, either of which I would have misread as evidence about the socket. **Each was caught by the same question — did the probe MOVE anything — and not by intuition.**

- **A-067 (2026-08-17, overnight) — THE 24 LEADS ADJUDICATED BY FOUR INDEPENDENT AGENTS: 21 CONFIRMED, 1 already closed, 1 refuted, 1 unproven. Two fixed here; nineteen recorded with verdicts and revised severities.** **THE HEADLINE IS THE HIT RATE.** These were the findings round five's reviewers produced and I had NOT reproduced — the ones I recommended folding into round six on the argument that an independent re-finding would be better evidence than my confirmation. **Twenty-one of twenty-four hold under adversarial adjudication.** The adjudicators were told explicitly that ALREADY-CLOSED was as valuable a verdict as CONFIRMED and that they should assume each claim wrong until they had made its failure happen; they still confirmed 21. **The reviewers were accurate, and my instinct to defer them was the weaker call.** **ONE SEVERITY WAS RAISED, and it is the finding I would not have prioritised: `D-08`, MEDIUM → HIGH.** The decoder's strictness predicates are pinned by ONE witness each — the dirty-address test puts its bad byte at high-byte position 0, the non-canonical-bool test uses the value 2 — so `slice(0, 24)` could be narrowed to `slice(0, 2)` and `/^0*1$/` widened to `/^0*[13]$/` with **all 409 tests green**, while an address differing above byte 1 decoded clean and a bool word of 3 decoded as `true`. The adjudicator raised it because what is unguarded is the exact invariant the decoder's own chain-differential test declares must never break: never more permissive than the chain. **FIXED: the witnesses now cover the PREDICATE rather than a point of it** — all twelve high-byte positions, and a spread of non-canonical bool words — and both of the adjudicated mutations are now caught. **`H-4` FIXED, AND IT IS A KNOWN STRUCTURAL DEFECT RECURRING.** On the receipt path a payload-hash MISMATCH became a PASS when the contradicting file was DELETED: stage `case-1-allow` with `case-3`'s `action.json` and the run fails; `rm action.json` and the identical receipt verifies `=> PASS`, exit 0, `1/1 sample(s) verified`. Stripping all three payload files verifies too — a receipt whose committed hashes match nothing in front of the verifier, certified. **A-041 already found and NAMED this defect in the S2 pack — "SKIP counted as ok=True in the aggregate, so 'was not checked' summed as 'passed'" — fixed it for the refusal envelope, and left it standing on the receipt path.** Absence is not agreement: both branches now FAIL and say why. **The sibling `§3.3(4)` branch was closed in the same change rather than left for the next round**, because fixing the branch a reviewer exploited and leaving the identical one beside it is the defect that cost this project a CRITICAL (A-028 → A-043). **Three regression tests, falsified against the restored pre-fix behaviour: two fail, and the intact-bundle control passes in both directions so the suite cannot go green by rejecting everything.** **NINETEEN CONFIRMED FINDINGS ARE RECORDED AND NOT FIXED**, with the adjudicators' revised severities — several DOWN (`D-07`, `G-3` to LOW) and the evidence for each in `docs/review-2026-08-17/`. **They are not fixed because the sensible next move is round six, not another night of repairs by the same hands: nineteen fixes authored by me would be nineteen more things for an independent lens to find wrong, and the loop exists precisely because the build loop does not find its own defects.** **`C-5` REFUTED** — `npm test` does set a timeout, so a hung signer socket does not hang the gate indefinitely. **`A-3` UNPROVEN** — the log-truncation the reviewer saw could not be reproduced deliberately; it is an artifact of the harness rather than a property of the project, and it is recorded as unproven rather than dismissed, because I hit exactly that truncation myself during this session. **SUITE: verifier 173 → 176, TypeScript 409 → 426, Foundry 75. Floors ratcheted in the same edit. Deep gate green.**

- **D-051 (2026-08-18) — FOUR RULINGS AT A FACILITATED WALKTHROUGH, one item at a time, each with the agent's recommendation on the table and none of the calls made by it.** **(a) THE §7.1 CONTAINMENT CORRECTION IS CERTIFIED.** A-063's repair stands as drafted, and the "awaiting certification" markers are removed from the proposal, `scripts/test.sh`'s coverage boundary and `docs/gate-s2-evidence.md`. **What was certified is a repair from something FALSE to something MEASURED** — the drain of a capped vault to zero by 100 valid receipts, with the control confirming one action at cap+1 still reverts — **and not a new claim.** John was shown the blunt half before ruling: the row now says in terms that per-action authority is bounded and cumulative authority is not bounded at all, which is a strong admission for a portfolio artifact and is the thing this project exists to be honest about. **The distinguishing feature from D-042's failed correction is that this one is a sentence PLUS a test that fails if the limit is ever closed**, so the row cannot rot back into an assumption. Round six's claims lens and vault lens both audit it inside the loop regardless. **(b) THE NINE MEDIUM FINDINGS GET FIXED; THE NINE LOW AND ONE INFO BECOME DOCUMENTED LIMITS.** **This reverses the agent's own overnight recommendation and the reversal is the point.** A-067 argued for leaving all nineteen, on the reasoning that nineteen self-authored repairs would be nineteen more things for an independent lens to find wrong. That argument does not survive D-047: **a round that finds only things you decline to fix is not clean, and declining is itself a change to a claim** — which is the same argument that justified fixing clusters A and B the night before. **The distinction that made the ruling available:** leaving a finding open and ACCEPTING it as a documented limit are different acts. The first is nobody deciding; the second is John deciding what the product is, in the §11 "what is NOT in evidence" discipline this project already runs. Only the second gives round six a declared baseline. **(c) ROUND SIX RUNS AFTER THE FIXES, AND THE AGENT STOPS AT ADJUDICATION.** It verifies the findings and brings them back rather than acting on them, **because whether the loop closes is the judgment D-047 reserves and it should not be made by the hands that just spent two days repairing the thing under test.** **(d) THE THREE STALE WORKTREES GO, AND THEIR PROBES ARE KEPT.** Two held the working artifacts of the reviews that produced A-040 and A-043 — the project's only CRITICAL. Preserved under `docs/review-2026-08-16/` with a README stating that they are probes rather than evidence and that every finding they produced is already recorded, fixed and regression-tested. Consistent with D-050(4)'s ruling on round five's evidence; the alternative was that they vanished with a scratchpad.

- **A-068 (2026-08-18) — THE NINE MEDIUM FINDINGS, under D-051(b). Seven fixed and falsified; TWO ARE DESIGN FORKS AND ARE NOT AN AGENT'S TO CLOSE.** **THE FORKS FIRST, because they are what John needs and they were not visible when he ruled.** He ruled "fix the nine MEDIUMs". Doing the work showed that **`E3` and `E4` each change what the product GUARANTEES**, and both adjudicators said so independently — CLAUDE.md's test is "if the answer changes what the product is, ask John". **`E3`: the signer attests an ALLOW anchored to ANY historical block**, including one at which the vault had no code, and the vault executes it. The check establishes that the named block exists with the named hash, not that it is recent; anchor recency is bounded by nothing here or downstream. **The fork is whether the receipt should bind to the signer's OWN observation** — an `observedAtBlock`, or a required proximity between the caller's anchor and the signer's. **[CORRECTED 2026-08-18 (D-055). THIS ENTRY RE-OPENED A RULED ITEM AND IS THE ERROR IN THE PAIR, not A-044.** `A-044(f)` — *"THE SIMULATION ANCHOR IS RECORDED AS A DECLARED LIMIT, NOT FIXED"* — was **ruled by John on 2026-08-16**, under a header that states "Ruled by John one at a time", on the reasoning that a recency bound is a policy parameter nobody had reasoned about. Round five's `E3` is the SAME defect, not a new one. A-068 called it an "open design fork" that is "not an agent's to close" **without citing A-044(f) at all** — its one reference to A-044 is for `E2`. The finding did not change; the record did, and it drifted in the direction that creates work. **BOTH ARE NOW SUPERSEDED: D-055 rules the anchor SHALL BE FIXED**, by pinning signer reads to one block rather than by inventing a recency timeout.]** **`E4`: the §5.6 bundle's `normalizedAction` and `expectedEffects` are checked by NEITHER the signer nor the D-010 verifier.** The `evidenceHash` commits to them so they are tamper-evident, but nothing compares them to the action and mandate they purport to describe, so a bundle can state expected effects its own action does not imply and still verify. **The fork is whether the SIGNER should compare them — D-014 deliberately kept conformance out of the signer — while whether the VERIFIER should is cheaper and needs no ruling.** **WHAT WAS DONE ON BOTH WITHOUT RULING: the comments that implied the property held are corrected in place**, at `attest.ts` and `decode/index.ts`, because a comment describing a gap and filing it as satisfied is the A-028 shape this project has paid for twice. **THE SEVEN FIXED, each falsified against the pre-fix code.** **`D-04` — a REVIEW receipt could state NO reason.** `failingCodes` narrowed from `outcome !== "PASS"` to `=== "VIOLATION"` dropped every UNRESOLVED code from `reasonCodes` with all 426 tests green, and the `reasonCodesHash` then committed to the empty list. **The receipt is the product; a verdict whose stated reasons are empty is what §5.4's reason codes exist to prevent, and it was three characters away.** Fixed by asserting, for EVERY non-PASS row in the check table rather than for Case 4 alone, that the code reaches the receipt — plus a paired positive requiring a conforming baseline to state none, so the suite cannot pass by naming everything. **`D-05`** — `EVAL_ACTION_BINDS_MANDATE_AND_POLICY` folds two conditions under one code and only the mandate half had a witness; a row per HALF now, the same generalisation A-064 ratified for the window bounds and did not carry to conjunctions. **`D-06` — every ceiling and deadline was pinned only from OUTSIDE.** All five comparisons could flip `<=` to `<` with nothing failing, so a value EXACTLY at a ceiling — the commonest real case, and the one a mandate author would think they had authorised — would have been REFUSED. Four at-the-boundary PASS rows added. **`E2` — two `uint256` wire fields escaped `bounded()`**, so an out-of-range value parsed and then threw inside `leftPad` at signing time: a SIGNER_ERROR from within the signing routine instead of a BAD_REQUEST at the boundary, which is A-044's defect in the two fields it did not reach, and it means the one request a caller could trigger at will produced NO D-012 refusal record. **`C-3` — the whole internal-call trace walk could be replaced with `return []`** and all 426 tests stayed green, because every real trace in the suite is a vault→DemoPay call with no subcalls and the end-to-end assertion checks only that the KEY is present. So `EVAL_CALL_GRAPH_EXPECTED` and §3.3(11)'s unexpected-internal-call defence rested on a function nothing measured. Three pure unit tests now pin depth-first order, recursion past the first level, and the empty case; the mutation produces six failures. **`G-4` — the D-011(c) disagreement rate is the declared S2 HALT CONDITION and had no sample-size floor**, so at n=0 it printed "Rate is within the declared threshold" — a halt condition reporting itself satisfied on no evidence. A floor now says so loudly, and the static caveat is derived from the sample rather than asserted in prose. **`F-VAULT-3` — "It now covers both" stated an EXECUTION-PATH bound and was read as a CHECK-coverage bound.** Corrected in the S2 pack: the repaired campaign still cannot construct a violation of ANY of the vault's twelve action- and receipt-validation checks, all twelve mutations survive all eleven invariants, and Gate 6 is carried by the deterministic tests while the campaign corroborates — which `scripts/test.sh` has said since 2026-08-16 and the pack did not. **[CORRECTED 2026-08-18 by A-073: "the campaign corroborates" is WITHDRAWN. Re-measured, its marginal contribution is ZERO — the one mutant it caught was also caught without it — and it passes 11/11 on a vault that can never execute anything. Gate 6 is carried ENTIRELY by the deterministic tests. RATIFIED AND CERTIFIED by John 2026-08-18 (D-054(b)).]** **TEN FINDINGS ACCEPTED AS LIMITS rather than fixed (D-051(b)), written into `gate-s2-evidence.md` §11.0 with the adjudicators' reasoning.** **TWO FALSIFICATIONS FAILED BEFORE THEY WORKED and both are recorded:** the `E2` revert did not compile, so whole suites failed for a reason unrelated to the bound and would have read as a successful falsification; and a `C-3` probe's grep pattern matched nothing, which reads identically to "the mutation was caught". **Deep gate green: 75 Foundry, 481 TypeScript, 176 verifier, 78 tamper cases over 30 modes.**

- **A-069 (2026-08-18) — E4's VERIFIER HALF, built under D-051 after John ruled "verifier only". The recommendation turned on a fact that was measured rather than argued.** **THE QUESTION THAT DECIDED WHERE THE CHECK GOES.** E4's gap is that `normalizedAction` and `expectedEffects` are checked by NEITHER the signer nor the D-010 verifier: `evidenceHash` commits to them so they are tamper-evident, but nothing compared them to the action and mandate they purport to describe, so a bundle could state expected effects its own action does not imply and verify. The objection to putting the comparison in the verifier was that it would make the verifier a SECOND EVALUATOR — the exact coupling D-014 refused for the signer and the thing D-010's independence argument rests on. **That objection is refuted by the artifacts, and I checked before recommending rather than after: both fields are pure PROJECTIONS.** `normalizedAction` is the §5.3 ActionPayload restated verbatim plus `callData`; `expectedEffects` is seven fields copied from the mandate, `maxAllowanceIncreaseBaseUnits` from the policy, and the native ceiling. **No judgement is imported. The question the verifier now asks is only "does this bundle describe the documents it claims to describe".** **WHAT WAS BUILT:** `normalizedAction` compared field by field against `action.json` across all of `ACTION_FIELDS`; `keccak256(normalizedAction.callData) == action.dataHash`, because agreeing field by field while the BYTES the evidence was computed over are something else is the hole that comparison would otherwise leave; and `expectedEffects` against the mandate and policy. **THE SIGNER HALF WAS NOT BUILT AND THAT IS THE RULING, NOT AN OMISSION.** D-014 deliberately keeps conformance out of the signer; the verifier closes the same hole from the independent side without touching it, and putting it in both would weaken the independence that makes the D-010 artifact worth having. **FALSIFIED TO A-056's STANDARD, AND THE FIRST ATTEMPT FAILED THAT STANDARD.** Mutating `expectedEffects` in place was caught — by the CANONICALIZATION and hash checks, not by the new one, which proves nothing about the new one. The real test needed a bundle that is wholly self-consistent and lies: re-canonicalised, re-hashed, re-bound into the receipt and **re-signed with the deployment key**. Against that bundle **exactly one check fails and it is the new one**, and with the new checks neutered the same bundle verifies `1/1 sample(s) verified`. Three regression tests plus a RESEAL CONTROL that must verify, so the suite cannot go green because the reseal produces malformed bundles. All three fail against the pre-fix code. **THE FIXTURE GAP IS WORTH MORE THAN THE CHECK.** §5.2's published text says mandate and policy constraints are INTERSECTED, so the binding native ceiling is the lower of the two — but **all 50 corpus fixtures carry equal ceilings**, so a verifier written against the wrong reading would pass every test in this repository. The intersection is asserted by unit tests that construct the divergence and by nothing in the corpus. **This is the D-010 experiment working as designed for the second time** — an implementation derived from the published spec finding what the corpus does not exercise; the first was §5.5.1, which the specification did not define at all (A-042). A fixture with divergent ceilings is owed at v1.1 and does NOT fire D-043(b)'s re-label trigger, since it adds a fixture rather than moving a label. **ONE THING I DID NOT DO:** the adjudicator also asked for a correction at `docs/gate-s1-evidence.md:152`. I read it and judged it sound — that sentence is about the signer binding its OWN decoding, which is real and untouched by this gap — so it is left alone rather than "corrected" into something false. **Verifier 176 → 180. Deep gate green.**

- **D-052 (2026-08-18) — ROUND SIX RULED NOT CLEAN, AND THE LOOP IS PAUSED TO RECUT BOTH THE REPAIR PROTOCOL AND THE TERMINATING CONDITION. Two rulings at a facilitated session; the agent presented verified facts, options, costs and a recommendation for each and made neither call.** **(a) ROUND SIX IS NOT CLEAN, AND IT IS ALSO NOT A QUALIFYING CLEAN ROUND — TWO SEPARATE THINGS, RULED SEPARATELY.** Nine reviewers at frozen `140c59e`, one per D-050(1) surface, each with its own worktree and evidence directory, each returning a coverage statement: **91 findings.** Setting aside every lead the agent did not reproduce itself, the independently reproduced set alone requires changes to code, comments, documents and recorded claims — so D-047's condition is not met and **declining any of them would itself be a change to a claim.** **John ruled the provisioning defect does NOT erase the findings**; it only means round six cannot serve as the qualifying clean round D-047 requires. **The reproduced set, because a count is not a finding:** D-014's ratified justification and `docs/gate-s1-evidence.md:124`/`:152` — a SIGNED pack — name a conformance comparison the D-010 verifier does not perform (`grep -c decodedSelectorAndParameters verifier/verify.py` → 0; `_calldata_check` binds calldata→`dataHash` only, `normalizedAction` is compared to the action and `expectedEffects` to the mandate, and nothing compares the signer-attested decoded parameters to the mandate's purpose fields); A-063's own record claims "a grep for the claim's other spellings found no fifth site" while two remain, one of them `contracts/src/SentinelVault.sol:12-16`, the vault's own NatSpec, and the other `§4:238`, which `F-VAULT-1` had named BY LINE NUMBER; `docs/gate-s2-evidence.md:458` (§11.0, the section D-051(b) created) states "the nine MEDIUM findings were fixed" where A-068's own headline says seven, two are design forks; `A-062` and `A-065` have NO entry in this file while `session-state.md` asserts "every entry below has a full entry there"; `HANDOFF.md:93` still says "verified — no git remote" and a remote exists; `verify.py` is 2069 lines where two documents say 1681; **F006 diverges by a factor of 500** (`mandate.maxNativeValueWei` 1e18 vs `policy` 2e15), refuting A-069's and register §13.5's "all 50 corpus fixtures carry equal ceilings" — a claim written the same day, and the premise §11.0 used to downgrade `D-09(c)`; `scripts/mutate.sh`'s `C3b` anchor is dead (`isDeclared` → `isDeclaredAt`) so `session-state.md:573`'s "batch C 14/14 caught, 0 failed to apply" is false; **`check-secrets.sh` passes a 64-hex value bound to `SENTINEL_SIGNER_KEY`, this repository's own variable, because it lacks a `0x`** — the fourth hole in that guard, reproduced with a blocking control; the §7.3 latency columns are outside all provenance (`test.sh:243` drops `micros`, which is the sole input to the published p50/p95) and a fabricated-latency tree passes the gate; and `_refusal_checks` returns at `verify.py:401` while `_override_checks` sits at `:474`, unreachable on the refusal path. **(b) THE LOOP IS PAUSED. BOTH THE REPAIR PROTOCOL AND THE TERMINATING CONDITION ARE RECUT BEFORE ANY ROUND SEVEN.** John's ruling in his own terms: **first reproduce and remediate the confirmed Critical/High findings, repair the worktree apparatus, and require repairs to be tested against SIBLING PATHS and the UNDERLYING ARGUMENT; at the same time replace D-047's unbounded "entirely clean round" requirement with a BOUNDED, RISK-BASED EXIT CRITERION SET BY JOHN. No round seven and no pre-publication begins until both are complete.** **WHAT TRIGGERED THIS, AND THE AGENT SAID IT RATHER THAN LEAVING IT TO BE INFERRED, WHICH IS WHAT D-048(b) OBLIGES.** D-048(b): *if a round returns severe INSTRUMENT defects at round one's rate, the loop is not converging, and an agent must SAY SO and hand the decision back rather than continue on the letter of this ruling.* **The honest signal is not the raw count** — round six had nine reviewers against round five's eight and a wider ratified scope, so 91 versus 51 is partly just more looking, and that was put to John first. **The signal is WHERE the findings sit: four of the last five repairs were defeated by the same failure mode they were written to avoid, within 48 hours of being made.** A-063 (certified by John the same week) claims no fifth site and left two; A-066 fixed one of three call sites of the identical socket construction; A-068's `C-3` pinned the pure walk and left its input and its output deletable, its `D-06` pinned four of five boundaries, and its `G-4` added a sentence rather than a check; A-069 closed E4 for two of three fields and left the one D-014's justification rests on, and its entry records refusing an adjudicator's request to correct `gate-s1-evidence.md:152` on the ground "I read it and judged it sound" — **the adjudicator was right.** Together with three consecutive rounds finding live certification defects inside `verify.py`'s previous repairs, **repairs are generating findings at roughly the rate they close them**, and D-048's tolerance rested explicitly on findings "converging in KIND". They have not. **D-050(1)'s OWN REVERSAL CONDITION ALSO FIRED, on its own terms** — *"a definition that cannot be met is worse than none… that comes back to John, it is not an agent's to quietly under-run."* The ratified definition of full breadth prescribes provisioning (`session-state.md` §1 step 1: symlink both `contracts/lib/*` submodule directories) that makes its own condition 1 unmeetable: with symlinks forge resolves **4** remappings and omits `@openzeppelin/contracts/=lib/openzeppelin-contracts/contracts/`, which changes `settings.remappings` in the solc metadata, the CBOR trailer, the deployed bytecode and therefore `targetCodeHash` — so all 50 committed view digests mismatch and the deep gate cannot pass. With real directories forge resolves **5** and the build is byte-identical to the live tree; **the two builds agree for the first 617 bytes and differ only in the metadata digest.** Reproduced in both directions on a clean rebuild. Five lenses hit it independently. **Consequence for the record: register §13.1 is MISDIAGNOSED** — it blames the worktree for the `targetCodeHash` divergence; the cause is the symlinking, so round five wrote off a class of reviewer coverage on a cause that was never the cause. **AND THE APPARATUS FAILED IN A SECOND WAY THAT IS THE AGENT'S FAULT AND IS RECORDED AS SUCH:** the worktrees were provisioned with `ln -sfn` against existing empty submodule directories, which NESTS the link instead of replacing it, breaking `forge build` in most trees; reviewers then repaired their own trees, the coordinator's fix overwrote at least one reviewer's real copies with symlinks, and worktree state changed under running reviewers in both directions. **The round froze the commit and never froze the environment**, and one lens measured two identical invocations 31 seconds apart returning FAIL then PASS with HEAD unchanged. A consequence is honestly unresolved and left so: one lens reports a green deep gate having reported no environment repair, which the reproducible mechanism says should have been impossible — **so whether ANY lens satisfied condition 1 on the prescribed provisioning is UNKNOWN, not yes.** Nine concurrent reviewers also drove load average above 100; three lenses recorded probes that flaked to a false CAUGHT and reversed on re-run, and one lost a probe mid-mutation to a timeout that left a 0-byte log beside an "exit 0" notification. **THE COUNTER-ARGUMENT, RECORDED BECAUSE JOHN HEARD IT AND RULED ANYWAY:** D-048's own — Sentinel is a portfolio lab whose value is being seen, the gates that would let anyone see it (Gate 8, the D-016 rename) have not moved at all, and pausing to recut two protocols adds further delay ahead of them; there is a version of "finish the loop first" that is simply never shipping, and a pause to redesign the process is exposed to it twice over. Against that: the loop is finding real, live defects — a false claim inside a SIGNED pack, and a verifier that certifies artifacts it should refuse — so stopping outright was rejected, and **remediating the Critical/High findings DURING the pause is what makes the pause safe rather than a deferral.** **REVERSAL CONDITIONS, three, and they bind the recut rather than the loop:** (a) if remediation performed under the new sibling-and-argument protocol itself produces defects at the rate the last five repairs did, the protocol change has not worked and it comes back to John rather than being iterated by an agent; (b) if the bounded exit criterion, once set, would have passed any of rounds one through six on their actual findings — including a round carrying a live certification defect — it is too loose and comes back; (c) if reproducing the Critical/High findings shows a material fraction were reviewer error rather than real, the non-convergence reading in (b) is weaker than stated and the pause should be reconsidered. **WHAT NEITHER RULING AUTHORISES:** no gate is signed, no public claim is certified, D-016 still blocks all publication and the repository is PRIVATE, Gate 8 remains PRE-PUBLICATION under D-032, the five D-008 comprehension questions stay unseen, and **the bounded exit criterion is JOHN'S TO SET — an agent may prepare measured input for that decision and may not author, adopt or infer the criterion**, which is D-047's standing rule and is not relaxed by being replaced.

- **A-070 (2026-08-18) — THE FIRST REMEDIATION UNDER THE D-052(b) REPAIR PROTOCOL: the D-010 verifier's absence-is-agreement class, the credential guard's fourth hole, and TWO EXPLOITABLE SIBLINGS NINE REVIEWERS DID NOT REPORT. Authorised by D-052(b) ("first reproduce and remediate the confirmed Critical/High findings"); this entry records the REMEDIATION, D-052 records the ruling.** **THE ARGUMENTS, written before the code and stated as general properties rather than as the probes that broke them, which is protocol step 1.** (i) *A bundle that does not CARRY a required §5.6 projection cannot be certified as describing the documents it claims to describe* — omission is the cheapest evasion and must cost more than contradiction, not less. (ii) *A check that establishes a property of a bundle must run on EVERY path that certifies a bundle.* (iii) *A 64-hex value bound to a key-shaped identifier is a credential however it is spelled.* (iv) *A guard must not depend on the CASE of the thing it is scanning.* **WHAT WAS REPRODUCED FIRST, from scratch, with my own probe and controls — not by re-running a reviewer's script (D-051(c), carried into D-052(b)).** A reseal harness (`re-canonicalise → re-hash → re-bind receipt.evidenceHash → RE-SIGN with the deployment key`) so that only the check under test can reject a bundle, with two controls that must move in opposite directions: an unmutated reseal must VERIFY, and a contradicted `expectedEffects.beneficiary` must FAIL on exactly one named check. Against the pre-fix verifier, **six routes certified `=> PASS`, exit 0**: `expectedEffects` deleted, `expectedEffects` wrapped in a one-element list, `normalizedAction` deleted, `normalizedAction` wrapped in a list — and then the two siblings below. **THE SIBLING SWEEP IS THE PART THAT JUSTIFIES THE PROTOCOL, AND IT IS WHY THIS ENTRY EXISTS.** Protocol step 2 requires enumerating siblings MECHANICALLY rather than from memory, so `grep -nE "if .*isinstance\(" verifier/verify.py` was run over the whole file rather than only the two sites round six named. Twenty gated blocks; the sweep found **two more that silently emit NO Check at all**, reported by NONE of the nine lenses: **`evidence.anchor` deleted verifies `=> PASS`** — and A-056 had added the `receipt-anchor-split` tamper mode precisely because "THE ANCHOR HAD NO TEST AT ALL", so deleting the anchor outright was the cheaper attack on the same binding, left open by the repair that closed the expensive one — and **`evidence.verdict` deleted verifies `=> PASS`**, never compared against the receipt's enum. **The same sweep also showed the file disagreeing with itself:** `reasonCodes is a list`, two hundred lines away, already had the correct shape and emits a FAILING check on the wrong type. The correct pattern was in the file the whole time. **STEP 2's SECOND QUESTION — "which paths does this check NOT run on?" — produced the round's L6-2 as a by-product.** `verify_sample` calls `_refusal_checks` and RETURNS at `:401`, while `_override_checks` (`:474`) and `_chain_checks` — and therefore the §5.6 projections — sit below that return. So the entire §5.5.1 refusal path reached none of them, and an `override.json` minted by an arbitrary outsider key rode along inside a bundle this verifier printed `=> PASS` over, while the SAME artifact is correctly rejected on the receipt path by A-059's owner-identity check. **The verifier's own `--tamper all` arm testified against it**, printing six consecutive `WRONGLY ACCEPTED` lines for the override modes on the very bundle the certifying arm passed. **REJECTED RATHER THAN CHECKED, deliberately:** §0 of `_refusal_checks` already refuses a bundle presenting "a decision OR a refusal, not both" on the stated ground that certifying it would certify whichever half the reader looked at; an override IS an authorization, so a refusal carrying one is that same shape. **WHAT WAS NOT ADDED TO THAT PATH, stated so it reads as a decision rather than an oversight:** the anchor and evidence-verdict checks are receipt-side by construction — both compare against fields of a §5.4 receipt, which a refusal bundle does not have — so their absence there is correct. **FALSIFIED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS AND THROUGH BOTH INVOCATION SHAPES (steps 3 and 5).** All six absence/wrong-type routes and the refusal-override route now FAIL; the two reseal controls still behave; **the seven committed samples still verify 7/7 and the tamper arm still reports 7/7 across 30 modes.** For the credential guard: three evasions closed — no `0x` prefix (`SENTINEL_SIGNER_KEY=<64hex>`, **this repository's own variable**), a digit anywhere in the identifier (`KEY_1`), and a collection value (`{"signerKeys": ["0x…"]}`) — plus two routes NO reviewer demonstrated (a lowercase TypeScript type annotation, a MNEMONIC-family name holding a collection), plus the scan-scope asymmetry: `git ls-files` is TRACKED ONLY while `check-vendor-honesty.sh` beside it already scanned untracked on the stated reasoning that such a file is "one `git add -A` away from being published". Falsified through the default AND `--staged` shapes, with four negative controls still passing (both Anvil allowlist spellings, a genuine placeholder, a legitimate non-key `bytes32`). **EIGHT REGRESSION TESTS, EVERY ONE RUN AGAINST THE PRE-FIX CODE AND CONFIRMED TO FAIL** — seven fail, and the eighth is the paired reseal/refusal CONTROL which passes both before and after, because a control that fails pre-fix is not a control. Verifier suite **180 → 189**, and `VERIFIER_MIN_TESTS` was ratcheted in the SAME EDIT as the suite it bounds, which is the rule that line exists to enforce and the rule it has broken three times. **RESIDUALS, REQUIRED BY STEP 6 AND THE REASON THIS ENTRY IS NOT A CLAIM OF COMPLETENESS.** (a) The credential guard matches a key-shaped name only where it binds the value DIRECTLY: `{apiKey3: {"v": "<64hex>"}}` still passes, because the hex is bound to `v`. One bracket level is covered; arbitrary nesting is not, and chasing it with a regex would trade a real false-negative for a worse false-positive rate across a repository full of `bytes32` literals — under argument (iii) this case sits with the design note's declared bare-literal residual. (b) The §5.6 projections are now required on both paths, but WHAT THEY COMPARE is unchanged: `decodedSelectorAndParameters` is still compared to nothing, which is round six's separate and larger finding that D-014's own justification names a conformance comparison this verifier does not perform. **That one is not fixed here and is not an agent's to close — it changes what the product guarantees and it sits inside a SIGNED gate pack.** (c) The repair protocol itself is prose, and `check-label-prompt.sh`'s header states the project's own standard that "a durable project rule gets a mechanical guard rather than prose"; the mechanical half is unbuilt, unscoped, and John's to rule on. **THREE STALE CLAIMS CORRECTED IN PLACE, each stating the error before its content (A-048's rule).** `verify.py`'s §5.2 comment and register §13.5 both said "no corpus fixture has them diverge / all 50 carry equal ceilings" — **`F006` diverges by a factor of 500** (mandate `1e18` vs policy `2e15`), its declared intent says so in words, and its result file records `BLOCK` on `EVAL_VALUE_WITHIN_POLICY`; measured across all 50, exactly one diverges. The claim IS true of the seven committed SAMPLE bundles, so it attributed the gap to the wrong artifact — the prescribed remedy would have added a fixture to a corpus that already has one, and **§11.0 used the same false premise to downgrade `D-09(c)`**. What is owed is a divergent SAMPLE. And register §8.2's own record of the case-sensitive path scan exhibited the hole with a LITERAL capitalised home path in a tracked file, which failed the workspace machine-state guard; the example is now a `<Name>` placeholder and **the underlying scan is fixed to `[A-Za-z]` on both branches** — the identical defect A-047 fixed one file over, where the vendor scan was case-sensitive while the label scan beside it was not, and the argument was not carried across at the time. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO:** no gate is signed, no public claim is certified, nothing in the SIGNED S1 or S2 packs is edited, D-016 is untouched, and none of round six's product-guarantee findings — the nonce-reservation cycle, the vault's unbounded rate and the atomicity of the drain — is acted on; those go to John as decisions before any architecture changes.

- **A-071 (2026-08-18) — THE REVIEW APPARATUS REPAIRED AT THE ARGUMENT LEVEL: remappings pinned, and A-066's socket fallback collapsed from one-of-three copies into one shared implementation. Authorised by D-052(b) ("repair the worktree apparatus"), John's stated priority 1, "so later evidence is reproducible".** **THE ARGUMENTS, written first (protocol step 1).** (i) *The compiled bytecode must not depend on HOW the libraries happen to be mounted.* (ii) *A construction that must be correct in three places should exist in one.* **THE DEFECT WAS NEVER "WORKTREES", AND THAT MISDIAGNOSIS COST A WHOLE ROUND.** Register §13.1 recorded, from round five, that "a corpus run in a worktree differs from all 50 committed views on `targetCodeHash`, so the committed-view comparison cannot be exercised from a worktree either" — an inherent limit on reviewer capability. It is not inherent. `contracts/foundry.toml` declared TWO remappings and let forge auto-detect the other three by walking `lib/`; `session-state.md` §1 step 1 told every reviewer to SYMLINK `contracts/lib/*`; and through a symlink that walk does not find OpenZeppelin's own nested `remappings.txt`. Four entries resolve instead of five, omitting `@openzeppelin/contracts/=lib/openzeppelin-contracts/contracts/`. That list is recorded in solc's `settings.remappings`, which is hashed into the metadata, which is the CBOR trailer of the deployed bytecode — so the remapping LIST is an input to `targetCodeHash`, the value every mandate binds and all 50 committed views carry. **Measured in both directions on a clean rebuild: symlinked libs → 4 remappings and a differing DemoPay bytecode; real directories → 5 and byte-identical to the live tree. The two builds agree for their first 617 bytes and differ only in the metadata digest.** **WHY THIS MATTERED MORE THAN A BUILD ANNOYANCE.** D-050(1) made "at least one reviewer must be able to run the DEEP profile" a CONDITION of a valid full-breadth round, and `docs/round-six-brief.md` recorded the blocker as **"SOLVED 2026-08-17 (A-066)"**. A-066 solved the SOCKET half and its verification measured that `npm run corpus` produces 51 result files — which is the gate REACHING the comparison it then fails, not the gate passing. So round six ran against an apparatus that could not satisfy its own ratified definition, five lenses hit it independently, and whether ANY lens met condition 1 on the prescribed provisioning is recorded as UNKNOWN. **THE FIX IS A PIN, NOT A PROCEDURE, AND THE DISTINCTION IS THE POINT.** Telling reviewers to use real submodule checkouts would have worked and would have been the wrong repair: a procedure that must be remembered is exactly what failed here, twice. `auto_detect_remappings = false` plus the full five-entry list makes the build a function of committed configuration alone. **A `remappings.txt` does NOT achieve this — forge merges auto-detected entries on top of it regardless, which round six measured before recommending it.** The list is byte-for-byte what the live tree auto-detected, in solc's own order. **FALSIFIED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, INCLUDING THE ONE THAT MATTERS FOR EVIDENCE:** the live tree's `DemoPay` bytecode is UNCHANGED by the pin, so every committed view digest and the whole §7.1 corpus are untouched; and **`./scripts/test.sh --gate` now returns `GATE PASSED` from a SYMLINKED worktree — the exact provisioning that made the condition unmeetable — with "50 fixtures executed; committed views verified FILE BY FILE" and "51 result files identical to the committed set".** Those two stages had never once been exercised by a reviewer. **THE SECOND HALF: A-066's SIBLINGS (round six L7-3).** The identical socket construction appears at THREE call sites. A-066 fixed `ts/src/corpus/run.ts` and left `ts/src/tools/sample-check.ts` and `ts/src/tools/emit-samples.ts` raw, so both still died `connect EINVAL` from a worktree — and `sample-check` is John's D-006 adversarial-sampling instrument, named as a runnable command in the SIGNED Gate S1 evidence (`Run it yourself with: npm --prefix ts run sample-check`), while `emit-samples` generates the D-010 verifier's entire fixture corpus. **Adding the fallback twice more would have been generalising the demonstration**, which is the failure mode the protocol exists to stop, so the logic moved to `ts/src/signer/socket-path.ts` and all three callers share it. A fourth caller cannot now get it wrong by omission. **Falsified: `npm --prefix ts run sample-check` runs to completion from a 133-byte worktree path, where it previously aborted before reaching any code under test.** Typecheck clean, TypeScript suite 481/481, fast gate PASSED, workspace guards PASS. **RESIDUALS (step 6).** (a) `session-state.md` §1 step 1 keeps the symlink instruction, because the pin makes it safe — but three worktree hazards are now recorded there rather than left to be rediscovered: `ln -sfn` NESTS the link inside an existing directory instead of replacing it (which broke most of round six's trees, and is mine); `git status` with no pathspec exits 128 in a symlinked worktree, silently truncating `&&` chains; and `git checkout -- .` destroys the symlinks and then reports a CLEAN tree while a cached `forge build` still exits 0 — a revert that verifies clean with the toolchain gone. (b) The pin makes the build reproducible across mountings; it does NOT make the campaign's revert tallies deterministic, and `foundry.toml`'s existing note on that stands unchanged. (c) Nothing here re-runs round six. A round run on a broken apparatus is not retroactively valid, and whether round six's coverage should be re-taken under the repaired apparatus is John's call under D-052(b), not an agent's.

- **A-072 (2026-08-18) — D-06 CLOSED AT THE ARGUMENT LEVEL (six unpinned edges, not the one it named), THE CALL-GRAPH PIPELINE PINNED AT BOTH ENDS, AND THE ~9% FLAKE THAT MADE EVERY BASELINE UNRELIABLE. Authorised by D-052(b); John's stated priority 2.** **(a) `D-06`: A-068 PINNED FOUR OF FIVE, AND A MECHANICAL SWEEP FOUND FOUR MORE IT NEVER CLAIMED.** A-068's own test comment reads *"`<=` could become `<` on all five and nothing failed… this is that generalisation"* and then adds FOUR at-the-boundary PASS rows. `EVAL_APPROVAL_CEILING` — the fifth, which `D-06`'s reproduction text names by line — got none, and `docs/decisions.md`, the test comment and register §13.4 all record the repair as complete. **Protocol step 2's mechanical sweep is what turned one finding into six:** `grep` every comparison in `ts/src/evaluate/checks.ts` → **8 sites, 10 edges, 4 pinned.** Beyond the named fifth, A-064 had split both window checks BY BOUND but given each bound only a VIOLATION row — which pins the comparison's DIRECTION and not its EDGE — so `EVAL_MANDATE_WINDOW`'s and `EVAL_POLICY_WINDOW`'s lower AND upper edges were unpinned too, as was `EVAL_ALLOWANCE_EFFECT_WITHIN_CEILING`. **MEASURED BEFORE THE FIX, with the already-pinned `EVAL_ACTION_DEADLINE` edge as a control that correctly FAILED: all six mutations left `evaluate.checks.test.ts` at `pass 96 / fail 0`.** Six at-boundary PASS rows added; **all ten edges now fail their mutation**, controls included. The harm is A-068's own stated one and it is not hypothetical: a value or timestamp EXACTLY at a declared limit is the commonest real case and the one a mandate author would believe they had authorised, and every one of these flipped it to a refusal. **(b) THE CALL GRAPH: A-068 PINNED THE PURE WALK AND LEFT ITS INPUT AND ITS OUTPUT DELETABLE.** Round six deleted both with the whole 481-test suite green — `subcalls = internalCalls(callTrace).map(…)` → `[]`, and `{tracer: "callTracer"}` → `{tracer: "prestateTracer"}`. **The tracer swap is the worse of the two: `prestateTracer` returns an account map with no `calls` key at any depth AND DOES NOT ERROR, so `internalCalls` returns `[]` for every transaction there can ever be and `SIM_CALL_TRACE_UNAVAILABLE` never fires.** Silence reads exactly like "this call made no internal calls" — the project's failure mode 6, in the product rather than in a probe. **No profile of the gate could catch either**, and that is measured rather than argued: `internalCallCount` is 0 in 46 of the 50 committed views and null in the other 4, and `internalCallTrace` is `[]` in all seven sample bundles, so **no committed artifact anywhere carries a non-empty call graph to compare against.** Since `EVAL_CALL_GRAPH_EXPECTED` is a hard `require_(simulation.internalCalls.length === 0, …)`, an always-empty producer makes §3.3(11)'s unexpected-internal-call defence pass unconditionally. Two tests now pin the two ends — a synthetic nested trace injected through the real `control` so the chain still runs and only the trace's shape is ours, asserting depth-first mapping excluding the root; and a stub client asserting the tracer parameter is `callTracer`. **Both fail against the exact mutations that survived round six.** **(c) THE FLAKE, fixed because it was blocking a reliable gate rather than because it was on the list.** `simulate.test.ts`'s *"is repeatable: the same simulation twice gives the same effects"* compared `expiryAfter - expiryBefore` across two runs. Both are snapshot-isolated so `expiryBefore` is 0 in both, and the quantity actually compared was `block.timestamp + duration` — **the wall clock.** Anvil keeps block timestamps strictly increasing, so it failed whenever the two simulations straddled a second boundary: one round-six lens measured **2 failures in 22 runs**, another hit it independently, and it was the sole reason one lens's baseline was not green. **A test that fails ~9% of the time for a reason unrelated to what it names is worse than no test, because its failure is indistinguishable from the leak it exists to detect.** THE PROPERTY IS ABOUT THE PRE-STATE: "the first run left nothing behind" means the second simulation must OBSERVE the same starting chain, which is `expiryBefore` and is clock-independent. **Falsified rather than assumed: no-op'ing the snapshot revert — a real leak — still fails the rewritten assertion.** Two consecutive fast gates now pass at `tests 489 / fail 0`. **RESIDUALS (step 6).** (a) **There is still NO gate floor on the Foundry or TypeScript suite counts** — `grep -n 'MIN_\|FLOOR' scripts/*.sh` finds floors only for the verifier, so a SHRINKING TypeScript suite is invisible to the gate. That is round six's `L8-14`, it is the same defect A-047 closed for the verifier and did not generalise, and it is deliberately NOT fixed here: it is outside D-052(b)'s Critical/High scope and adding a floor is a change to what the gate asserts. Recorded in the register. (b) The six new rows pin the EDGES; they say nothing about whether the ceilings are the right ceilings, which is §5.2's intersection question and is separately recorded. (c) `EVAL_CALL_GRAPH_EXPECTED` is still asserted by no CORPUS fixture and no SAMPLE — `D-025` reserves that class deliberately — so the pipeline is now pinned by unit tests only, and the gate remains blind to it. Stated so a green gate is not read as coverage it does not have.

- **A-073 (2026-08-18) — THE INVARIANT CAMPAIGN'S CLAIMS CORRECTED TO WHAT WAS MEASURED: marginal contribution ZERO, the enumeration incomplete, and a campaign that passes identically on a vault that can never execute. Authorised by D-052(b); John's stated priority 3, "correct or rebuild the invariant campaign's claims based on its measured zero marginal power". CORRECTED, NOT REBUILT — and the choice is stated rather than assumed.** **REPRODUCED FIRST, INDEPENDENTLY, because round six's lens 4 was a lead I had not run.** Baseline on clean code, both arms separated: campaign alone (`--match-test invariant_`) **11 passed**, deterministic alone (`--no-match-test invariant_`) **64 passed**. **THE VACUITY PROBE IS THE SHARPEST RESULT AND IT REPRODUCED EXACTLY.** Inverting the chain check to `action.chainId == block.chainid`, so that NO action can ever execute, leaves the campaign at **11 passed / 0 failed** while the deterministic suite produces **42 failures**. All ten live invariants are `assertFalse(ghost)` predicates and zero executions satisfies every one. **The campaign cannot distinguish a working vault from a dead one.** §7's `test_nonVacuity_*` tests are real and do fire, but they run as SEPARATE test instances — **nothing asserts that the campaign's OWN 16,384-call run executed anything**, which is a different guarantee from the one §7's prose implies. **THE MARGINAL-POWER MEASUREMENT.** Nine validation checks deleted one at a time, each mutant run through both arms separately: **the campaign caught 1, the deterministic tests caught 9, and the one the campaign caught was also caught without it.** Marginal contribution: **zero**. The single exception is the `keccak256(callData) != action.dataHash` recompute — which §7's list of twelve does not name either, so it is not even in the set the claim is about. Lens 4 independently found the same single exception from a 25-mutation sweep, which is the strongest corroboration available for a figure like this. **§7's "TWELVE" IS INCOMPLETE:** `ReceiptActionMismatch` and `OverrideExpired` also survive all eleven invariants and are not among the twelve, so the true figure is **at least fourteen**. **WHY CORRECTED RATHER THAN REBUILT, since John's instruction allowed either.** §7 already states the remedy — handler arms that build OUT-OF-BOUND bundles — and already scopes it as v1.1. Rebuilding the campaign is therefore a scope expansion into work the pack itself defers, and house rule 7 sends scope additions through John. **What a rebuild would need is now stated precisely rather than gestured at:** out-of-bound arms (a `valueWei` above the cap, a foreign target or selector, an expired receipt), AND a non-vacuity assertion that binds the campaign's own run rather than a sibling instance. The second is the harder half and is the one the Z1 probe exposes; an `afterInvariant` hook cannot do it, because Foundry shrinks a failing sequence to its minimum and any one-call sequence has zero executions by construction, which is exactly why the hook version was rejected in the first place. **WHAT IS WITHDRAWN AND WHAT IS NOT.** Gate 6's status is **UNCHANGED and still MET** — it always rested on the deterministic tests, they remain green, and they remain the thing that kills mutants. What is withdrawn is any reading in which the campaign ADDS assurance: it currently adds none that is measurable. **This is a repair of a claim to what was measured, not a new capability claim** — but it edits the SIGNED S2 pack, so on A-063's precedent it was offered for ratification rather than treated as ratified. **RATIFIED AND CERTIFIED BY JOHN 2026-08-18 (D-054(b)).** **RESIDUAL (step 6):** my sweep was NINE mutations against lens 4's twenty-five, chosen to cover the named twelve plus the four it said were missing; I did not re-run at the gate profile (2048×128), and lens 4's structural argument for why depth cannot change the result — `_bundle` hardcodes six fields and bounds `valueWei` to exactly the cap — I verified by reading rather than by measuring. A mutation whose only killer is a rare long sequence would not appear in what either of us ran.

- **D-053 (2026-08-18) — TWO PRODUCT-GUARANTEE RULINGS: the atomic drain is an ACCEPTED v1 BOUNDARY, and the nonce guard is REPAIRED to preserve reservations by authorisation basis. A facilitated session; the agent reproduced both findings itself with controls, presented facts, options, costs and a recommendation for each, and made neither call.** **(a) THE NATIVE-VALUE CEILING BOUNDS THE SHAPE OF EACH ACTION, NOT AGGREGATE LOSS AND NOT EXECUTION RATE. John's ruling, in his terms:** *"A compromised signer can issue sequential nonces and atomically drain the vault in one transaction; pause cannot interleave and provides protection only before execution begins or between transactions. This is an explicitly accepted v1 boundary of the testnet lab. No cumulative or rate-limited bound is added."* **REPRODUCED BEFORE BEING PUT TO HIM,** since lens 4 was a lead the agent had not run: a relayer contract calling `executeWithReceipt` in a loop drained a capped, funded vault to zero — **100 valid ALLOW receipts inside ONE transaction, ~75,700 gas each, `block.number` delta 0 and `block.timestamp` delta 0** — with the paired control confirming `cap + 1` still reverts `ValueOverCap`, so the drain is measured against a LIVE ceiling. At a 30M block limit that is roughly 396 actions per block. `nonReentrant` stops NESTING, not REPETITION, which is why the reentrancy guard is irrelevant here. **WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN "THE CAP IS PER-ACTION", which the pack already said.** §7.1 had retreated, under A-063 and John's own certification at D-051(a), to *"only PAUSE bounds a compromised signer — it bounds damage after somebody notices, which is a different and weaker guarantee."* **That retreat is itself too generous and it was the last position the row had left.** Because the drain is atomic there is no interval during it in which anyone could notice or act: no block boundary, no interleaved owner transaction, nothing. Pause protects only before execution begins or between transactions. **THREE THINGS JOHN ADDED, and each closes a gap the agent would otherwise have left.** (i) **Apply the correction to EVERY sibling claim, not only the certified row** — the proposal's §4 language, the vault's own NatSpec, §7.1, the S2 pack, and `scripts/test.sh`'s printed coverage boundary. All five are corrected in place, each stating the error before its content (A-048's rule). This is the same sibling discipline `docs/repair-protocol.md` step 2 requires, applied by the ruling rather than by the agent. (ii) **Remove `vm.warp` from the limit test and assert that the block number and timestamp are UNCHANGED across all 100 executions.** The test used to advance the clock a year between actions, so a reader took away "100 actions spread over a century" and the "after somebody notices" framing read as a real window; the warp is unnecessary and the test now asserts the opposite property, with regression messages naming what a failure would mean. (iii) **The existing "a cumulative or rate-limited ceiling is v1.1 work" commitment is WITHDRAWN** and relabelled an OPTIONAL FUTURE EXTENSION. Adding one would change what a mandate means onchain and would change the deployed bytecode and therefore `targetCodeHash`, which every mandate binds and all 50 committed corpus views carry. **(b) THE NONCE GUARD PRESERVES EVERY UNEXPIRED RESERVATION BY AUTHORISATION BASIS. John's ruling:** *"A request conflicts when any reservation for the same chain, vault, nonce, mandate and policy names a different action. Basis changes remain immediately usable, but cycling back cannot erase the earlier reservation. The mechanism remains per-process, best-effort defence in depth; the vault nonce remains the durable single-use guarantee."* **REPRODUCED WITH A CONTROL:** A1 signed under M1; a second action under an unchanged M1 correctly REFUSED with `SIGNER_NONCE_ALREADY_ATTESTED`, so the guard works; A2 signed under M2 (Case 4's remedy, by design); and then **A3 signed under M1 again — two live executable credentials at one nonce, both naming the SAME active mandate**, where A-012(a)'s escape clause ("the vault reverts `MandateNotActive` on the stale one") cannot engage because neither is stale. The mechanism: the map was keyed `chain:vault:nonce`, held ONE entry, and `record()` OVERWROTE on a basis change — so a change ERASED the earlier reservation rather than superseding it. **THE HARM, STATED PRECISELY SO IT IS NOT OVERSOLD:** the vault's nonce is authoritative, so only one of the two can ever execute. This is not a double-spend; it is the "whoever relays first wins, and the owner authorised only one of them" hazard the guard's own header names — and it needs NEITHER of D-013's two declared limits, no restart and no second process. **THE REPAIR COVERS THE ARGUMENT, NOT M1→M2→M1, because John enumerated the cases and the enumeration is the ruling.** Reservations are keyed by basis, so every unexpired one survives and a cycle finds the original still holding. Tested: **mandate cycles; POLICY cycles** (the basis is both halves, and pinning only the mandate would be the partial repair this project keeps making); **idempotent re-sign of the same action after a cycle** (must still succeed — preserving reservations must not deadlock the retry path a lost response needs); **expired superseded reservations** (expiry, not overwriting, is now what releases, so a cycle must not wedge the nonce for a whole TTL); **REVIEW as well as ALLOW** (REVIEW reserves because an owner override makes it executable — pinning only ALLOW would carry the hole through the human-in-the-loop path where it matters most); **signing-failure release removing only its own reservation** (the release now takes the basis, so it can only reach the reservation the request itself took — without that, preserving siblings would have let a signing error delete a live reservation under another basis, which is the very hazard the actionHash comparison guards within a basis); and **existing concurrency behaviour**, re-run unchanged. **PRUNING AND THE MEMORY RESIDUAL, since John named it:** `prune(now)` already runs on every request and deletes expired entries, and it is unchanged — but reservations are now per-basis, so a session that cycles bases rapidly holds proportionally more entries between prunes. Bounded by the TTL and by the prune on each request; noted as a residual rather than left to be discovered. **EVERY ABSOLUTE CLAIM CORRECTED.** *"strictly safe rather than a trade"* and *"two credentials can never be live"* are struck from `attest.ts`'s header. **The honest formulation John set, now the wording in the code: a second live executable attestation at one nonce is PREVENTED WITHIN ONE UNINTERRUPTED SIGNER PROCESS.** Restart and shared-key multi-process remain live limits, and the vault's nonce remains the durable guarantee. **FALSIFICATION, and the split matters.** Five tests added; against the pre-fix guard **three FAIL** (mandate cycle, policy cycle, REVIEW cycle) and **two PASS both before and after** — the idempotent re-sign and the expired-supersede cases are REGRESSION GUARDS for behaviour that must not break, so a test that failed pre-fix would be the wrong test. TypeScript 494/494, concurrency suite 3/3, Foundry green, fast gate PASSED. **WHAT NEITHER RULING AUTHORISES:** no contract change and no new onchain semantics; `targetCodeHash` and every committed view are untouched; no gate is signed; no public claim is certified. **The §7.1 row was certified by John at D-051(a) and this correction edits it, so on A-063's precedent it was offered for ratification rather than treated as ratified. RATIFIED AND CERTIFIED BY JOHN 2026-08-18 (D-054(a)), superseding D-051(a) ONLY where the earlier wording is inconsistent with the atomic-drain boundary.**

- **D-054 (2026-08-18) — TWO RATIFICATIONS, BOTH AS OFFERED. John's rulings at a facilitated session; the agent presented each correction, its measurement and its control, and recorded the ruling without making it.** **(a) THE D-053(a) ATOMIC-DRAIN CORRECTION TO §7.1 IS RATIFIED AND CERTIFIED.** What is certified, in the terms John ratified: **the native-value ceiling bounds the SHAPE of each action, not aggregate loss and not execution rate; a compromised signer can issue sequential nonces and drain the vault ATOMICALLY in one transaction; and pause protects only BEFORE execution begins or BETWEEN transactions.** **SCOPE OF SUPERSESSION, stated narrowly because that is how it was ratified: D-053(a) supersedes D-051(a) ONLY where the earlier wording is inconsistent with the demonstrated atomic-drain boundary.** Everything D-051(a) certified that is NOT inconsistent with it — that `maxNativeValueWei` is compared per action, that no cumulative bound exists anywhere in `contracts/src`, that of the four items D-042 listed only pause bounds a compromised signer at all — **stands unchanged and remains certified.** What is superseded is the single retreat: "it bounds damage after somebody notices" described a window that does not exist. **THE EVIDENCE CERTIFIED ON, not on a summary of it:** a relayer contract calling `executeWithReceipt` in a loop drains a capped, funded vault to zero — 100 valid ALLOW receipts inside ONE transaction, ~75,700 gas each, `block.number` delta 0 and `block.timestamp` delta 0 — with the paired control confirming `cap + 1` still reverts `ValueOverCap`, so the drain is measured against a LIVE ceiling. Asserted by `test_LIMIT_nativeCeilingIsPerActionAndBoundsNoAggregate`, which no longer warps the clock and now asserts the block number and timestamp UNCHANGED across all 100 executions, with regression messages naming what a failure would mean. **Also certified as part of this ruling:** the withdrawal of the "a cumulative or rate-limited ceiling is v1.1 work" commitment. **Aggregate native-value rate/cumulative bounding is an OPTIONAL FUTURE EXTENSION and is not promised; the per-action TOKEN-ALLOWANCE ceiling remains declared v1.1 work.** The unbounded aggregate is an explicitly accepted v1 boundary of a testnet lab. **(b) THE A-073 GATE 6 CORRECTION IS RATIFIED AND CERTIFIED.** What is certified: **Gate 6 remains MET on the deterministic evidence, while the invariant campaign supplies NO independently measurable assurance and can pass a vault that executes nothing.** §7's enumeration of twelve unreachable action- and receipt-validation checks is corrected to **at least fourteen** (`ReceiptActionMismatch` and `OverrideExpired` also survive all eleven invariants). **THE EVIDENCE CERTIFIED ON:** nine validation checks deleted one at a time, each mutant run through both arms separately — the campaign caught **1**, the deterministic tests caught **9**, and the one the campaign caught was also caught without it, so its marginal contribution is **zero**; and inverting the chain check so that NO action can ever execute leaves the campaign at **11 passed / 0 failed** while the deterministic suite produces **42 failures**, because all ten live invariants are `assertFalse(ghost)` predicates and zero executions satisfies every one. The `test_nonVacuity_*` tests run as SEPARATE instances, so nothing asserts that the campaign's own run executed anything. **WHAT NEITHER RATIFICATION DOES.** **The Gate S2 signature (D-041) otherwise STANDS, and NO gate's status changes** — Gate 6 was and remains MET, and it always rested on the deterministic tests. Nothing here re-signs a gate, certifies a public claim in the §2 capability table, README or resume language, or touches D-016. **What is withdrawn in (b) is only a reading:** that the campaign adds assurance. **RECORDED BECAUSE IT IS THE HONEST SHAPE OF BOTH RULINGS:** each certifies a repair from something FALSE or OVERSTATED to something MEASURED. Neither is a new capability claim, and neither improves the artifact — both make its description smaller and truer. **REVERSAL CONDITION for (b), from the measurement itself:** if the campaign is ever given out-of-bound handler arms AND a non-vacuity assertion binding its OWN run rather than a sibling instance, its marginal power must be re-measured before any document again describes it as adding assurance. Until then, no artifact may say it corroborates Gate 6.

- **D-055 (2026-08-18) — THE EXIT CRITERION IS ADOPTED (C1 + T1–T4, with John's clarification), AND THE THREE BLOCKERS ARE RULED. This replaces D-047's terminating condition. John's rulings; the agent prepared the measured packet and made none of the calls.** **(a) THE TERMINATING CONDITION, replacing D-047's "one full-breadth round with no finding".** **EXIT REQUIRES: one independent, FIXED-SCOPE post-D-052 review using the REPAIRED apparatus; a passing deep gate and workspace guards; ZERO unresolved confirmed Critical/High defects; and ZERO known false or unsupported signed/certified claims. Confirmed Medium/Low findings may remain ONLY when individually adjudicated, accurately documented as limits, and reflected in affected claims. "Zero findings of any severity" is expressly NOT the termination condition.** **THE FOUR TIGHTENINGS, adopted with it, each traceable to a defect this project shipped.** **T1 — a limit's BASIS must be verified, not just its wording.** §11.0 accepted `D-09(c)` on the premise "no corpus fixture has divergent ceilings", which F006 refutes; an adjudication can be accurate in form and false in basis. Each accepted limit's stated factual basis is independently verified and the verification recorded beside it. **T2 — severity is assigned by the independent reviewer/adjudicator**, not by the party exit depends on; any downgrade is recorded with reasoning and countersigned by John. **T3 — scope is fixed by JOHN BEFORE the review**, covering at minimum the nine D-050(1) surfaces plus every surface touched by D-052 remediation. **T4 — the exit record states the gate's carried and RATCHETED items explicitly**, so a green deep gate is not read as coverage it does not have. **JOHN'S CLARIFICATION, and it closes the gap the packet could not:** **an unadjudicated Critical/High lead is PENDING, not silently "unconfirmed"** — it blocks until it is adjudicated, so the criterion cannot be satisfied by leaving leads unexamined. **And a confirmed High ceases to block ONLY through verified repair, or through John's EXPLICIT acceptance as a documented product boundary.** An agent may do neither on its own. **WHY THIS IS NOT D-047 RENAMED.** D-047 was unbounded in principle and, as D-050(1) implemented it, unmeetable in practice. This is bounded by a single fixed-scope review and by a severity threshold the packet BACKTESTED against all six prior rounds: it blocks exit on every one, including all four that carried a live certification defect. **"Zero CRITICAL only" was rejected on evidence, not taste: round six had NO critical, and its live certification defects were HIGH — that rule would have permitted exit with one standing.** A budget-boxed rule would have exited mid-loop with 41 surviving mutations. **(b) BLOCKER 1 — THE D-010 VERIFIER IS FIXED; THE CLAIM IS NOT WITHDRAWN.** The signed Gate S1 pack says the verifier "does the conformance comparison" and `grep -c decodedSelectorAndParameters verifier/verify.py` returns 0. **John ruled the architecture, not the sentence, is what must hold: D-014 deliberately keeps conformance OUT of the signer precisely because the verifier completes it, so withdrawing the claim would leave the architecture without the half that justifies it.** **THE REQUIREMENT: any evidence verdict of `ALLOW` must have signer-attested decoded parameters CONFORMING TO THE MANDATE, on EVERY receipt and refusal verification path. Legitimate nonconforming `BLOCK`/`REVIEW` bundles must remain verifiable** — the corpus is full of them and they are the point. `docs/repair-protocol.md` applies, and the wrong-purpose ALLOW must be shown to PASS pre-fix and FAIL post-fix. **(c) BLOCKER 2 — E3, AND A RECORD CONFLICT RECONCILED FIRST AT JOHN'S DIRECTION.** **A-044(f) is correct and A-068 is the error.** A-044 — header: "Ruled by John one at a time" — recorded item (f) as *"THE SIMULATION ANCHOR IS RECORDED AS A DECLARED LIMIT, NOT FIXED"*, ruled 2026-08-16 on the reasoning that a recency bound is a policy parameter nobody had reasoned about. Round five's `E3` is the SAME defect. **A-068 re-characterised it as an unruled "open design fork" without citing A-044(f) at all** — its single A-044 reference is for `E2`. The finding did not change; the record drifted, in the direction that creates work. Both are now superseded. **THE RULING: FIX IT, AND DO NOT INVENT A TIMEOUT.** John's mechanism: **pin all signer state and code reads to ONE block, and require the evidence anchor to match that exact block number and hash, retrying if the head moves.** This is why the original acceptance was right to refuse a recency bound and wrong to stop there — the defensible answer is not a number, it is consistency: the signer attests against the state it actually read. **(d) PRE-REVIEW PREREQUISITES, all required before the final review runs.** Correct register §13's status column, stale for ~17 of 24 rows and named by every brief as the authority on what is already recorded. **Independently verify all ten accepted-limit factual bases (T1 applied retroactively), and REOPEN `D-09(c)`, whose basis F006 refutes.** And **John authorises Foundry and TypeScript count floors** — the "shrinking suite stays green" failure mode is round six's `L8-14`, is the same defect A-047 closed for the verifier and did not generalise, and is cheap to close now. **(e) THEN RUN THE BOUNDED FOUR-REVIEWER POST-REPAIR REVIEW.** John's words: *"That is a credible completion path — not another open-ended review loop."* Scope per the packet §5 and T3. **WHAT THIS DOES NOT AUTHORISE:** pre-publication does not begin (D-048 makes a clean result a PRECONDITION, never a trigger, and that is unchanged); no gate is signed; no public claim is certified; D-016 still blocks all publication.

- **A-074 (2026-08-18) — THE CONFORMANCE COMPARISON D-014 ASSIGNS TO THE D-010 VERIFIER IS BUILT. The signed Gate S1 claim was false when signed and is now true; John ruled it FIXED rather than WITHDRAWN (D-055(b)).** **THE ARGUMENT, written before the code (protocol step 1):** *a bundle whose evidence verdict is ALLOW ASSERTS that the action conforms to the mandate; a verifier that certifies it without comparing the signer-attested decoded parameters to the mandate's purpose fields certifies the ASSERTION rather than the FACT.* **WHY IT COULD NOT BE ANSWERED BY WITHDRAWING THE SENTENCE.** D-014 rejected giving the signer conformance checks, and the ground it gave — carried into `docs/gate-s1-evidence.md:124`/`:152`, a SIGNED pack — is that "a wrong-purpose ALLOW is detectable after the fact by the D-010 verifier, which does the conformance comparison". It did not: `grep -c decodedSelectorAndParameters verifier/verify.py` returned **0**. **Withdrawing the claim would have left D-014's architecture without the half that justifies keeping conformance out of the signer**, which is why John ruled the architecture must hold instead. **WHERE IT LIVES, AND THAT IS PART OF THE REPAIR.** Inside `_evidence_describes_the_bundle`, which is reached from `_chain_checks` on the receipt path AND from `_refusal_checks` on the §5.5.1 path since A-070 — so the comparison runs on EVERY verification path rather than the one a reviewer probed. "Which paths does this check NOT run on?" is the step-2 question that chose the placement. **DERIVED FROM THE PUBLISHED SPEC, NOT TRANSCRIBED FROM THE ENGINE.** §5.7.1's list ("Decoded parameters against the mandate": `EVAL_PURCHASE_RESOURCE`, `_BENEFICIARY`, `_DURATION`, `_RECURRENCE`, `EVAL_APPROVAL_SPENDER`, `EVAL_APPROVAL_CEILING`) is the source. That is the whole point of D-010 — two implementations of one published rule, so a divergence is a finding rather than a copied mistake. **THE BOUND, and it is the half that is easy to get wrong: this binds ONLY to ALLOW.** A BLOCK or REVIEW bundle is legitimately nonconforming — that is what it is FOR, the corpus is full of them, and `case-3-wrong-purpose-block` is the flagship. **Requiring conformance of those would reject the artifacts this project exists to produce**, so the negative is asserted by a test rather than left to reasoning. **ABSENCE IS NOT AGREEMENT, carried over from A-070:** under ALLOW, a missing or non-object decoded record, a missing mandate, an undecoded call, or a schema this verifier cannot evaluate all FAIL. An ALLOW nobody can check is not an ALLOW anybody should certify. **FALSIFIED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, WHICH IS WHAT JOHN REQUIRED.** Eight routes — the flagship wrong-purpose ALLOW built by flipping `case-3`'s verdict, plus wrong resource, wrong beneficiary, widened duration, forbidden recurrence, a foreign selector, an absent decoded record, and a non-object parameters field — **ALL PASS against the pre-fix verifier and ALL FAIL against the current one**, while the nonconforming BLOCK fixture and the conforming ALLOW verify throughout and all 7 committed samples still verify 7/7. **A DEAD PROBE CAUGHT AND RECORDED, because it would have read as a success.** The first version of the flagship probe flipped only the evidence verdict and the receipt enum; it FAILED pre-fix — but on *"verdict 2 decodes to ALLOW, matching the case label"*, because `meta.json` still said BLOCK. **Rejected by a DIFFERENT check than the one under test is failure mode 7**, and had it been reported as-is it would have claimed the fix worked using evidence that never touched it. The probe now moves the case label too, so only conformance can reject it. **Nine regression tests; eight fail against the pre-fix verifier and the ninth is the BLOCK-still-verifies control, which correctly passes both before and after — a control that failed pre-fix would be the wrong control.** Verifier suite **189 → 198**, floor ratcheted in the SAME edit. **RESIDUALS (step 6).** (a) Conformance is checked for the two schemas the corpus contains, `DemoPay.purchase` and `DemoERC20.approve`; **an ALLOW carrying any other schema now FAILS rather than passing unchecked**, which is the fail-closed direction but means a new demo contract requires a matching arm here. (b) The comparison uses the parameters the SIGNER attested, so it inherits D-014's boundary: the signer checks the parameters match the bytes GIVEN THE SELECTOR, and does not check the selector belongs at that target. (c) `evidence.decodedSelectorAndParameters.description` — the human-readable line §7.5's Gate 8 will be graded on — is still compared to nothing; round six's `L3-04` showed it can state the opposite of the parameters beside it. Recorded in the register, not fixed here.

- **A-075 (2026-08-18) — D-055(d)'S FOUR PRE-REVIEW PREREQUISITES, ALL FOUR DONE. E3 IS BUILT; THE REGISTER'S STATUS COLUMN IS CORRECTED; THE TEN ACCEPTED LIMITS ARE T1-VERIFIED AND `D-09(c)` IS REOPENED; THE FOUNDRY AND TYPESCRIPT SUITES HAVE COUNT FLOORS.** **NOTHING HERE STARTS THE BOUNDED REVIEW — T3 makes its scope John's to fix before it runs, and it is not scoped.** **(1) E3 — THE ARGUMENT, WRITTEN BEFORE THE CODE (repair protocol step 1):** *every state and code value the signer reasons about comes from ONE block, and the receipt's anchor must name that exact block — so a receipt can never assert a verdict about state the signer did not read.* **NOT A RECENCY BOUND, WHICH IS THE HALF THAT IS EASY TO GET WRONG.** D-055(c) rejected inventing a timeout: a permitted age is a policy parameter nobody had reasoned about, and the property actually missing was CONSISTENCY. An old anchor is now refused as a CONSEQUENCE of disagreeing with the snapshot, not because a threshold called it old — which is why the original A-044(f) acceptance was right to refuse a recency number and wrong to stop there. **WHAT WAS ACTUALLY BROKEN WAS WIDER THAN THE DEMONSTRATION.** The reviewer demonstrated one thing: an ALLOW anchored to any historical block is attested and executed. Reading `vault.ts` showed a second, undemonstrated half — ten `eth_call`s, a `getCode` and a `getBlockNumber`, all at "latest", each its own request, with a comment conceding they "could in principle straddle a block boundary" and arguing it away because a local Anvil has no competing producer. So `observedAtBlock` was a number NO field was guaranteed to have been read at, and **nothing consumed it**, so the inconsistency was invisible. **A repair aimed only at the demonstration would have special-cased old anchors and left that standing.** **THE MECHANISM.** `readVaultState` now takes the head with ONE `getBlock` (number and hash from the same response — deriving the hash separately would reintroduce the straddle in the identifier of the pin itself), passes `blockNumber: at` to all ten `eth_call`s and to `getCode`, then re-reads the head and discards the attempt unless it is still the same number AND the same hash. Five attempts, then `ChainUnstableError`. **`attest.ts` then requires `simulationBlockNumber`/`simulationBlockHash` to equal `observedAtBlock`/`observedBlockHash`, both fields**, and pushes the new CONFORMANCE code `SIGNER_ANCHOR_NOT_OBSERVED` otherwise. **CONFORMANCE, NOT EXECUTABILITY, and the tiering is load-bearing:** §4.2 Case 4 names "the anchored RPC state conflicts" as a REVIEW trigger, so ALLOW is refused and REVIEW stays reachable — asserted by a test, because tiering this one step stricter is the exact mistake `SIGNER_SIMULATION_BLOCK_MISMATCH` made and an adversarial review caught. **A SECOND NEW CODE, AND IT IS NOT PEDANTRY.** Snapshot exhaustion is FATAL like `SIGNER_VAULT_UNREACHABLE`, so the tiering cannot distinguish them — but "the vault could not be read" and "the vault was read repeatedly and the chain moved each time" are different facts, and filing the second as the first would put a claim in the signed refusal record that the evidence does not support. `SIGNER_CHAIN_UNSTABLE` exists so the record says which, and a test asserts neither code is emitted for the other's condition. **THE SIBLING SWEEP (step 2), RECORDED RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** `grep` finds six call sites of `readVaultState` — `corpus/run.ts` (x3), `tools/sample-check.ts`, `tools/emit-samples.ts`, `cases.e2e.test.ts`, `propose.e2e.test.ts` and the attestor's own `probe()` — and **all six go through `createChainReader`, so the pin lives in one helper and cannot be missed at a call site.** The five `VaultState` literals needing the new field were found by the COMPILER, not by memory. The anchor comparison has exactly one entry point, `evaluateAndSign`, exercised both in-process and over the real socket. **FALSIFIED SEVEN WAYS, EACH BY A ROUTE THE REVIEWER DID NOT USE (step 3), AND EACH FAILING THE TEST THAT NAMES IT** — checked explicitly, because "rejected by a DIFFERENT check than the one under test" is failure mode 7 and is what the A-074 probe hit.
