Drop the pin from one `eth_call`; drop it from `getCode`; delete the head re-confirmation; compare the height and not the hash; accept a hashless head; drop the hash half of the anchor comparison; file the unstable chain as unreachable. **AND THE EXPLOIT ITSELF, END TO END AGAINST A REAL CHAIN: pre-fix the signer SIGNS an ALLOW anchored to a superseded block ("expected a refusal, got a signature"); post-fix it refuses.** That test asserts `SIGNER_SIMULATION_BLOCK_MISMATCH` is **ABSENT** — the stale anchor is a genuine block with its genuine hash, so if the old check were what rejected it the test would be proving the wrong thing — and carries a control that the same scenario anchored at the head still signs, without which a signer that had simply stopped signing would pass. **ABSENCE IS NOT AGREEMENT (step 4):** a pending head (`hash: null`) produces NO snapshot and the reads are never issued, asserted directly, because a snapshot returned with a missing `observedBlockHash` would make the anchor comparison compare against nothing. **RESIDUALS (step 6).** (a) The pinned-read test drives `createChainReader` against a scripted JSON-RPC server, so it proves what the reader ASKS FOR, not what a node does with the request; that a pinned `eth_call` genuinely returns historical state is the node's contract and is not asserted here. (b) `SNAPSHOT_ATTEMPTS = 5` bounds WORK, not recency, and on a chain producing blocks faster than the signer can observe one the signer refuses rather than degrades — untested against a real fast chain, because this project has only Anvil. (c) The anchor now binds to the block the signer read; **nothing binds the EVALUATOR's simulation to that block except this refusal**, so a caller whose simulation is one block stale is refused and must re-simulate. That is the intended behaviour and it is a REAL behaviour change, stated here rather than left to be discovered. (d) `SIGNER_SIMULATION_BLOCK_MISMATCH` is now SUBSUMED by the new check and kept deliberately, because a forged block and a stale one are different diagnoses. **(2) REGISTER §13.4's STATUS COLUMN — 22 OF 24 ROWS WERE WRONG, not the ~17 D-055(d) estimated.** Every row bar `D-08` and `H-4` read `open`: nine had been FIXED (A-067/A-068/A-072/A-073), ten John had ACCEPTED as limits, and three adjudication had ruled were not defects at all (`A-3` UNPROVEN, `C-5` REFUTED, `H-6` ALREADY-CLOSED). **The direction of the error is what made it a prerequisite:** every brief names this table as the authority on what is recorded, so `open` against a fixed item invites a re-report, and `open` against an ACCEPTED limit invites a reviewer to treat John's decision as an oversight. §13.3's surrounding prose was a 2026-08-17 snapshot never rewritten; it is labelled as history rather than edited, and the table is now the record. **(3) THE TEN ACCEPTED LIMITS, EACH BASIS VERIFIED AGAINST THE TREE (T1).** **EIGHT HOLD. ONE IS REFUTED. ONE IS NARROWER THAN IT READS. TWO HAVE NO RECORDED BASIS AT ALL.** **`D-09(c)` IS REOPENED, as D-055(d) directs:** its basis was "no fixture distinguishes the two ceilings", and measured across all 50 committed fixtures **`F006` diverges by a factor of 500 — mandate `1e18` against policy `2e15` — exactly one of 50.** **`G-5`'s basis is the interesting one, because it is TRUE and INSUFFICIENT:** "the demonstration route is detected by A-062" was measured and holds — mutating the literal `50` to `49` makes the regenerated report differ from the committed one — **but that is the reviewer's route, not the finding's argument.** The literal is a `w("describe 50 fixtures …")` string and the generator derives no fixture count anywhere, so if the CORPUS changed size the generator and the committed report would still agree and A-062 would stay green. A basis that covers the demonstration and not the argument is the same shape `docs/repair-protocol.md` exists to catch, one layer up. **`D-10` carries a T2 discrepancy:** the corpus is single-case as claimed (9 distinct addresses, 0 non-lowercase occurrences across 50 fixtures), but the adjudicator wrote "for (c) I would raise to MEDIUM" and the limit is accepted at LOW with no recorded countersigned reasoning. **`H-5` and `H-8` have NO adjudicator reasoning recorded** — both entries read "Adjudicated CONFIRMED." and stop — **so T1 cannot be satisfied for them by verification at all.** Both mechanisms were reproduced today (`H-8`: `--all` over an empty directory prints `0/0 sample(s) verified` and exits 0). **`G-3`'s mechanism is now MEASURED rather than asserted:** `failingCodes` is `outcome !== "PASS"`, and exactly two classes — `conflicting-block-state` and `runtime-code-change-or-proxy-target` — are credited only on UNRESOLVED outcomes. **`F-VAULT-4`'s basis is now STRONGER than when accepted**, because A-073/D-054(b) measured the whole campaign's marginal power at zero. **NONE OF THIS RE-DECIDES AN ACCEPTANCE.** T1 asks whether each stated reason is TRUE, not whether a true reason is sufficient; the second is John's and is untouched. The `G-5`, `D-10`, `H-5` and `H-8` items are RAISED, not resolved. **(4) FOUNDRY AND TYPESCRIPT COUNT FLOORS** — `FOUNDRY_MIN_TESTS=75`, `TS_MIN_TESTS=507`, both ratcheted in the SAME edit as the suites they bound. **THE COUNT COMES FROM A REPORT THE SUITE DOES NOT SHARE A STREAM WITH, which is the whole lesson of the verifier floor's three defeats.** Foundry moves from `forge test -vv` to `forge test --json`: a Solidity test cannot write into the JSON, because `console.log` is captured INTO it as `decoded_logs`. One run, not two — keeping `-vv` and adding a `--json` run was MEASURED at +62s, so the per-test lines are reconstructed from the JSON with the same name, status and gas. TypeScript adds a SECOND reporter writing TAP to a private file while `spec` still goes to stdout, wired through `$SENTINEL_TEST_REPORTERS` in `ts/package.json` **so the gate runs the same npm script a developer runs and the flags cannot drift from the test glob**. **SIX FALSIFICATIONS, ALL FIRING.** A Solidity test skipped with `vm.skip(true)` leaves the total at 75 — **the floor alone does NOT catch it** — and the separate skip branch does; un-prefixing one test drops 75 → 74 and breaches the floor; a build that emits no JSON fails closed rather than reading as clean; a shrunk TypeScript suite breaches; `skip` and `todo` are caught by their own branch; a missing TAP file fails closed. **AND THE A-048 DEFEAT REPLAYED DIRECTLY: a test printing `# tests 99999` to stdout does not move the floor** — the spoofed text lands indented inside the TAP file and **exactly one line matches the floor's `^# tests N$` pattern, the real one.** **ONE REAL REGRESSION FROM THE `--json` SWITCH, RECORDED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED:** `-vv` streamed its `[PASS]` lines as tests finished and `--json` buffers until the run ends, so under the gate profile the log now goes SILENT for the stage's whole duration and a watcher cannot distinguish a slow fuzz campaign from a hang. **NO WALL-CLOCK FIGURE IS CLAIMED FOR IT: the only deep-profile timing this repository has measured is 447s and that was the `-vv` run; `--json` under the gate profile was not separately timed, and quoting the old number beside the new command would be a figure that was true once about something else.** Nothing is lost from the final record — every line is reconstructed from the JSON — but live observability is. **WHAT THE FLOORS DO NOT DO,** carried from the verifier's: this is a ratchet against ACCIDENT, not intent, and it says nothing about whether the tests that remain assert anything. **SUITE: 75 Foundry · 507 TypeScript (494 → 507, all from the E3 repair) · 198 verifier · 78 tamper over 30 modes · deep gate green.** **A PROCESS HAZARD THAT BIT TWICE IN ONE SESSION, RECORDED BECAUSE THE SECOND TIME PROVES IT IS NOT AN ANECDOTE.** **Editing `scripts/test.sh` while a gate run is executing it corrupts that run**, because bash reads a script incrementally by BYTE OFFSET: inserting lines shifts everything after them and the shell resumes mid-token. **The first occurrence produced the worst possible artifact — a run that threw a syntax error at line 500, never printed `GATE PASSED`, and EXITED 0.** It was caught only by reading the output instead of the exit status, which is house rule 4 doing its job. **The second occurrence happened to me an hour later, in the same session, while writing the note about the first** — an edit landing in the Foundry stage of a running deep gate. That run was killed and discarded unread rather than being interpreted. **Both runs were re-run clean with no edits in flight.** **THE GENERALISATION, which is the point: an exit status is not evidence that the thing you meant to run is the thing that ran.** Two of this project's recorded defect classes meet here — a dead probe whose silence reads as a pass, and a green result from an instrument that was not measuring what it names. **A mechanical guard is possible and is NOT built:** the gate could stamp its own file's hash at start and re-check it at the end, failing loudly if the script changed underneath itself. That is new tooling and outside D-055(d)'s scope, so it is recorded in `docs/v1-1-register.md` and raised rather than silently absorbed (repair protocol, "do not widen scope to satisfy the protocol").
- **D-056 (2026-08-18) — JOHN AUTHORISES ONE BOUNDED PRE-REVIEW STABILIZATION CHECKPOINT (six named items), AND FIXES THE T3 SCOPE OF THE D-055(e) REVIEW. His words and his calls; the agent recorded them and made none of them.** **(a) THE CHECKPOINT, AND ITS BOUNDS STATED FIRST BECAUSE A GRANT OF WORK IS WHERE SCOPE CREEPS.** *"This is authorised work before the D-055(e) review; it is not another review round and does not authorise any additional scope."* Six items only: `D-09(c)`, `G-5`, `D-10`, `H-5`, `H-8`, and a mechanical protection against the gate script being mutated mid-run. **Each carries John's own constraints, and several of them narrow what an agent would otherwise have done** — which is why they are recorded rather than paraphrased. **`D-09(c)`: prove the evidence bundle records the LOWER/intersected ceiling when mandate and policy diverge, and show the existing min→max mutation SURVIVES before the new test and FAILS after. DO NOT add or relabel a corpus fixture — F006 already supplies the divergent corpus case** — and **keep the absence of a divergent D-010 SAMPLE recorded separately as a v1.1 residual** unless the work independently requires otherwise. **`G-5`: remove the hardcoded fixture count AND the hardcoded presence assumptions for the named fixture caveats, deriving both from the generator's actual inputs; the canonical report must remain BYTE-IDENTICAL while the underlying facts hold. Falsify the ARGUMENT, not the reviewer's literal-edit demonstration** — a controlled corpus-size or fixture-presence change must either update the prose correctly or produce a detected mismatch. **`D-10`: ADOPT THE ADJUDICATOR'S MEDIUM FOR PART (c) until the repair is verified — "do not preserve LOW through an undocumented downgrade"** — which is D-055's T2 applied by John to his own earlier acceptance. Three focused regressions (mixed-case target, mixed-case selector, approval spender against mandate beneficiary where principal and beneficiary differ), and **all three recorded mutations replayed. John states the evidence standard explicitly and it is not the usual one: "The implementation is currently correct, so the evidence here is that each mutation survived before the new tests and is killed afterward — not that the clean implementation previously failed."** **`H-5`: correct BOTH the receipt and refusal diagnostics, distinguishing three states — metadata file absent; metadata present but the relevant field absent; usable metadata present — with paired tests that fail on the old false diagnostic and PRESERVE the genuinely-absent-metadata control.** **`H-8`: `--all` over zero discovered bundle directories must exit NONZERO with a clear diagnostic; sweep the sibling invocation shapes (empty directory, directory of non-bundle material, nonexistent path) and keep a successful nonempty control.** **(b) THE GATE-MUTATION PROTECTION, AND JOHN REJECTED THE AGENT'S PROPOSED DESIGN ON ITS MERITS.** A-076's predecessor recorded a naive remedy — hash the script at start and re-check at the end. **John ruled it insufficient, with the reason: "The ending check can itself be skipped or corrupted when bash resumes at a shifted byte offset."** That is correct and it is the whole difficulty: a guard living inside the mutable body cannot be trusted to run. **THE REQUIREMENT INSTEAD: the running gate must execute an IMMUTABLE SNAPSHOT or stable external body.** The bootstrap must establish that the source was copied consistently, execute the snapshot with all original arguments, and make the immutable execution FAIL if the original source changes before completion; temporary state is cleaned up. Falsified by a controlled lightweight harness covering four properties — unchanged source succeeds; a mid-run edit cannot corrupt the running parser AND yields a nonzero "gate source changed" result; arguments are preserved; temporary state is removed. **And a procedural bound: "Do not edit the real gate while a real gate run is active."** **(c) THE VERIFICATION PROCEDURE, WHICH IS STRICTER THAN THIS PROJECT HAS USED BEFORE AND IS THE POINT.** Focused tests and mutation replays first; then the canonical fast gate and workspace guards, **reading their output**; commit as ONE coherent checkpoint; then **from a CLEAN ISOLATED WORKTREE AT THAT EXACT COMMIT, run the deep gate including committed-views-verified-file-by-file**; if anything must change, new commit and repeat the exact-commit deep verification. **"The SHA you report must be the SHA actually verified."** Primary worktree confirmed clean. **Do not push.** **(d) T3 IS NOW SATISFIED: JOHN HAS FIXED THE SCOPE OF THE D-055(e) REVIEW BEFORE IT RUNS, which is exactly what T3 requires and what an agent may not do.** Four reviewers, and the partition is his. **Reviewer 1 — certification and instruments:** `scripts/**` and all gate/guard behaviour; `verifier/**` and `fixtures/samples/**`; A-070, A-071, A-074 and the new gate-stabilization work; related comments and printed output; and the cross-cutting canonical records — **both signed gate packs, decisions, session-state, HANDOFF and README**. **This reviewer must run the DEEP profile from the repaired isolated-worktree apparatus.** **Reviewer 2 — authorization and effect pipeline:** `ts/src/signer/**` and the Solidity type mirror; `ts/src/evaluate/**` and `ts/src/decode/**`; `ts/src/simulate/**`, `ts/src/propose/**`, `ts/src/tools/**`; A-072, D-053(b), A-074 and A-075/`E3`; all claims and comments owned by those surfaces and the corresponding proposal sections. **Reviewer 3 — onchain and corpus:** `contracts/src/**` and the deterministic and invariant Foundry suites; `ts/src/corpus/**`, `ts/src/ablation/**`, `fixtures/corpus/**`; atomic-drain behaviour, the invariant-campaign boundary, `D-09(c)`, `G-5`, `D-10`; generated ablation evidence and all related claims, NatSpec and proposal sections. **Reviewer 4 — a WHOLLY FREE LENS: no assigned target, no preferred direction, no surface hints** — only the standing adversarial brief, the frozen SHA, the current baseline, and the rules for distinguishing recorded limits from findings. It may inspect any surface. **BEFORE DISPATCH, a SCOPE MANIFEST is required** mapping every D-050(1) surface and every file touched by A-070 through this checkpoint to reviewers 1–3, assigning code comments and printed claims WITH THEIR SOURCE FILES, and **explicitly partitioning the remaining tracked documents "so the claims surface is not covered merely by assertion"**. Reviewer 4 stays genuinely unassigned. **(e) THE REVIEW CONTROLS, all John's.** Four reviewers only — one bounded review, not round seven. Serial, or at most two concurrent. Separate worktree AND evidence directory each. Every reviewer runs an untouched baseline first and returns findings, null results, **dead probes**, a coverage statement, a brief critique and a provenance attestation. **No repository edits or repairs while reviewers are running.** **Each Critical/High lead stays PENDING until independently adjudicated — "an unadjudicated lead is not 'unconfirmed'"**, which is D-055's clarification restated. **Cross-adjudicate within the four so nobody adjudicates their own finding.** Any severity downgrade needs recorded reasoning AND John's countersignature. **Every Medium/Low is individually adjudicated too** — repaired, or brought to him as an accurately bounded acceptance. **A confirmed High stops blocking only after repair plus targeted INDEPENDENT reverification, or John's explicit acceptance as a documented product boundary.** Repairs follow the repair protocol and get targeted reverification **by a reviewer who did not author them**; no second full review. **"If a repair materially expands architecture or product guarantees, return it to me rather than implementing it."** Then rerun focused checks, fast gate, exact-commit deep gate and workspace guards, and present the D-055 exit assessment. **(f) WHAT IS STILL FORBIDDEN, restated by John inside the authorisation:** do not sign or reopen a gate, do not declare publication authorised, do not start pre-publication, do not push, do not change D-016.
- **A-076 (2026-08-18) — THE BOUNDED PRE-REVIEW STABILIZATION CHECKPOINT: all six D-056(a) items closed, each falsified against the state it repairs. Suite 75 Foundry / 513 TypeScript / 209 verifier; every floor ratcheted in the same edit as its suite.** **THE HEADLINE IS NOT THE SIX REPAIRS. It is that two of them found the recorded finding UNDERSTATED, and one found a live false claim in a generated evidence document — which is what D-055's T1 exists to make possible and is the third time this project has learned it.** **(1) THE GATE CAN NO LONGER BE MUTATED MID-RUN, AND JOHN'S REJECTION OF THE FIRST DESIGN WAS CORRECT ON A POINT THE AGENT HAD MISSED.** The proposal was "hash at start, re-check at end"; John ruled that *"the ending check can itself be skipped or corrupted when bash resumes at a shifted byte offset"*. **A guard living in the mutable body is precisely the code the attack makes unreachable.** THE ARGUMENT INSTEAD: *a gate result is a statement about a specific script body, so that body must be immutable for the whole run.* The mutable file now does one thing — hash itself, copy itself to a snapshot, hash both again to prove the copy was not torn, and `exec` into the snapshot with `"$@"`. **From the exec onward bash reads a private file nobody has a path to, so an edit cannot corrupt the running parser AT ALL; the failure mode is REMOVED, not detected.** The original is re-hashed in an EXIT/INT/TERM trap that lives in the snapshot, so a changed source is refused a zero exit (code 4) on every exit path, and the snapshot is cleaned up on all of them. **`scripts/check-gate-immutability.sh` falsifies five properties and is itself falsified by six mutations, all caught.** It EXTRACTS the bootstrap verbatim from `scripts/test.sh` between markers, so it cannot test a drifted copy, and refuses to report a pass if extraction yields nothing. **TWO REAL DEFECTS WERE FOUND BY BUILDING THE HARNESS, AND BOTH WOULD HAVE SHIPPED.** **(a) THE HARNESS ITSELF WAS BLIND, and only a mutation revealed it.** Its first version replaced the original with `mv`. **That test passes against a script with NO protection whatsoever** — `mv` renames a new inode into place and the running shell keeps reading the old one — measured across bodies from 442 bytes to 1.5 MB, every one completing cleanly. Removing the bootstrap's `exec` still reported 4/4. **The hazard is an IN-PLACE rewrite, truncate-and-write on the same inode, which is what Python's `open(path,"w")` does and what corrupted both real runs.** The harness now edits in place AND runs an UNPROTECTED CONTROL through the identical probe first: if the control is not corrupted, the probe has stopped being dangerous and every later pass is meaningless. **A test that passes against no protection at all is the instrument-defect class this project has recorded more than any other, and it was reintroduced by the person writing the fix for it.** **(b) `SENTINEL_GATE_SNAPSHOT` IS EXPORTED, so every child of a running gate inherited it.** The first bootstrap skipped its work whenever the variable was merely SET — so the moment the gate ran another script carrying the same block, that child concluded it was already a snapshot and **ran unprotected from its own mutable file.** Found by wiring the harness in as a gate stage and watching it get corrupted. The re-exec is now keyed on whether the variable names THIS file. A third, smaller error is recorded because it cost a hang: comparing an unresolved `mktemp` path against a `cd`-resolved one made the snapshot permanently unequal to itself on macOS, where `$TMPDIR` sits under the `/var` → `/private/var` symlink, and the snapshot re-exec'd forever. **(2) `D-09(c)`.** THE ARGUMENT: *`expectedEffects.maxNativeValueWei` is what the bundle CLAIMS was authorised, and §5.2 intersects mandate and policy, so it must be the LOWER of the two whichever side is lower.* Inverting `minOf` to a max left **507/507 green**, and neither committed corpus artifact catches it — views omit `expectedEffects` by construction and result files do not carry it — so the deep gate's byte comparisons are blind too. **The field was read by nothing, and under the inversion a bundle capped at 2e15 would attest that 1e18 was authorised, five hundred times the real limit, to the D-010 verifier that compares against exactly this value.** Three tests assert BOTH binding directions plus the equal case, read out of the CANONICAL BYTES rather than the in-memory object because that is what `evidenceHash` commits to. Post-fix the recorded mutation is killed, and so are two sibling routes — "policy only" and "mandate only" — each caught by the mirror the other row exists to be. **No fixture added or relabelled, per John: F006 already supplies the divergent corpus case.** **THE RESIDUAL IS UNCHANGED AND STAYS RECORDED: there is still no divergent SAMPLE, which is what the D-010 verifier reads** (register §13.5, v1.1). **(3) `D-10`, and the severity call is John's.** He adopted the adjudicator's MEDIUM for part (c) *"until the repair is verified; do not preserve LOW through an undocumented downgrade"* — D-055's T2 applied to his own earlier acceptance. THE ARGUMENT: *a binding comparison must be pinned to the FIELD it names and the VALUE it names, independently of how the corpus spells either.* The corpus cannot distinguish these: 9 distinct addresses, zero non-lowercase occurrences, and every fixture sets `principal === beneficiary`. All three recorded mutations survived the full suite before these tests and are killed after, each by the test that names it. **A PROBE THAT MEASURED NOTHING WAS CAUGHT BEFORE IT COULD MISLEAD:** the first mixed-case probe upper-cased `DEMO_PAY`, which is `0x2222…` — all digits, so the transformation was a no-op. It failed loudly only because a "did the probe move anything" assertion was written first, and that assertion is kept. **(4) `G-5`, AND THIS ONE WAS NOT LATENT.** John required the count and the named-fixture caveats derived from the generator's actual inputs, with the canonical report byte-identical if the facts hold, and the ARGUMENT falsified rather than the reviewer's literal edit. Done: the count is `results.length`, the inert-class caveat is keyed on D-025's reserved CLASS, and the attribution caveat is derived from `primaryEnforcement` — **the same field the attribution note below it is built from, so the two can no longer disagree.** Falsified by controlled corpus changes: 50→49 updates the count; F035 leaving the delegated set drops it from the sentence; F051 leaving the reserved class drops it from the inert sentence; and the empty case prints a real sentence rather than a dangling list, on both. **THE REPORT WAS ALREADY WRONG, which the "byte-identical" condition surfaced rather than hid.** The prose named *"`F035` and `F057`, whose enforcement is the isolated signer and the vault"*. **`F057`'s `primaryEnforcement` is `conformance-engine` — it never belonged — and `F028`, `F029`, `F054`, `F055` and `F056` do belong and were omitted.** The derived attribution note eight lines below had listed the correct six all along. So the report changes by exactly two lines, and **that diff is the finding**: G-5 was accepted as prose that *could* drift, and it had already drifted, in a document published as evidence. **(5) `H-5`.** THE ARGUMENT: *a diagnostic must describe the bundle it is about.* Both label cross-checks collapsed two situations into *"no meta.json/index.json to cross-check against"* — true when no metadata exists, FALSE when metadata exists and is silent about the field, which is the case that occurs. Three states are now distinguished, and the middle one FAILS on A-067's rule rather than skipping: an artifact that claims to be labelled and does not state the thing being verified cannot be cross-checked. **Fixed in ONE shared helper because the identical construction appeared TWICE** — receipt path and refusal path — and repairing one occurrence while its sibling stands is the A-066 defect. Five tests; the two "present but silent" rows fail pre-fix on both paths, and **the genuinely-absent-metadata control is preserved and still skips**, as John required by name. **(6) `H-8`.** THE ARGUMENT: *"verified nothing" must never be reported as "verified everything".* `--all` discovers its own work, so zero discovered bundles printed `0/0 sample(s) verified` and **exited 0** — in a gate reading exit status, an empty corpus was indistinguishable from a passing one. Now exit **2**, deliberately distinct from 1 so "nothing to check" stays separable from "something failed". **The whole discovery surface is swept, not the one shape the reviewer tried:** empty directory, directory of non-bundle files, unenumerable path (which previously died in a bare traceback — nonzero for the wrong reason and no usable diagnostic), a FILE named under `--all`, and **one empty root among several, which an aggregate "did we find any targets" check would have passed**. Six tests; five fail pre-fix and the sixth is the nonempty control, which correctly passes both before and after — a control that failed pre-fix would be the wrong control. **RECORDS UPDATED SO NOTHING FIXED STILL READS AS ACCEPTED:** §11.0's heading moves from TEN to FIVE and names what remains (`D-07`, `D-09`(a),(b), `E5`, `F-VAULT-4`, `F-VAULT-5`); register §13.4's rows for all five move to FIXED. **RESIDUALS.** (a) The immutability harness proves the bootstrap's properties on a SYNTHETIC body; it does not prove the gate's stages are correct, and it defends only `scripts/test.sh` — a reviewer editing `verifier/verify.py` mid-run is out of scope and is covered by the frozen review worktree. (b) A torn read that happened to leave the source byte-identical is indistinguishable from no write; that is the honest bound of a hash comparison. (c) `H-8`'s exit 2 is a new exit code in this CLI's contract; nothing in the repository keys on it beyond nonzero, verified by grep, but a downstream consumer that tested `== 1` would need updating. (d) `G-5` derives the caveat sentences from `primaryEnforcement` and `class`; it does not verify that those FIELDS are themselves right, which is a corpus-correctness question and not a generator one.
